# Orca requirements

## A-6 Orca

A screen of its own (`nav-orca`, `#orca`; the button is 「開発」 and this screen holds Orca's projects with the formations of A-37 above them, A-5) shows which Claude agents run in Orca, a desktop agent orchestrator with a CLI (`orca`), shows their terminals (A-8, A-12) and starts new ones. It stores nothing about a session: every read asks the CLI again. Only agents of type `claude` are listed and started; an agent of another type (Codex) has no row, and a launch always starts Claude.

The screen is two panes side by side (`#orca` is a `.split-main`): the list pane `#orca-list` on the left, the divider `#orca-split` (a `.split-bar` with `role="separator"`, `aria-orientation="vertical"`, `aria-label` "一覧の幅", `aria-valuemin` 15, `aria-valuemax` 50 and `tabindex` 0), and the work pane `#orca-work` (while a formation is selected, the panes of A-37 stand in its place). The list is 19% of the screen at first and the user sets it between 15% and 50% by dragging the divider, or with the arrow keys (Right widens, Left narrows, 2% a step); the ratio is kept in memory (as the divider of S-1 and S-6, `ts/split.ts` serves both). The list pane has a header bar (`#orca-header`) as high as every header (44 px at least, A-23), level with the tab strip of the work pane, and no heading: it holds only the button of the list (A-23), the button that makes a formation (`#formation-new`, A-37) and the button that adds a project (A-38), each 28 px high. It has no reload button (the list is read by the clock and when the screen is shown; at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-14) and no new button: a session is started from the plus of the work pane (A-16). The pane has no footer, no status label of the target and no menu of it, and a read that failed or a missing `orca` shows in the notice above the list (removed at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16; #712). Under the header and the notice, the list shows the formations (A-37) under their heading, and then, under the heading 「プロジェクト」 (`.orca-list-head`, whose row has the magnifier of the search, A-19) and the projects (A-8), and nothing else: there is no working view that groups the agents by state (入力待ち, 作業中, 完了), and no button that switches to one (removed at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-14; #577). The agents are seen as the sessions under their projects (A-8) and in the count of the Orca button. `nav-count` of the Orca button shows the number of sessions that need the user: the rows that wait (入力待ち) plus the notices below, with the label "Orca（確認待ち n 件）"; 作業中 is not counted. The count is added to the Dock badge (`set_badge`, T-1), so the Dock shows a number when only Orca has a session that needs the user, and it goes when the sum is 0.

The work pane is split into panes (A-17); each has the tab bar (A-8, A-16: the tab strip of the selected project, shown also when it is empty, and the plus right after its last tab), and under it the terminal of the tab it shows (A-8, A-12), or a line (`.orca-empty`) that says why there is none: 「プロジェクトを選んでください」 when no project is selected, 「セッションはありません」 for a project with no session, and 「このセッションのターミナルはありません」 for a Claude session with no terminal handle. The screen has no composer and no history: the user types into the terminal. The pane of A-9 shows the terminal of the session it handed to (A-9).

New session. A session is started from the plus of the work pane (A-16), which calls `orca_start(path, environment)` with the place of that worktree (its `environment`: the saved environment's name, null for this machine): it starts Claude in that worktree with no first prompt (the user types it in the terminal). The dialog of the worktrees (`openWorktreeDialog`, `showMenu`, `role="dialog"`, shared with the chip of A-9) has a filter field (`aria-label` "worktree を絞り込む", case-insensitive on "{repo} ・ {branch}") and two-line rows (the repository, then the branch) of the worktrees it is given; picking one closes it. A launch is never tried again, because after a lost reply a second one would start twice (the CLI forbids a fresh retry of a launch); a failure shows the CLI's message (`error.message`) on a card (A-28). The side pane of A-9 launches and sends through the same checks (`orca_handoff` composes the prompt and then runs the same launch or send once); a send whose answer says `result.send.accepted` is false is a failure too (Japanese message).

After a launch. A launch that works follows the session it started (`orca_start` answers `{paneKey, handle}`, and A-9 uses the same answer): the project and the tab that were selected stay meanwhile, and once a read holds the row of that `paneKey` or of that `handle` (the one that is not `null`; the read after the launch, a later one or a list of the stream of A-12, for at most 30 seconds), the screen selects its project and its tab, so its terminal shows. Until the row is in the list the work pane shows a note (`#orca-started`, `role="status"`: 「セッションを始めました。一覧に現れるのを待っています…」), and the plus is disabled, so a second session cannot be started meanwhile. Selecting a project, a tab or a row, or a read after the 30 seconds (one that failed too) drops the wait; the launch is never followed after the user selected another project or tab while it was on its way. When Orca's reply had neither a `paneKey` nor a `handle` (both `null`), nothing can be waited for: the note says 「セッションを始めました。Orca の画面で開けます。」, the plus stays enabled, and the note goes at the next selection.

The CLI is found among the absolute places `/usr/local/bin/orca` and `/Applications/Orca.app/Contents/Resources/bin/orca` (a GUI app has no shell PATH), and run with an argument list, never through a shell, in the temporary folder, with stderr dropped and a timeout (15 seconds for a read, 60 seconds for a launch and a send) after which the child is killed, off the UI thread. The commands are `orca worktree ps --limit 200 --json` and `orca terminal list --json` (the list), `orca terminal send --terminal <handle> --text=<text> --enter --json` (send), `orca terminal create --worktree path:<path> --json` (a new terminal, A-16), `orca worktree create --repo=id:<repository id> --name=<name> [--branch=<branch>] [--base-branch=<ref>] --no-parent --json` (a new worktree, A-9; `--branch` and `--base-branch` only when given; `orca_worktree_create` answers `result.worktree.path`), `orca agent launch --agent claude --worktree path:<path> --surface terminal --prompt=<text> --json` (launch; `orca_start` gives no `--prompt`, and only the handoff of A-9 gives one; its reply holds `result.outcome` `{kind: "terminal", handle, paneKey}`, which `orca_start` and `orca_handoff` return as `{paneKey, handle}`, each `null` when the reply lacks it or holds it in a shape that is not a pane key (at most 256 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9:_-]`) or a terminal handle), and `orca environment list --json`; the text of the user is joined to its flag (`--text=--json` is accepted, while `--text --json` is refused as "requires a value"), and `--environment=<name>` is added to every one of them but the list of environments when a target is chosen. The output is JSON, `{ok, result, error: {code, message}}`, also on failure, so stdout is read whatever the exit code and `error.message` is shown (the code when there is no message); an empty output or one that is no JSON gives a Japanese message. The prompt text is in no log line.

Rows are built by a pure function from the two documents (`result.worktrees[].agents[]` and `result.terminals[]`), tolerant of absent fields. An agent is kept when its `agentType` is `claude`, it is top-level (`parentPaneKey` null) and `hasLivePty` is true. Its group follows `state`: `waiting`, `blocked` and `permission` are 入力待ち, `done` is 完了, and every other state (`working`, and a state not known yet) is 作業中. The line (the title of the row and of its tab) is the first line of `conversationName`, else of `prompt` and `taskTitle`, in this order (each read as A-8 says: only what the owner or Claude wrote, never a message of the harness, data or the output of a process), else the first line of the `title` of the terminal of the same pane (`result.terminals[]`, joined as below) without the status mark Claude Code puts in front of it (a first character that is no letter or digit, followed by a space; taken off once, with the spaces after it), whatever the state. Every one of these texts is read as A-8 says ("Sessions under a project"): the line is the first line that has text left, and a text with none gives the turn to the next one. The branch is the worktree's `displayName`, the repository its `repo` and the path its `path`. A row also carries the latest message (`latest`: the first line of the newer of the agent's `prompt` and `lastAssistantMessage`, read as A-8 says and cut to 200 characters; null when the newer one has no text, so a JSON object, the output of a process and a message of the harness are no latest message, and the older message is not taken in its place). Orca gives no time of either message, so Rust (`answer_is_newer` of `orca.rs`, a pure function) reads which is newer from the state of the turn: the answer is newer when the agent's `state` is `done`, or when its `mainAgent.state` is `done` and its `mainAgent.stateStartedAt` (a time that is missing counts for nothing) is not before the agent's `stateStartedAt` (the main agent ended its turn after the turn began, and its subagents still work); in every other state a turn is in progress and the prompt is newer. The list draws the latest message in place of the line (A-8) and the tab does not, the number of its subagents at work (`subagents`: the items of its `subagents[]` whose `state` is `working`; 0 when the field is absent or is not an array, and an item that is not an object counts for none; Orca lists them only while they run), which the list draws at the right end of the row (A-8) and the tab does not, and the agent's `paneKey` (null when it has none), which names its tab (A-12), and its `status` and `projectKey` (A-8). The time is `stateStartedAt` for an agent that runs or waits ("2 分", the time since) and `updatedAt` for a done one ("18 分前"), sent as epoch milliseconds and formatted by the screen. The terminal handle is found by joining the agent's `paneKey` with the terminals' `"{tabId}:{leafId}"` (an orphaned terminal by its `recordedPaneKey`, as below). Rows are sorted by `updatedAt`, newest first. The launch targets are all worktrees, with their path, repository and `displayName`.

Just before it acts, Rust reads the list again (with the environment): a send goes only to a handle that belongs to a row of that list (a top-level agent of a live terminal; `writable` is not used, because a plain shell terminal is writable too; an orphaned terminal, one that lost its tab and still runs, belongs to the agent whose pane is its `recordedPaneKey` when its `agentIdentity` is `claude` and no other orphaned terminal recorded that pane, and a live terminal of that pane comes first), and a launch or a new terminal (A-16) only into a worktree of it; otherwise the answer is a Japanese error and nothing is sent. Rust validates before it runs anything and answers in Japanese: the text is not blank and at most 100,000 characters; the handle begins with `term_` and holds only `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; the path is absolute; the environment is one listed by `orca environment list --json` (`result.environments`, each a name or an object with a `name`, else an `id`).

The target is "このマシン" or a saved environment (a paired server): the one place the screen is connected to besides this machine, so at most one saved environment is read at a time. While a saved environment is the target, this machine is read too and its items are in the same list (A-33). The target is read from `orca-settings.json` in the app config directory (`{"kind":"local"}` or `{"kind":"environment","name":"..."}`), and only when the file does not exist yet the first saved environment is used, else this machine. The app never writes the file and has no place to choose the target: no screen, menu or card changes it (the label and the menu of the list pane were removed at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, #712, and the card of the Orca tab of the settings at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, #714); the target is the saved value, and a change to the file is taken at the next read. A saved environment that is no longer listed, or a file that cannot be used while an environment is saved, never switches the target by itself: the read is a failed one with 「保存した接続先が見つかりません」 (shown in the notice above the list), and the owner corrects the file (with no environment saved there is nothing else to choose, so a file that cannot be used gives this machine). The list pane shows no label of the target and no menu of it, and the pane has no footer (removed at the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16; #712). What a read says is in the notice above the list: 「読み込めませんでした: {message}」 in the failed style when it failed (the rows of the last good read stay while the target is the same), and 「orca コマンドが見つかりません。…」 when no `orca` was found. While this machine is the target that is the one line of the notice; while a saved environment is the target, this machine and the saved environment are read apart and each read that failed has a line of its own that names its place, 「{place} を読み込めませんでした: {message}」 (A-33). When a read names a target other than the one held (the file was changed), the notices and the base of the old target are dropped, and the rows are those of the new target. When `orca environment list` itself fails, the screen keeps the target, the environments and the rows it had, and shows the failure. While the target is a saved environment that is listed, its list is streamed (A-12) and not read by the clock; this machine's list beside it is read by the clock (A-33).

The list is read at startup (for the count), every 10 seconds while the Orca screen is shown and every 60 seconds otherwise, one read at a time, and no more by the clock once the command was found missing (showing the screen looks again); a streamed list (A-12) is not read by the clock (this machine's beside it is, A-33). The count of the Orca button follows every read (the clock's read for the count alone included) through one function. It counts the sessions of this machine and of the saved environment, and no session of an SSH host: a Claude agent in a worktree whose `hostId` is not `local` makes no row, so neither the count, the badge of the Dock nor a notice has it (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, issues #783 and #785). A row has stopped when its group is 完了 or its status is `failed` or `interrupted` (Orca says an agent stopped with Esc as `state` `done` with `interrupted` true). A stopped row has a notice when, while the app runs, it went from not stopped to stopped between two reads, or it first appeared in a read after the first of the target and had already stopped (a pane the app has seen stopped is remembered, so it has no new notice when it leaves the list and comes back stopped, and the memory ends when it is seen running or a read names another target). The rows of the first read of a target (at startup, and after a read names another target) are only the base and have no notice, so what had finished before is not counted; a read that names another target than the one held drops every notice. A notice goes when the session is shown on the Orca screen (the screen is shown, the window is not hidden, and the row is the selected tab; the session seen only in the side pane of A-9 does not clear it), when the row runs again (it is no longer stopped), or when it leaves the list; a row that stops while it is shown has no notice. A row with no pane key cannot be opened, so it has no notice (it is still counted while it waits). A failed read, and a read that did not find the rows while the command is there, leave the notices as they are; a missing command drops them. The notices are held in memory only. While one row or more is working (its group is 作業中 and its status is `working` or `monitoring`), the icon of `#nav-orca` (the `svg` only, not `.nav-count`) is yellow (`--agent-working`) and fades slowly (`opacity` between 1 and .55 over 2 seconds, `ease-in-out`, endless), by the class `working` on the button; under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` it stays yellow and does not fade. The number that work is not on the button: it is in the `aria-label` (read aloud, and in the tooltip on hover), after the count of the waiting ones (「Orca（確認待ち 1 件、作業中 2 件）」, 「Orca（作業中 2 件）」, 「Orca（確認待ち 1 件）」, else 「Orca」). The class and the label follow every read the count follows, so a read for the count alone moves them too; a failed read cannot say that a session works, so it takes the mark and that part of the label away and leaves the count of the waiting ones as it is, and a missing command or a change of the target removes them all.

The environments are read (`orca environment list`, and the target resolved) only at startup, when the screen is shown and in a read after a failure (`orca worktree ps` and `orca terminal list` of this machine are read at the same time as `orca environment list` whatever the file says: they are the target's own lists when this machine is the target, and the lists beside it (`local`, below) when a saved environment is); the clock reads the agents and terminals of the target held while the screen is shown, and only `orca worktree ps` (for the count, with no terminal list) while another screen is shown. A read in the background swaps the rows in place and keeps the selection (dropped only when its session is gone), the terminal shown and the scroll position, and never calls `show()`.

Where an item is. Every row, worktree, project and plain terminal that `orca_overview` and the stream of A-12 answer has `environment`: `null` for this machine, else the name of the saved environment it was read from (the stream places each list it sends in its environment, in the sink of `orca_stream_open`). While a saved environment is the target, `orca_overview` also reads this machine's lists, side by side with the target's (every scope, with the same terminals as the scope reads: `Badge` reads no terminal), and answers them as `local` (`OrcaLocal`: its own `state` and `message`, and the rows, worktrees, projects and terminals, each with `environment` `null`); `local` is `null` when this machine is the target, whose lists are the ones of the overview itself. The two reads fail apart: a failed or missing read of this machine is `local.state` `failed` or `missing` and leaves the target's lists and state as they were read, and a failed read of the target leaves `local`. The rest of the answer (the state, the rows and the count, and the notices) is the target's alone. The screen draws both places in one list (A-33): the lists of `local` and the target's are mixed, a project and a session of a saved environment carry its name (`environment`), and the place of an operation is read from `environment` ("Where an operation goes" below). The count of the Orca button and the notices stay the target's, whatever `local` holds. Both fields may be absent in TypeScript (`optional = nullable`), so a screen that does not read them is not changed by them.

Where an operation goes. An operation on a row, a tab or a worktree is called with the `environment` of that item (the saved environment's name, null for this machine) and not with the target the screen holds: the close of a terminal, the start of Claude or of a terminal from the plus (the worktree picked, A-16), the scratchpad (the worktree's, A-29), the stream or the mirror of a terminal with its reads, its keys and the files dropped on it (the tab's, A-10 and A-12), the file pane with its files, history, branches, downloads and diffs and the files it opens (the worktree's, A-18, A-24, A-25 and A-27), and the layout of A-17 and the files and diffs open in a project (the project row's; a project that the list does not hold takes the target). The list the screen draws holds both places (A-33), so the place is read from each item and each row gets its own. The side pane of A-9 keeps its own destination. The E2E specs cover it with a list that holds the rows of a saved environment and of this machine side by side (`orca.e2e.ts`: the close, the start, the layout, the files, the mirror's reads and keys and the attached image; `orca-files.e2e.ts`: the history).

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover row building, the argument lists (the `--flag=value` form and the environment), the validation, the parsing of the output and the environments and the target setting, the place of every item of a read of a saved environment and of this machine (`assemble`, `place_in`, and `placed_in` of the stream), and that `local` comes beside the target's lists and that a failed read of either side leaves the other (`with_local`); none of them runs the real CLI. The E2E spec `orca.e2e.ts` covers (the mix of the two places is A-33's) the header, the two panes and the divider, the selection, the start from the plus (A-16: its menu, the dialog, the arguments of a launch and the following of its session, a failed launch), the missing command, a failed read shown in the notice, no label or menu of the target in the list pane, a read in the background, and the count and its notices.

## A-7 Orca session history

Retired (#478, the owner's decision 2026-11-12). The history of a session (what the user asked, what the agent answered and the tools it ran) is no longer read or drawn, and the app has no command for it: the pane of A-9 and the Orca screen show the terminal of the session (A-9, A-12), where a question of Claude is answered as well (A-11).

What stays is the connection to Orca's runtime, which the history was the first to use and which the other requirements name as A-7's (`src-tauri/src/orca_link.rs`, with the framing of `orca_e2ee.rs`). The runtime is not a public interface. It was written against Orca 1.4.248, and an Orca update can change it. This machine is reached over the unix socket of `orca-runtime.json` in Orca's settings folder (`authToken`, and the transport of kind `unix` with its `endpoint`): one JSON line `{id, authToken, method, params}` and one line back, a connection for each call. The socket exists on macOS and Linux; on another OS a call fails with a message that says so. A saved environment (A-6) is reached over its WebSocket (`ws://` only: the frames are encrypted end to end, with the pairing's key, and TLS is not built in) with the device token and the public key of `orca-environments.json`, using Orca's own framing. Both files are read at each connect and never written. A failed call shows Orca's code (「Orca がエラーを返しました（{code}）」), never Orca's own message; when Orca is not running on this machine the message is 「Orca に接続できません。Orca が起動しているか確認してください」. A token, a key or an authentication frame is in no log, no error text and no event. A caller that keeps a connection open tries again after 1, 2, 5, 10 and then 30 seconds.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the framing and encryption of the paired connection against fixed vectors, the choice of the pairing and the endpoint, the answer of a call (the result, or the code and never the message), the handshake and a refused token against a stand-in server, a call over the unix socket, and the back-off; none of them talks to a real Orca.

## A-8 Orca terminal and project list

The Orca screen (A-6) shows the terminals of Orca, one for each tab, and its list pane is a list of projects with a switch to the working agents. It was written against Orca 1.4.248.

Projects. The list pane shows the projects. A row (a button, `.orca-project` in a `.orca-proj`, `aria-pressed` for the selected one) has the status icon, the name and the number of sessions (nothing when it is 0). The number stays a number when the project is open. It counts the sessions the list shows under the project: the sessions in the worktree of a formation's child are not counted in it (below, "Sessions under a project"). A pure function builds them from the two documents of A-6: one project for each `repoId` among the worktrees that are not archived (`isArchived` is not true), have a path, and whose `hostId` is `local` (a worktree with no `hostId` is this machine's); the name is the worktree's `repo`. The worktrees of an SSH host (any other `hostId`) are not projects, and the list draws no row for them (below). The sessions of a project are its Claude rows (A-6) and its plain terminals. The projects with a working session (a session of the project whose icon is the yellow ring; the icon of the project itself may be a question or a failure, which outrank working, and the project is still one of them; also a project whose own icon works when no Claude row of it does, as when another agent or a worktree's status works) come first. The stopped projects follow: those with one session or more (the number of sessions shown on the row is not 0) that are not in the working group. The projects with no session come last. The stopped projects are newest first by the latest `lastActivityAt` of the project's worktrees, as the pure function lists them, and so are the projects with no session. The working projects are not reordered by their activity, which moves with the work: the screen keeps them in the order they entered the working group, in memory only. A project that begins to work (it had no working session at the last read that worked, or it is new, or the screen has just started) goes in front of the ones that already work, and several that begin together keep the order the function lists them in (newest first); one with no working session left leaves the group and goes among the stopped projects, or those with no session. A project whose sessions only wait for the user is not in the working group and stays below it; one with a session that works and another that waits is in the group, and a question asked or answered there moves nothing. A read that failed changes none of this, and a change of the target starts it again. The list draws no SSH host: no heading "SSH" and no row `ssh:{hostId}`, and so the sessions of an SSH host's worktrees are not listed (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, issues #783 and #784). Rust leaves out the worktrees of an SSH host (any `hostId` but `local`) when it makes the rows, the projects and the plain terminals, and answers no `ssh` or `hosts` list in the overview, the stream or `local` (issue #785). While a saved environment is the target the list holds this machine's projects beside its own, newest first as one list, and a project of the saved environment carries its name as a chip (A-33). The search field above the list (A-19) narrows them by name.

Sessions under a project. Each project opens and closes by itself, so any number can be open at once. A project that has a tab other than a file (a Claude session with a pane, or a plain terminal) has an arrow at the left of its row (`.orca-arrow`, an `.icon-btn` of 24 px with a chevron that turns down when open, `aria-expanded`, `aria-label` "セッションを開く" and "セッションを閉じる"); a project with no session has none. Selecting a project (its row, a session row, or a session started from the plus) opens it and closes no other. The arrow opens or closes its own project whether it is selected or not; it changes only the list, and the selection, the tabs, the panes and the terminals of the work pane stay as they are. Selecting a closed project again opens it. At first every working project is open and the others are closed, and a project opens, also when the user had closed it with its arrow, when a read has a working session in it that the last read that worked did not have (a session that begins to work, or a project that begins to work, above; a project that works with no working Claude row counts as one such work); one whose working sessions are the same as at the last read stays as the user left it. The title of a session row (and of its tab) is the line of A-6, taken from the agent's `conversationName`, `prompt`, `taskTitle` or the title of its terminal, in this order, as Orca's own list names a session by the conversation first and by the prompt only when there is no name (the last answer is no title: the latest message of a row, below, is the text of the list's row only). The list shows the owner's question and Claude's answer only, and no message of the harness, no result of a tool and no output of a process (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, issue #760). Orca's `prompt` is the last message sent to Claude, so a message of Claude Code's harness that came after the question (an `<event kind="…" at="…">` that holds a result, a `<task-notification>`, a `<system-reminder>`) is the `prompt` whenever one came. So Rust (a pure function in `orca.rs`, `first_text_line`) reads each text of A-6 (the conversation's name, the prompt, the task's title, the title of the terminal and the last answer) in these steps. Only the first 4,000 characters are read. The elements of the harness are taken out with all they hold: `<event …>` (with its `<result>` and `<log>`), `<result>`, `<log>`, `<task-notification>`, `<system-reminder>`, `<local-command-stdout>`, `<local-command-stderr>`, `<local-command-caveat>` and `<user-prompt-submit-hook>`, each up to its closing tag; when it is never closed or its opening tag is cut (Orca cuts a prompt to 200 characters), up to the end of the text if it starts a line (only spaces before it on its line), else only its opening tag (a cut opening tag in the middle of a sentence stays as typed), so `Fix the <log> view` gives `Fix the  view`; and `<name/>` alone; what is between a pair of backticks stays as typed. The other tags are taken off every line (`<name …>`, `</name>` and `<name/>`, with a quoted value that may hold `>`; the name starts with an ASCII letter and holds only letters, digits, `-`, `_` and `.`, so `<https://…>` is not a tag; a `<` that follows an ASCII letter, a digit or `_` is a generic such as `Vec<String>`, not a tag; what is between a pair of backticks stays as typed, as in `<div>`). The first line that still has text is taken, cut to 200 characters: a line that is only tags is skipped, so the text the owner typed after an `<event …>…</event>` is the title. A text gives no line when nothing is left, when what is left opens as data nobody types (the first three lines that have text, joined by a space, start with a keyed object `{ "key" :` or one whose key has no quotes, as a REPL prints a JS value, `{ key :` (a name of ASCII letters, digits, `_` and `$` that does not begin with a digit, as in `{ a: { isAsync: true, status: 'async_launched' } }`; `{ not json`, `{ a b` and `Fix {a:1}` stay titles; issue #693), an array of objects `[ { "` or of strings `[ "key" ,`, or an XML or HTML document `<?xml ` or `<!doctype `, in any case, as Orca's `getAgentPromptLabel` decides, but for the key with no quotes, which this app adds), or when its first line opens as the output of a process (`[stderr]` or `[stdout]`). A text with no line gives the turn to the next one in the order: a `prompt` that is only a message of the harness gives it to `taskTitle` and then to the title of the terminal (which Claude Code names after the question), and the `latest` of a row whose newer message is such a text (a JSON object of a tool, an `<event>` with a result) is none, and the list then draws the line. The title is never the last answer. The shapes met in a real Orca (2026-11-16) are the examples of its unit tests: `<event …>` holding a `<result>` of a JS value, of `git status` or of a script, a prompt cut inside an `<event>`, a `[stderr]` line, and a last answer that is a JSON object. A row of a saved environment is made by the same function from the tabs of the stream, whose `agentStatus` has no `conversationName` (not checked against a real stream), so its title is the `prompt` when the owner's text is in it, else the title of its terminal. The screen draws the line as text (`textContent`), so a tag that stays is shown as the characters it is. Under an open project (`.orca-sessions`, one level in from the project: its rows start right of the project's name, with the status icon and the title) is one row for each session of its tabs (`.orca-session`, a button: the status icon of the tab and its title, its latest message when the row has one, else its title, `data-session` the id of the tab; not `data-tab`, which marks a tab of the work pane). The row's text is its `latest` (A-6: the newer of the owner's last instruction and Claude's last answer, so it is the answer after Claude has answered and the next instruction once the owner has sent it) in `.orca-session-title`, as text (`textContent`); a row with no `latest` shows its title (the line of A-6), and the tab of the work pane shows the title whatever the latest message is. The conversation's name no longer leads the row: the latest message does, and the name (the line) serves only where the row has none. Why: the owner wants the latest exchange with Claude in the list, as Orca's own side bar shows it (2026-11-15, issue #690), and the owner's instruction stayed in the row after Claude had answered it (issue #806). A row whose `subagents` (A-6) is more than 0 ends with a badge (`.orca-session-subagents`, `role="img"`): a robot icon (an inline SVG) and the number as text, in the yellow of `--effort-high` on `--effort-high-bg`, right of the title and at the right end of the row (`margin-left: auto`, and it does not shrink, so a long title is cut with its ellipsis before the badge, never pushed past it). Its `title` and `aria-label` are 「サブエージェント {n} 件が作業中」. A row with 0 has no badge, and the tab of the work pane never has one. The list is drawn again when the number changes, and the tab strip is not. Why: the owner wants to see which session has subagents at work (2026-11-15, issue #693). A session row carries no chip of the environment (A-33), and the tab of the work pane never does. A file open in the editor (A-18) and the diff of a file (A-24) are tabs but not sessions, so they have no row. The rows are in the order of the tabs of the project as the work pane first sees them (the order of "Tabs" below, whatever the panes it is split into, A-17). The selected row (`aria-pressed`, the `--soft` background) is the row of the tab selected, which is the tab the focused pane shows (A-17), so pressing a tab in any pane moves the selection of the list to its row (only in the selected project); no row is selected while a file tab is. Pressing a row selects its project (when it is not the selected one) and its tab (it takes the focus of its pane, as pressing the tab does). Whether a project is open is kept in memory only (as the order of the working projects). The order of the working projects is kept in TypeScript with the open state, because it is a state between reads, which Rust does not keep (every read asks the CLI again); the status and the order of the rest stay in the pure function. The sessions of a launched child of a formation (#947, A-37) are not under the project: neither its Claude row nor a plain terminal of it is drawn there, because the child is under its formation's row. A session is a child's (`isChildSession` of `ts/formation-list.ts`, from the last read of the list of the formations) when it is in a worktree that tsunagiai made for a child (`FormationEntry.children[].created` true: the `path` and the PC of the item are the child's `path` and `environment`, `local` for this Mac, which the list gives as no environment), or when it is the terminal of a child that works in a folder that was there (`created` false, a launch with `folder`, A-37: the Claude row whose `paneKey` is the child's `paneKey`, and a terminal of the list whose handle is the child's `handle`, both on the child's PC). The folder of a child that was not made by tsunagiai is not hidden, because it is the owner's own (often the repository's own folder): the sessions of the owner in it stay under the project, and the folder stays among the worktrees that the plus (A-16) and 「Orca に渡す」 (A-9) offer. The folder `.claude/worktrees/` as a whole is not hidden either, because the owner's own worktrees are there too. The work pane of the project (A-6, A-17) has no tab of a child's session either: the tabs and the rows of the list are the same sessions. A project whose sessions are all a child's has no arrow, and its number is the sessions that are shown. The rows are grouped by the sessions that are shown: a project with only a child's sessions is among those with no session, not among the stopped ones, and not in the working group for the child's work (below). A work of a child does not open its project (the rule that a project opens when work appears in it does not count the child's working session, nor a project icon that works only for it); the list of the formations is read first, and the screen takes no work as the owner's before its first read has ended (with an answer or a failure), so that a read of Orca that comes first does not open the project for a child's work. The project's own icon and the sidebar button of A-6 count the child as before: the count of the sessions that wait, the notices and the yellow that fades while one works are not changed. A child that was closed leaves the record, so its worktree, when it stays, is an ordinary session of its project from the next read. The list of the formations is not read by this screen alone: a read of it that finds the children changed draws the list again.

Status icons, the same as Orca's. A pure function maps an Orca state word to one of seven: `waiting`, `blocked` and `permission` are `question` (the `message-circle-question-mark` icon, orange); `failed` is a small red dot; `working` is a yellow ring that turns (it stops under `prefers-reduced-motion`); `monitoring` is the `activity` icon; `interrupted` and `unconfirmed` are a small gray dot; `done` and `active` are a green dot ("完了", or "起動中" on the tab of a terminal); `inactive` is a faint gray dot ("動いていない"). The colors are variables in `:root`. A word not known yet is idle for a worktree and working for an agent, as in A-6, and an agent with `interrupted` true is interrupted unless it waits or failed. The status of a row is its agent's; the status of a project is the highest rank among its top-level agents with a live pty, its worktrees' `sidebarStatus` (else `status`), and its plain terminals (a green dot), in Orca's rank: question, failed, working, monitoring, interrupted, done, idle (`unconfirmed` is drawn as interrupted and ranks with it, one place above where Orca ranks it).

No SSH host is read or drawn by the screen (the owner's instruction, 2026-11-16, issues #783 and #784). The list of A-8 had them under the heading "SSH", read from this machine's Orca over its unix socket (`ssh.listEditableTargets`, the socket of A-7, not a public interface). Nothing of them is drawn, searched (A-19) or selected, and no plus (A-16), file pane (A-18) or scratchpad (A-29) works from them. Why: the owner does not want SSH hosts in the list, and the scratchpad of an SSH host (A-30) was opened only from the row of one, so it is retired with them. Rust does not read them (issue #785): it does not call `ssh.listEditableTargets`, `OrcaOverview` has no `ssh` and no `hosts`, and the rows, the projects and the plain terminals leave out the worktrees whose `hostId` is not `local`.

Tabs. Selecting a project opens it in the list (its sessions under it, above; the other open projects stay open) and shows its sessions as tabs in the work pane, which is split into panes (A-17; a project that was never split has one pane with all its tabs), and selects the tab that the pane with the focus shows (A-17; the first of a project that has no saved layout). Each pane has its own tab strip (`.orca-tabs`, `role="tablist"`, each tab a `role="tab"` button with `aria-selected`, which is true for the tab each pane shows). The tab bar of a pane (`.orca-tabbar`) is 44 px high at least (`--header-h`, A-23), level with the header of the list pane, and grows with the size chosen for the work pane (A-20; 32 px in proportion to it when that is more than 44 px, so that its text stays inside); it holds the strip (`.orca-tabs`, as wide as its tabs, which scrolls sideways when the tabs do not fit the bar) and, right after the strip, the plus of A-16 (when the tabs scroll, the plus stays in view at the right end of the bar, before the button of the file pane (A-18) in the pane that has it), and it is shown also when the strip is empty. A tab is 4 px lower than the bar (40 px at the first size) and at most 220 px wide, the selected one on the `--surface` ground with a thin border; it has the status icon and the title on one line, and a close button (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` "閉じる") beside it, which never selects the tab. The tabs are the project's Claude sessions (rows with a pane key), and its plain terminals, in the order they were first seen, so a read that reorders the sessions does not move them (a tab keeps its place in its pane, A-17). A project with no session says 「セッションはありません」 in the work pane, with the strip empty, and the plus of A-16 is the way to start one. The selection survives a read while its tab exists; when its session leaves Orca's list the pane shows the tab that followed it, else the one before (A-17). Nothing closes by itself, a finished session included. Every tab is a terminal (below, and A-12): a Claude session shows the terminal of its handle, and a plain terminal its own. A terminal in which `claude` starts is listed as a Claude session under the same handle (and as a plain terminal again when it ends there): its tab keeps its place in its pane, its selection and its screen (A-17).

Closing. The close button of a Claude session or a plain terminal of Orca calls `orca_close(handle, environment)`, which runs `orca terminal close --terminal <handle> --tab --json` (with `--environment=<name>` for a saved environment) with a timeout of 15 seconds; the handle is validated as in A-6, and the environment must be a saved one. The answer's `ptyKilled` is not looked at (it is false on a real machine although the process stops). `error.code` `terminal_handle_stale` is not a success, because Orca answers it for a terminal that runs too (its log shows `--tab` closes refused with it for a running terminal, which then stopped with `ptyKilled` true when closed with no `--tab`): the terminal is then closed once more by `orca terminal close --terminal <handle> --json` (the same timeout and environment), and the answer of that is read like any other, so `terminal_handle_stale` again, or any failure, is an error and the tab stays. On success the screen reads the list at once (a streamed list, A-12, says it by itself and is not read) and the tab leaves with it; on a failure (of the first close or of the second) the message is shown (on a card, A-28) and the tab stays, and the screen reads the list again (a streamed list is not read), so that a tab Orca no longer holds leaves at once. A tab whose session is gone from Orca's list leaves at the next read. A Claude session with no terminal handle cannot be closed.

Terminals of Orca. A terminal of `orca terminal list` that no Claude row of the same read holds (A-6: the row's handle is the terminal's; a shell, or another agent), still has its tab (not `orphaned`), and is in the worktree of a project is a tab of that project, titled by its `title`, with the green dot; the floating terminal has no worktree and is left out. A Claude session's tab shows the terminal of its handle. The kind is the row's alone, and `agentIdentity` is not looked at, because Orca sets it from the process or the title before `worktree ps` has the agent (and the other way round when the agent ends): so in every read a terminal is held by a Claude row or is a plain terminal, never neither and never both. Every terminal is drawn by xterm.js (`src/vendor/xterm.js`, `xterm.css`, `addon-fit.js`, `addon-unicode11.js` and `addon-web-links.js`, kept as distributed, with their version and license in a comment, and read as classic scripts by `ts/vendor-load.ts` the first time a terminal is to be shown, not at start-up, so a terminal waits for that one read and the start of the app does not; the CSP is unchanged; scrollback 5000 lines; `ts/orca-terminal.ts`). The colors of every terminal, and of the pane around it, are those of the theme chosen in the settings for the appearance of A-15 (by default the system's, A-14); the font and the keys that send a text are Ghostty's (A-13). With a saved environment as the target the terminal is streamed (A-12). With this machine as the target the tab is a mirror, because this machine's Orca cannot stream the bytes of a terminal to this app (A-12 has the measured reason): sized to its pane when it is shown (`fit`, and a `ResizeObserver` on the host element of each pane whose fit waits until the size has rested for 50 ms), and while it is shown (in each pane, the tab it shows, A-17) the screen reads the terminal over this machine's unix socket (the socket of A-7; `terminal.read` with `{terminal, screen: true}`, which is what `orca terminal read --screen` sends, so no CLI process is started: the CLI costs about 0.1 seconds of start-up each) every second, one at a time, and takes `result.terminal.tail`, an array of lines with no colors; an answer with no `result.terminal.source` is an Orca that ignores `screen` (it answers the accumulated output), which is refused with a message to update Orca, as the CLI refuses it (`incompatible_runtime`), and draws the frame again in xterm.js when it changed. The keys typed are sent with no Enter added, so control bytes reach the terminal as typed, batched (at most 50,000 characters a send) and one send at a time, and the frame is read again soon after while the terminal is shown; the keys not sent yet go on to the terminal when it stops showing, and are dropped only when it leaves the list (A-12). What is typed can be a password, so it is kept out of the arguments of a process (`ps` shows them): the keys go to this machine's Orca over the unix socket of A-7 as `terminal.send` (`{terminal, text, enter: false, client: {id: "tsunagiai", type: "desktop"}}`, as the CLI sends them; the answer's `result.send.accepted` false is a failure). A key text is not checked for blank, but it may not be empty or hold a NUL or more than 100,000 characters. The handle must belong to a row or a plain terminal of the list read just now, for a read as for keys; it is checked the first time a read or keys go to that handle, and again after a read or a send of it failed. The mirror has no text colors (it draws the screen in the theme's foreground on its background), a full-screen program is only approximate, and a size change is not sent. A terminal's screen and keys are in no log line, error text or event of the app. A failed send shows its message (on a card, A-28); a failed read is tried again at the next second. The reads of a terminal stop while another tab is shown in its pane, another screen is shown or the window is hidden (the terminals shown read at once when the window is shown again), and the screen of a terminal is dropped when it leaves the list. Links. A URL in the text of a terminal (this machine's mirror and a saved environment's stream alike), and a link that a program sets with OSC 8, is a link: the text `http://` or `https://` up to a space, a quote, a bracket, or a full-width punctuation mark or bracket (U+3000 to U+303F and U+FF00 to U+FFEF, so a URL in a Japanese sentence ends where the sentence goes on), found by `addon-web-links.js` (`@xterm/addon-web-links` 0.12.0, the version that goes with xterm.js 6.0.0) and, for OSC 8, by `linkHandler` of xterm.js with `allowNonHttpProtocols` (as Orca sets it; without it xterm.js drops a link of another scheme before the click), both with the same click rule. A click opens the link in the default browser only with ⌘ held, Ctrl and Alt not held, and the left button; a click without ⌘ selects as before. After it opens, the selection is cleared. While the pointer is over a link, the URL is the `title` of the terminal's box. The click calls `orca_open_url(url)`, which reads the URL again with the `url` crate and opens it with the opener only when its scheme is `http` or `https` and it has a host (any other scheme, such as `file:` or `javascript:`, and text that is no URL, fail with 「開けるのは http か https の URL だけです」, shown on a card (A-28)). The rules follow Orca 1.4.250's own terminal and its `shell:openUrl`.

Keys. While a terminal has the focus the app's own key handlers do not act: Escape only closes a menu, popup or hint that is open (none is while a terminal is clicked), and Cmd+Z of the inbox leaves a textarea's own, which is xterm.js's input. Cmd+C copies the selection and Cmd+V pastes, as in any field. Cmd+J of A-19 is the one key that acts then: it moves to the search field.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the status mapping and rank, the projects and their order, count and status, the plain terminals, the worktrees of an SSH host left out of the rows, the projects and the plain terminals, the screen lines, the stale answer, the arguments of close, read and send, the params and the answer of the screen read over the socket, the params of the keys sent over the socket, the validation of keys and handles (a Claude session's and a plain terminal's), the one-time check of a mirrored handle (kept for each target: a handle checked on this machine or in one environment is not checked for another), and the check of the URL of a link (`http` and `https` only); none touches Orca or the settings folder. The E2E spec `orca.e2e.ts` covers the project list, the arrows and the sessions under the open projects (an arrow only for a project with sessions, the working projects open at first, several projects open at once, a session row indented right of the project's name, the close and the reopen by the arrow with the tabs, the work pane and the number kept, the rows in the order of the tabs, a row selecting its project and its tab and a tab moving the selected row), the order of the working projects (kept through a read that reorders them, a project that begins to work in front and open again, one that stops below, one that only waits below the working ones, one with a working and a waiting session in the group and open through a question and its answer, and one the user closed that opens when another session of it begins to work and stays closed on a read that adds none), the icons, the tabs and their selection and order, the close of an Orca tab (with its arguments, a failure and a tab that Orca closed first), and the mirror of a Claude session and a plain terminal (the reads, the frames, the fit inside the pane, the keys and the pause while the window is hidden), and the ⌘+click on a link (the URL sent to `orca_open_url`, nothing sent without ⌘, with Ctrl or Alt, or by another button, the URL cut at a full-width mark and before a closing bracket, the title, an OSC 8 link, and a refused URL shown) in the mirror and in the stream.

## A-9 Hand to Orca

The open mail, Slack thread or Todo can be handed to Orca (A-6) from the screen it is on, in a pane (on the right side of the screen for the mail and the Slack thread, under the detail of the task for the Todo). Nothing is sent to Orca by itself: the user sees what is handed over (a card), chooses the destination (the Orca to talk to), the project in it and the worktree of a new session (one of the project's worktrees, or a new one that is made then, with the branch it gets and the ref it starts from), and writes an instruction, which is handed over as it was written (Claude does not summarise it). The content of the card is summarised by Claude (`claude_summarize_handoff`, A-2), and the summary is put inside the quoted block of the content, above its text (below), not in the instruction. Only the arrow button "Orca に渡す" (and Cmd+Enter) hands it over, and the content goes to Anthropic only when the user presses it (and only while the card is there). The form starts a new session only: it lists no running session, and after a handoff the pane shows that session's terminal, which takes the keys (below). The pane is `ts/orca-handoff.ts` (one class, `HandoffPane`, with a host for each screen); the content of each screen is built in `ts/orca-handoff-cards.ts`; the prompt is composed in Rust (`src-tauri/src/orca_handoff.rs`). It was written against Orca 1.4.248, as A-6 to A-8.

Entry. The row of operations of the open mail (`#thread-actions` above the subject, M-2, after "Todo にする") has an icon button `#thread-orca` (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` "Orca に渡す", `aria-pressed`, the Orca icon of the sidebar) that opens the pane `#mail-handoff` (`aside.ho-pane.split-chat`, `aria-label` "Orca に渡す") on the right of the screen, with a divider (`#mail-split`, a `.split-bar` with `role="separator"`, `aria-label` "Orca の幅"), set by `ts/split.ts` as the Slack thread and the compose screen do and kept in memory. The mail's pane is 40% of the screen at first, at least 400px, and the divider sets it between a third and a half (`aria-valuemin` 33, `aria-valuemax` 50). The pressed button, or the pane's close icon, closes it. On the Todo screen the pane (`#todo-handoff`, `aside.ho-pane`) is not on the right side: it follows the detail of the open task in the left part of the screen, under the comments, inside the same scroll (`#todo-main`) as the detail, and is opened by the icon button `#todo-orca-open` (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` "Orca に渡す", `aria-pressed` true while the pane is open, the Orca icon of the sidebar) in the header of the tasks (`#todo-body > header`), at the left of "編集" (T-1). The button is shown only while a task is open (the detail is shown), because the pane is tied to the task; pressing it opens the pane and scrolls to it, so that the pane is in view (a press while it is open scrolls to it again). The pane is as wide as the detail (at most its 980px) and as high as its content, with a border and rounded corners; the form is its content as it is in the mail, and after a send the terminal of the session is shown in it at a fixed height (480px), as the pane has no height to fill. The right side of the screen holds Claude's pane alone (T-7) and nothing of this pane. A task that has a session shows this pane by itself, with the button pressed, and leaving the task, or the page of a draft or the token form, hides the pane at once. The Todo's pane has no close icon before a send, since nothing closes it but leaving the task; its title row "Orca に渡す" is its first row. In the Slack thread (S-6) the right pane of S-7 is tabs "Claude" and "Orca" (`#reply-tabs`, `role="tablist"`, `aria-label` "Claude と Orca", `role="tab"` buttons `#reply-tab-claude` / `#reply-tab-orca` with `aria-selected`; Claude first and selected at first; the selected tab has a 2px accent underline and bold accent text, over a line under the strip; while Orca is selected the pane is at least 400px wide, and its width is otherwise the shared divider's of S-7): Orca replaces the log and the request field of the Claude tab by the pane (`#st-orca`, which takes their rows of the grid) and hides "クリア"; S-7 is otherwise unchanged. The Orca tab has no title row of its own before a send (the tab names it), and its form takes the rows of the log and of the request field together.

What is in view decides the pane. A pane follows the item in view (`host.current()`: the mail thread shown, the Todo detail shown, the Slack thread shown with its Orca tab selected; checked after each open or close of the item and by a one-second tick): another item, or none, hides the pane and lets its read go; the item it is for shows the pane again only when a session was handed to it (below), else the pane stays closed (the Slack tab is always open while selected).

The form (before a hand-over). The form is one column from the top down (`.ho-form.ho-fill` under the title row, scrolling when the window is too low): the title row "Orca に渡す" with a close icon (`aria-label` "閉じる"; the Todo's has none, above), the destination row (`.ho-new`), the frame of a new worktree, the note and the message, the card, the files attached and the field of the instruction in the frame of `ts/composer.ts` (below). The destination row has three chips in the order destination, project, worktree, and the field for a new worktree's name: the chip `.ho-destination` (`aria-label` "送り先", `aria-haspopup` "menu") that names the Orca the pane talks to ("このマシン", or the name of a saved environment) and opens a menu of this machine and every saved environment (`OrcaOverview.environments`; picking one chooses it; the chip is disabled while this machine is the destination and no environment is saved), the chip `.ho-project` (`aria-label` "プロジェクト", `aria-haspopup` "menu") that names the project chosen (its `name`, else "プロジェクトを選ぶ", or "プロジェクトがありません" when the list has none, and then it is disabled) and opens a menu of the projects of the destination's list (`OrcaOverview.projects`, which holds no SSH host; picking one chooses it) with a search field at its top (below), and the chip `.ho-worktree` (`aria-label` "worktree", `aria-haspopup` "menu", disabled until a project is chosen) whose menu lists every worktree of the chosen project in the list (`OrcaOverview.worktrees` with its `projectKey`) except those that tsunagiai made for the launched children of a formation (#947, A-8, A-37: the worktree whose `path` and PC, the destination, are those of a child of `formation_list` with `created` true, so the folder of a child that works in a folder that was there stays; the list of the formations is read beside the read of Orca's list when the form is shown and its last read is older than 10 seconds, and not while the pane shows a session, which offers no worktree; the menu waits for the list only when it was never read, and otherwise opens at once with the children held and reads the list again behind it; a worktree chosen earlier that is a child's is not the chosen one any more), the project's own folder (`isMain`) first, and "新規作成" last. Each worktree is a row of two lines (`menu-two-line`): its name (`OrcaWorktree.branch`, Orca's `displayName`; "default" for the project's own folder) and under it the git branch checked out in it now (`gitBranch`; no second line when it has none). The menu has a search field (`aria-label` "worktree を検索", the match of the project menu below, over the name and the branch; 「一致する worktree がありません」 when none matches). The chip shows the worktree chosen as "{name} ・ {branch}" ("default ・ main" for the own folder; "default" alone when the list holds no such folder, and while no project is chosen). A project's own folder is chosen when the project is, and the choice of a worktree is cleared when the project changes. Choosing 新規作成 shows the frame `.ho-fresh` under the chips (below). The menu of the project chip is longer than the window when the list is long, so it has a search field: `input.menu-search` (`aria-label` "プロジェクトを検索", the placeholder 「検索」, which is only the field's name) above the rows, which takes the focus when the menu opens. The match is the one of A-19 (`partMatcher` of `ts/dom.ts`, shared with it): a part of the project's name, with case ignored, the text trimmed, a blank text no search, and a character that a regular expression takes as its own (`.`, `(`) matching itself. A row that does not match is not drawn (`hidden`); when none matches the menu says 「一致するプロジェクトがありません」 (`.menu-empty`, not a button). One row is the cursor row (`data-cursor="true"`, a line in the accent color): the first at first and after each change of the text; Down and Up move it along the rows shown, stop at the ends and scroll it into view; Enter picks it (the same as pressing it: the menu closes and the project is chosen) and with no row does nothing; an Enter or an arrow that confirms an IME conversion is not taken; Escape closes the menu, as for every menu; a click on a row picks it. The menu fits the window (`max-height`): the rows scroll in `.menu-list` and the field stays in view, and the menu keeps its top, left and width while the rows shrink. The menu of the destination chip has no field. The match is the same one-line comparison as A-19's, so it is in the frontend and not a Rust function (A-19 gives the reason). The branch of the own folder is never switched by a handoff. The frame of a worktree to make (`.ho-fresh`, shown only while 新規作成 is chosen) has three fields, each under its heading (`.ho-field-title`), from the top: 「名前」, the field `.ho-name` (`aria-label` "worktree の名前", no placeholder); 「作成元」, the chip `.ho-base` (`aria-label` "作成元", `aria-haspopup` "dialog") that shows the ref the worktree starts from, "既定" (Orca's default) or a branch, and opens the list of branches of `ts/orca-branch.ts` (A-25) with 「既定」 above the sections 「ローカル」 and 「リモート」 (the local branches of the project's own folder, read by `orca_local_branches`, and the remote ones found by the text of the filter, `orca_remote_branches` with the project's key; the headings and the first five remote branches (the last five picked) with the button for the rest are those of A-25, which also says that a pick of a remote branch is remembered) and no branch refused; and 「ブランチ」, two chips `aria-pressed` 「新しいブランチ」 and 「既存のブランチ」 (the first at first). With 「新しいブランチ」 the field `.ho-branch` (`aria-label` "ブランチの名前", no placeholder) names the new branch, and a blank one leaves the name to Orca. With 「既存のブランチ」 the chip `.ho-existing` (`aria-label` "既存のブランチ", 「ブランチを選ぶ」 until one is picked) opens the same list with no 「既定」 row and with a local branch that another worktree has checked out (`inUse`) or that the project's own folder has checked out now (`current`: a new worktree cannot take it beside that folder) disabled; the 「作成元」 chip is disabled then, and the worktree is made with `--branch` set to the picked branch (for a remote branch, its local name `localName`) with no ref to start from. Unverified: what Orca does with `--branch` when the branch already exists. Reading the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.256 (`out/main/index.js`, the creation of a worktree) suggests that it checks the existing branch out and makes no numbered new one; the command was never run with an existing branch. The hand-over is refused (the button disabled) while 「既存のブランチ」 is chosen and none is picked. The choices of the worktree (the existing one chosen, the ref, the existing branch) and the name and the new branch's name belong to the item (memory only): another item starts with the own folder, no name and 「既定」; the ones that name a branch or a worktree are cleared when the project changes. The sessions that are running are not listed, and nothing can be sent to one from the form. Under the chips: the card (`.ho-card`: the kind's icon, the title, the line under it, and a button `aria-label` "渡す内容を外す" that takes the card off), and the field of the instruction: a textarea (`#handoff-input`, `aria-label` "Orca への指示", no placeholder) that starts one line high and grows with its text up to 6 lines (then it scrolls), in the frame that `ts/composer.ts` makes for every Claude request field (S-3, S-7 and T-7 have the same part; the pane puts the frame, not the form that `createComposer` makes, in its own form, `#handoff-form`, so that the button submits that form), with in the frame one row under the field: at the left the model and effort selects of `orcaHandoff` (`#handoff-model`, the same values as the Claude tab of the settings, A-2: short texts such as "Sonnet" and "medium", no frame; loaded when the pane opens if they are not read yet), and at the right the button. The button is a round arrow icon (`.icon-btn.chat-send.chat-send-icon`, 28px, `aria-label` "Orca に渡す", not a text) and is the only way to hand over but Cmd+Enter; the field has no hint of the keys; there is no button "要約する" or "直す", no conversation about the summary, and nothing folds the chips into one line. The choices (destination, project, worktree and the name) are always chips. The name of a new worktree starts blank: Claude proposes none. The button is disabled while the field is blank, until a project is chosen, and, for the own folder, until the list holds it (the name of a new worktree does not enable or disable it, because Rust checks the name), while a hand-over of the item is on its way, and when the command is missing. It is disabled too while Claude cannot be used (A-4) and the card is there, and then a note (`.chat-note`, "設定の Claude タブで接続すると、Claude に要約してもらえます") is shown, as in S-7; the field stays usable, and taking the card off enables the button, because there is no content to summarise. Cmd+Enter (Ctrl+Enter off macOS) sends as the button does, and Enter is a line break. Pressing the arrow, with the card there, first calls `claude_summarize_handoff(context, instruction)` with the card (`{kind, title, body}`) and the instruction. Rust first checks the prompt without the summary and without the files (the instruction, the line that says the block is data and the block) against the limit of A-6, and refuses one that is over it at once with the message of the composition, before Claude is called and before anything is made or uploaded. Claude is sent the card alone: the instruction and the files attached (A-10) are not sent to Claude. With the card taken off it is not called, and the summary is empty. Then the worktree is made when 新規作成 is chosen, the files are put in it, and `orca_handoff` is called (below), all in the one press. A failure of the summary starts and makes nothing: the instruction stays in the field, and the message of Rust is shown above the card (`.pending.fail`); pressing again asks Claude again. The instruction, the hand-over on its way and its failure belong to the item, in memory only (another item neither waits for it nor shows its message; a failure while another item is in view puts the text back in the field of its own item, with its message, when it is shown again). The destination is, at first, the target the Orca screen holds (A-6); the pane then keeps the one chosen in memory for the next handoff (all panes share it), and choosing one never changes the target of the Orca screen or its list. The list of Orca (agents with their terminals, and the worktrees) of the destination is read once when the pane opens, and again after a send and when the destination is chosen (the first read, the read after a send and the read after a choice start although another read is on its way): when the destination is the target the Orca screen holds, by `refreshOrca` of `ts/orca.ts` (a list streamed from a saved environment, A-12, is taken as it is held, with no read), else by `orca_destination(environment, force)`, which reads the same lists of that Orca alone, after checking in Rust that `environment` is `null` (this machine) or one of the saved environments (a name that is not saved is a failed read, and the CLI is not run for it); `force` is false only for the poll of a shown session, which does not ask for the saved environments again when Rust checked that destination just before (the answer then holds none), and the poll does not start while a read of the destination's own lists is on its way. The pane draws from it; a missing command or a failed read, also of a destination that cannot be read, shows the notice of A-6 in the pane (`noticeOf`). A new destination drops the project chosen for the old one and waits for its own list. The project chosen is kept in memory for the next handoff (all panes share it, nothing is saved to a file; it is dropped when it is no longer listed), and is the first value of the project chip in the next one. Images pasted or dropped on the pane are put in the destination's worktree and their paths added after the instruction (A-10). Pressing "Orca に渡す" with a worktree of the list (the own folder or another) calls `orca_handoff` with the path of the worktree chosen. Pressing it with 新規作成 first calls `orca_worktree_create(project, name, branch, base, environment)` (`project` is `OrcaProject.key`, `name` is the field as it is, `branch` is the text of the new branch's field trimmed (`null` when blank) or the local name of the existing branch picked, `base` is the ref chosen (`null` for 「既定」, and always `null` with an existing branch)) once, and then `orca_handoff` with the path it answers; the files attached (A-10) are put in that worktree. A failure of the creation starts nothing: the instruction, the name, the files and the card stay, and the message of Rust (a Japanese error, such as the one for a blank or invalid name) is shown as below. A worktree that was made stays in memory with its project, name and destination until the session starts, so a send again after a failure of the files or the launch uses it and makes no second one. The points of the summary stay in memory with the item and the content they were made for in the same way, and a send again with the same content uses them and asks Claude no more. A failure of the launch keeps the instruction and the card and shows the message above the card (`.pending.fail`), as A-6. A hand-over on its way and its failure belong to the item: the pane held for another item neither waits for it nor shows its message, which is shown when that item is shown again.

The card. It is built when the pane opens (not for each item that is merely in view) and drawn as plain text (`textContent`): a mail is the open thread, oldest message first, each as `差出人: {from}`, `日時: {date}` and its text (the path of the summary, `mailText`), separated by `----`; the title is the subject and the line under it `{sender of the newest message} ・ 本文 {n} 字`. A Slack thread is its messages as the screen holds them, each `{name}  {time}` and the text (the text S-7 sends to Claude); the title is `#{channel} のスレッド` and the line `{first name} ほか ・ {n} 件` (the name alone when there is one author). A Todo is `タスク: {title}`, `プロジェクト: {project}`, `期限: {due}`, `説明:` and the description, and `コメント:` with the comments when they are already loaded (none are fetched for this); the title is the task's and the line `説明 {n} 字 ・ {due}` (`説明なし` when the description is empty). The card of the Slack thread and of the Todo is built again when the thread is read or the task is drawn again, until it is sent or taken off. The card shows no text of the content: its title and the line under it are all it shows, and there is no way to read the text that will be sent before it is sent (the body is exactly the string the command gets as `context.body`). The app does not try to detect hidden text (text hidden by a style, for one), so what a mail holds that the screen does not show is sent with the rest. The card keeps its nodes when it is drawn again (the Slack thread read again, the Todo drawn again, the one-second follow of the host): only their text is set. Another item drops it, and taking the card off drops it. Taking the card off makes the post a plain one, with no block.

The command. `orca_handoff(destination, instruction, files, summary, context, environment)`: `destination` is `{kind: "new", path}` (a worktree); `instruction` is what the user wrote, as it was written; `files` is the text that each upload of A-10 answered; `summary` is the points of Claude's summary of the content (empty without a card); `context` is `null` or `{kind, title, body}`; `environment` is the destination of the pane (`null`: this machine, else a saved environment's name), not the target the Orca screen holds. Rust composes the prompt with pure functions (`orca_handoff::handoff_instruction`, which makes the instruction as it was written, then, after a blank line, each file's text on a line of its own, a blank file left out; and then `orca_handoff::compose`, which quotes the content after it, with the summary inside the block) and then runs the launch of A-6 once, with its checks (the list is read again, the path is a worktree of it, the environment is a saved one, unless the worktree was just made by `orca_worktree_create`, below; the text is not blank and the whole prompt is at most 100,000 characters; Japanese errors; never tried again). A blank instruction is refused before anything runs (「指示を書いてください」), and a prompt over the limit says that the instruction and the content together may be 100,000 characters. The answer is `{paneKey, handle}` of A-6's launch. The path is a worktree of the list read just then (the list is read again at each launch, not taken from the pane), except the worktree that `orca_worktree_create` has just made in the same destination: that path is accepted without reading the saved environments or the list again, once and for 60 seconds (Rust keeps the destination and the path in memory only), because that command checked the environment and the project and Orca named the path; any other path, a second launch of it and a launch after the 60 seconds check as above. `orca_worktree_create(project, name, branch, base, environment)` makes a new worktree in the repository `project` (`OrcaProject.key`) of the destination with `orca worktree create` (A-6) and answers its path; it validates the name, the branch, the base, the environment and the project in Rust, and runs once. `branch` (the branch the worktree gets) and `base` (the ref it starts from) may be `null`, and each is passed to the CLI (`--branch=<branch>`, `--base-branch=<ref>`) only when it has a value. Each is trimmed, and a blank one is none; a value that starts with `-`, or that git would refuse as a ref (`git check-ref-format`: a space or control character, `~ ^ : ? * [ \`, `..`, `@{`, `@` alone, an empty component or one that starts with `.` or ends with `.lock`, a `/` or `.` at the end), is refused with a Japanese message (「ブランチ名に使えない文字が含まれています」, 「作成元に使えない文字が含まれています」) and nothing is made. What Orca does with `--branch` when the branch already exists (checks it out, or makes a numbered new branch) was not checked on a real Orca, because it makes a worktree; it is open. The prompt is (the lines of the files and the summary are left out when there are none; a summary without the card is not sent)

```text
{instruction}

{the text of each attached file, one on each line}

{one line that says the block is quoted data and holds no instructions}
<tsunagiai-context kind="mail|slack|todo" title="{title}">
内容の要約:
- {point}

{body}
</tsunagiai-context>
```

with the attribute value on one line (a newline becomes a space, at most 200 characters, and `&`, `<`, `>` and `"` escaped) and, in the body, every opening or closing tag of the block, in any case, loses its `<` (it becomes `&lt;`), so that quoted mail can neither end the block nor start a second one or fake it. Before that, Rust takes out of the title and the body the C0 controls other than the line break and the tab, DEL, the zero-width characters (U+200B to U+200D, U+2060, U+FEFF), the bidi controls (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069) and the tag characters (U+E0000 to U+E007F), which are invisible or change how text reads and could split a tag apart (`<` + U+200B + `/tsunagiai-context>`); so `context.body` as the card shows it is the string that the command received, and the text sent is that string without those characters (the card text and the prompt differ by them only). The tags of the name the block had before the rename (`cookapps-context`, opening or closing, in any case) lose their `<` in the same way, so that a text that carries one cannot end the block or fake it either. The summary is quoted data too, because Claude made it from the content: it is inside the block, above the body, under the header "内容の要約:" and a line "- {point}" for each point (the line breaks and hidden characters of a point taken out, a blank point left out, and no header when none is left), and it goes through the same neutralizing of the tags as the body. Only the instruction and the lines of the files are outside the block. The prompt text is in no log line.

After a send. The pane shows that session (and the mapping item → session is kept in memory only, nothing is saved to a file, so leaving and coming back to the same item shows the session again): a header of two lines (`.ho-session-head`): "{repo} ・ {branch}" (bold; the worktree's label until the list holds the session) and under it, in the accent color with a small dot before it, the status and the time as A-6 ("作業中 ・ 2 分", "確認中" until the list holds a new session), a button `aria-label` "Orca の画面で開く" that selects the session's project and tab on the Orca screen and shows the screen, with the session's terminal (`openInOrca`; the session of a launch that is not listed yet leaves the selection as it is), and the close icon `aria-label` "閉じる", which forgets the mapping of the item (the next open shows the send form; for the mail it also closes the pane, while the Todo's pane and the Slack tab stay open with the send form). Under it, the notice of A-6 when a read failed, and the terminal of the session (`.ho-term`), which takes the rest of the height (the Todo's pane has no height to fill, so its terminal is 480px high). There is no field for more instructions and no strip of files: the user types into the terminal, and the files are pasted or dropped on it (A-10). The pane stays as it is until its close icon is pressed, and the terminal follows Orca meanwhile (A-12). The list of the session's own destination (the one it was started with) is read again every 10 seconds while a session is shown (not while the window is hidden, not while the command is missing, and not while a read is on its way) for the header. A session whose row has been in the list and is no longer in a ready list shows "終了" in place of its state, and its terminal is let go: the stream is ended or the mirror stops reading, even though the launch had answered a handle, and the pane says the session was started (`.ho-note`).
The terminal. The terminal is that of A-12 (`ts/orca-terminal.ts` is the one place that loads and draws xterm.js; the pane is an owner of the terminals it shows, and shows and lets go of only its own). Its handle is the one the launch answered (`handle`), else that of the session's row in the list. A session of this machine is mirrored, as A-8 does: its screen is read every second, with no colors. A session of a saved environment is streamed when the Orca screen streams the list of that same environment (the stream of A-12 is one at a time, and is the Orca screen's); the stream of the terminal is subscribed through it, and the keys go through it. A session of a saved environment that the Orca screen does not stream (its target is this machine or another environment) is mirrored too: `orca_terminal_read` and `orca_terminal_input` take the environment, a read runs `orca terminal read --screen --json --environment=NAME` (the CLI route of A-6, with an argument list and a timeout of 15 seconds), and a send is `terminal.send` (`{terminal, text, enter: false, client: {id: "tsunagiai", type: "desktop"}}`, as the CLI sends it; the answer's `result.send.accepted` false is a failure) over a WebSocket of the environment that the call opens for it (the pairing of A-7, as the stream of A-12 opens its own), so the keys are in no argument of a process, as for this machine's mirror in A-8. Unverified: the send over a saved environment's WebSocket was tested against a fake Orca only (unit test), not a real paired server.
