The E2E spec `orca.e2e.ts` covers the call (its arguments with the tabs and with each operation), the panes drawn from the answer (a row, a column and a split in a split, each with its strip and plus, and the focus mark), the sizes of the panes from the shares of the answer, the drag of a divider (the two panes beside it following the pointer and the third unchanged, the `resize` it asks for at the release and the sizes of the answer drawn, the least share, a cancel, and a column's divider), the right-click menu and its two items with their line icons (no colored dot), the copies of a file's tab (their line, their texts, the clipboard and the toast, and the copies alone in a pane of one tab), no copy on a terminal, a Claude session and a diff, no menu for a pane of one tab with no copy, the drag to each edge and to the bar and the middle, the hint and its Escape, the drops that change nothing, the tab that was dragged not being selected, pressing a tab and a pane, the terminals of two panes on this machine and on a saved environment (each read or streamed, its events drawn in its own pane, the keys sent to its terminal, the close that names one terminal, and the new size of both after a drag of the divider), a tab that left and the answer without its pane, the plus of a second pane, and a failed call.

## A-18 Orca files and editor

The Orca screen (A-6) shows the files of a worktree as a tree on the right of the work pane, as Orca's own window does, and a file opens in an editor of つなぎAI as a tab of the work pane, where what is typed is saved by itself. Rust is `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs` (the commands `orca_files_list`, `orca_files_ignored`, `orca_file_read`, `orca_file_write`, `orca_file_create`, `orca_files_delete`, `orca_file_move`, `editor_settings` and `editor_settings_set`; the language of a file is A-21); the frontend is `ts/orca-files.ts` (the file pane), `ts/orca-editor.ts` (the editor), `ts/editor-settings.ts` (the card of the settings), `ts/orca-pane-tree.ts` (the button and the editor's place in a pane) and `ts/orca.ts`.

Why. Seeing and fixing a file next to the session that works on it took another window. The owner chose the layout and the behavior (2026-11-06, Discussion #119, issue #120): layout A for the file pane and design 1 for the editor.

The file pane. The Orca screen has three columns: the list (A-8), the work pane, and the file pane, with a divider between the work pane and the file pane that the user drags (its width is 15% to 40% of the screen, 22% at the start, and is kept in memory). The pane is hidden at the start. A button shows and hides it; it has the label 「ファイル欄」 and is pressed while the pane is shown. While the pane is shown, it has two rows: the upper row, as high as the tab strip of the work pane, holds the button at its right end (and, at its left, the three buttons that choose the files, the history or the language server, A-27 and A-34), and the lower row is the heading (below). While the pane is hidden, the button is at the right end of the tab strip of the pane in the top right corner of the work pane (the last pane of a row, the first of a column, and so on down to a pane), and no other pane has it. So the button is always one, at the right end of a row as high as the tab strip.
- The heading is the worktree the pane shows: 「{repo} ・ {branch}」, with no button at its right (the pane has no reload button: the read again is the one at the return of the focus, below; the owner asked for it to go, 2026-11-14, issue #583). While a tab is selected (the tab shown in the pane with the focus, whatever its kind), it is the worktree of that tab, and a tab of another worktree gives another tree, read at once. With no tab (no session and no file open in the project), it is the main worktree of the project selected in the list pane (A-8), that is, the one Orca marks `isMainWorktree` (the repository's own folder; `OrcaWorktree.isMain`, read from the list the screen already holds, with no new call), even when the project has other worktrees. The pane is empty when no project is selected or when the project has no main worktree in the list. The git branch of the worktree is a button in the heading, except for a worktree the list of the screen does not hold and a worktree on no branch (A-25). The tree belongs to one worktree of one target: nothing of the old tree is kept. Why: the files could be seen only after a terminal or Claude was started in the project, which was a nuisance for just looking at the files; the owner asked that the selected project's folder show without one (2026-11-09, issue #268).
- The tree starts with the entries of the worktree's own folder. A folder is a row with a chevron; pressing it the first time opens it and reads its entries from Orca; pressing it again closes it (no read), and pressing it once more opens it again and draws it from the entries that were read, with no read (a file added or removed on the disk in the meantime shows at the next return of the focus, below). A folder whose read failed has no entries to draw from, so it is read again when it is opened: pressing it closes it and pressing it once more reads it. Pressing it while its first read is on its way starts no second read. Why: Orca's own window draws a folder it has read without reading it again (2026-11-10, issue #361). Entries are one step in for each level. The folders come first, then the files, each group in the natural order of Orca's own list (a number in a name counts by its value, case does not decide). A folder that holds more entries than the 5,000 one read takes shows 「ほか {n} 件」 under its last entry. A read that fails shows its message in the place of the entries of that folder (an open folder keeps the entries it had).
- What git ignores (Orca's `git.checkIgnored`) is drawn in italics and in a faint color, as Orca does. A folder is drawn from `files.readDir` alone, with no entry marked; after it is drawn the screen asks `orca_files_ignored` for the paths of its entries, and the entries the answer names are drawn in italics and a faint color when it comes (the entries of the list are not redrawn otherwise). The entries a folder that is ignored holds are ignored too (a folder the list marked as ignored is read with `ignored` true, its entries are marked by the list itself, and `orca_files_ignored` is not asked). A folder that is read again (the return of the focus, below) keeps the mark of an entry the earlier listing marked, with the same path, until the answer of git comes, which replaces the marks (an entry it does not name loses its mark). A check that fails marks nothing new, leaves the kept marks as they are, and shows no message. An answer that comes after the tree became another worktree's, or after the folder was read again, is dropped. Why: the check took longer than the list and held the folder back, and Orca's own window draws the list first (2026-11-10, issue #363).
- The icon of a file (issue #965, the owner's decision of 2026-11-18: the icons of JetBrains). A file row draws the icon of the kind of the file in place of the line drawing of a sheet (`FILE_SVG`); a folder keeps its line drawing of a folder, and so does a file of a kind that has no icon (`notes.txt`, `LICENSE`, `Makefile`). Rust names the icon: each entry of `orca_files_list` has `icon` (`OrcaFileEntry.icon`, the name of an SVG, or null for a folder and for a kind with none), which `file_icon` of `src-tauri/src/orca_icon.rs` (a pure function of the file's name alone; the text is not read) decides, in this order: the whole name (`.gitignore`, `.editorconfig`, `Jenkinsfile`); the extension in lower case, for the kinds that A-21 gives no language (`toml`, `properties`, `patch` and `diff`, the images, the archives and the fonts); the language that `detect_language` (A-21) gives the name, so that the file pane and the editor agree on what a file is (`.rb`, `Gemfile` and `Rakefile` are `ruby`, and so have the Ruby icon; `ini` and `dotenv` have the icon of a config file). The screen only draws it: `ts/orca-files.ts` puts an `img` (`src` `vendor/jetbrains-icons/{icon}.svg`, `alt` empty, not draggable, 14 px, taking no pointer event, so a press and a drag on the row are the row's) in the `.orca-file-ic` of the row. The SVG files are the light icons of the New UI of JetBrains' `intellij-community` (Apache License 2.0) in `src/vendor/jetbrains-icons/`, with `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` (the commit they are taken from and where each file is from) beside them, 41 of them; they are the same origin as the page, so the CSP (`img-src 'self' data:`) is not loosened and nothing is loaded from outside. They carry their own colors: the name and the mark at the right take the color of a change from HEAD (A-39), and the icon does not. A kind that is added needs its SVG in that folder and a line in a table of `orca_icon.rs` (a unit test fails when the tables and the folder differ). Why: every file had the same icon, so the kind of a file could not be told at a glance (the owner's issue #965).
- What differs from HEAD is drawn in a color, with a mark at the right end (A-39).
- A file open in the editor (of this worktree) is drawn in the accent color. A single press on a row selects it, and the selected rows are drawn in the accent color with white text (`--accent` and `--on-accent`; the hover of a row that is not selected is as it was). With ⌘ (or Ctrl) a press adds the row to the selection or takes it off, with Shift it selects every drawn row from the row pressed last without Shift to this one, and a press with neither selects this row alone. A press on a folder with neither key also opens or closes it, as before; with ⌘ or Shift it only selects it. A row that is not drawn any more (its folder is closed, it is gone, or the tree is another worktree's) leaves the selection, so what is deleted is always drawn selected. A double press (or Enter on the focused row) opens the file. Why: the owner chose to select by pressing, as Finder and the editors do, and the selected rows in the accent color (2026-11-17, issue #826).
- The right button on a row, a file or a folder, opens a menu (`openMenu`, `role="menu"`, under the row). On a file it has four items in this order: 「パスのコピー」, 「相対パスをコピー」, 「ダウンロード」 (A-22) and 「差分を表示」 (A-24); a file whose name ends with `.html`, whatever the case, has a fifth after them, 「ブラウザで開く」 (A-31). On a folder it has 「新規ファイル」 and 「新規フォルダー」 first, then a line (`role="separator"`), then 「パスのコピー」 and 「相対パスをコピー」; the two that make something are below. On both a file and a folder the last two items come after a line (`role="separator"`): 「移動・名前の変更」, with a line icon of an arrow, and 「削除」, in the danger color (the variable `--danger`) with a line icon of a trash can; both are below. The browser's own menu does not show on a row. The first writes the path of the entry on the machine of the worktree (the worktree's path, `/`, and the entry's path from the worktree) to the clipboard, the second the entry's path from the worktree; each says 「パスをコピーしました」 or 「相対パスをコピーしました」 in a notice, and a refused write shows its message on a card (A-28). The menu closes on a pick, on a press elsewhere and on Escape, and its first item has the focus. Why: the owner asked for a menu in Japanese with the two copies, as Orca's own file pane has (2026-11-09, issue #269); the download of a file (#243) is A-22.
- A new file or folder. 「新規ファイル」 and 「新規フォルダー」 open the folder (it is read first when it was not, and an open one is drawn from what was read) and show, as the first row in it, a field for the name: indented like a child row of the folder, with the icon of a file or of a folder, the keys in it, and the label 「新しいファイルの名前」 or 「新しいフォルダーの名前」 (no placeholder or other help text). Enter makes it; Escape cancels it; a blank name (empty, or only spaces) cancels it; the field losing the keys makes it when a name was typed and cancels it when none was (the window going to the back is no such loss: the field stays as it is, with what was typed). The same two items are in the menu of the right button on the empty part of the tree (below the rows; the menu opens at the pointer, with no line), and make the entry in the root of the worktree, the field being the first row of the tree with no indent. A right button on a row never opens that menu. What is typed is kept when the tree is drawn again meanwhile (the ignore marks come after a read), and the field keeps the keys. One make is on its way at a time: the keys that come after it started do nothing. Rust makes it (`orca_file_create(path, dir, name, folder, environment)`, which answers the new path from the worktree). A name that is not one name is refused before any call, in Japanese (「名前が正しくありません」): after trimming it is empty, `.` or `..`, holds a `/`, a `\` or a control character, or is over 255 bytes (and the path made of it passes the checks of a path, below, and `dir` holds no `\`). When it is made, the folder is read again and drawn, and a new file opens in the editor as a double press on it does; a new folder is only shown. A name that is taken (an answer of `EEXIST` or "already exists", for a file or a folder) shows 「同じ名前があります」 on a card (A-28), makes nothing, and the field goes; any other failure shows its message on a card (A-28) and the field goes too. A result that comes after the tree became another worktree's is dropped. Why: the owner asked for the two items at the top of the menu of a folder, with a line under them, and for an input as the first row inside the folder as Orca's own window has (2026-11-14, issue #584).
- Delete. 「削除」 in the menu of a row and the Delete key on the tree (and the key that is called delete on a Mac keyboard, `Backspace`; not while a field of the tree has the keys) delete the selected rows. The right button on a row that is not selected selects it alone first, so its menu is of that row; on a selected row the selection stays, and when more than one row is selected the menu is the one red item 「{n} 件を削除」 (`n` is the number of selected rows), because the other items are of one row. The confirmation is one popup in the app (`confirmDelete` of `ts/hint.ts` for one row and `confirmDeleteMany` for several, a popup of the hint's frame, `role="alertdialog"`, under the name of the row that was pressed last; not `window.confirm`) with the title 「削除しますか？」, the text 「{name} を完全に削除します。元に戻せません。」 for one row (`name` is the last part of the path) or 「{n} 件を完全に削除します。元に戻せません。」 for several, and two buttons, 「削除」 in red and 「キャンセル」, which has the keys. Escape, 「キャンセル」, the press elsewhere and the same row's name close it and call nothing, and the selection stays; only 「削除」 deletes. A file is deleted as it is, a folder with everything in it. It is deleted for good: Orca's `files.delete` skips the Trash, so the text says it cannot be undone. Rust deletes them in one call (`orca_files_delete(path, entries, environment)`: `entries` are the selected rows as `{relative, folder}`, and the answer is `{deleted, error}`). It first makes a plan of the list (`delete_plan`, a pure function): every path is checked as a file's path is (never empty, so the root of the worktree is refused, and nothing outside the worktree, no `.` or `..`) and holds no `\` (a worktree on Windows has Orca turn `\` into `/`, so `\` alone would be the root), and a list with nothing in it is refused, all before any call; a path named twice is one (a folder if either says so); and an entry under a folder that is in the list too is dropped, because the folder takes it along (so a selected folder and the rows in it are deleted as the folder alone; a name that only starts like the folder's, as `src2` for `src`, is not under it). An SSH host's worktree is refused, and the target's list is read each time. `files.delete` then runs for each entry of the plan, in the order given (`recursive` for a folder alone), and stops at the first that fails: `deleted` holds the entries that are gone and `error` the message of the one that failed (null when none did). When entries are gone, the tabs of the editor of each file, or for a folder of every file under it (the same worktree of the same target), close at once without saving what was typed in them (the file is gone, so no save is made, and a save on its way is not waited for); the reads of each deleted folder and of every folder under it are dropped, they are no longer open, and each folder that a gone entry was in is read again, side by side, and drawn. A failure shows its message on a card (A-28), with what was deleted before it drawn as gone, and when nothing was deleted changes nothing on the screen (the selection stays). An answer that comes after the tree became another worktree's closes the tabs of the worktree it was made on and draws nothing. A diff tab of the file stays as it is. Why: the owner asked for 「削除」 last in the menu of both a file and a folder, in red, with a confirmation because it deletes for good (2026-11-14, issue #585), and for deleting several selected rows at once with one confirmation, a folder and the rows in it as the folder alone (2026-11-17, issue #826).
- Move and rename. The owner chose both ways of the two put to them (2026-11-14, issue #586). Rust moves in both (`orca_file_move(path, from, to, environment)`, which answers nothing; `from` and `to` are paths from the worktree): each path is checked as a file's path is (never empty, so the root of the worktree is neither moved nor a target, and nothing outside the worktree, no `.` or `..`), `from` holds no `\` (as for the delete), no part of `to` holds a `\` or is over 255 bytes (「名前が正しくありません」), a `to` that is `from` (「移動先が元の場所と同じです」) and a `to` inside `from` (「フォルダーを自分の中には移動できません」) are refused, all before any call; an SSH host's worktree is refused, and the target's list is read each time. Orca's `files.rename` overwrites nothing: a place that is taken shows 「同じ名前があります」 on a card (A-28) and moves nothing. The folders of `to` that are missing are made (below, "The calls"). Any other failure shows its message on a card (A-28). One move is on its way at a time.
  - The menu. 「移動・名前の変更」 turns the row into a field in its place (the indent and the icon of the row, the label 「移動先のパス」, no placeholder or other help text) that holds the entry's path from the worktree (`.orca/scratchpad/memo.md`), with the keys in it and the name selected: the last part of the path, for a file without its extension (a name that starts with its only dot is selected whole). Enter moves the entry to the path typed, trimmed; Escape cancels; a blank path and the path the entry has cancel and call nothing; the field losing the keys moves when another path was typed and cancels otherwise (the window going to the back is no such loss: the field stays as it is). While a move by a drag is on its way, Enter and the loss of the keys ask nothing and the field stays. What is typed, and what is selected, are kept when the tree is drawn again meanwhile. The field goes when the answer comes, moved or not. A new file or folder's field and this one are never shown together: asking for one takes the other away.
  - The drag. A press on a row, a file or a folder, that moves 5 px or more drags it (pointer events, not the HTML drag and drop, so the files dropped on the page, A-10, are not touched): the name follows the pointer, and the folder it would go to is marked (`data-drop="true"`, the accent outline): the row of a folder under the pointer, or the tree itself on the part with no row, which is the root of the worktree. The release moves the entry to `{folder}/{name}` (`{name}` at the root). Nothing is marked and the release does nothing on a file's row, outside the tree, on the folder the entry is in already, on the entry itself and on a folder inside it. A closed folder the pointer rests on for 600 ms opens (not the dragged folder or one inside it). Escape ends the drag, and the press that follows a drag opens or closes nothing.
  - After a move. The folder the entry left and the one it went to are read again and drawn; of the target, it is the nearest folder the tree has read, so that a folder made on the way shows; the folder it left is read first, alone, and the others side by side, as at the return of the focus. What was read of a moved folder and of the folders under it is dropped, and those of them that were open stay open at the new place and are read there. A tab of the editor of the moved file, or for a folder of every file under it (the same worktree of the same target), takes the new path: the heading and the title of the tab change, and the text, the typing not saved yet, the signature and the view (the language the file was opened with) stay. No save goes to the old path: from the moment the move is asked for, the saves of those tabs wait (a save on its way is waited for before Orca is called), and typing that came meanwhile is saved to the new path when the move is over; after a failed move it is saved to the path the file still has. An answer that comes after the tree became another worktree's moves the tabs and draws nothing. A diff tab of the file stays as it is.
- When the window gets the focus back (the `focus` event of the `window`, which fires once when the app is activated), and the Orca screen is shown and the file pane is shown with a worktree and the files are the part shown, the tree is read again, as the button of the heading did before (it is gone, 2026-11-14, issue #583: the owner does not need it). Nothing is read while the pane is hidden, the history is shown, or no worktree is shown, and nothing is started while a read of a folder is on its way. A read that failed is tried again by the next return of the focus, and a folder whose read failed is also read when it is opened.
- By the clock (issue #601). While the Orca screen and the file pane are shown and the window is not hidden (`document.hidden`), the clock of A-6 reads again every 10 seconds, with no return of the focus: the tree (the root, then the open folders, as at the return of the focus) while the files are the part shown, or the history (A-27); the files that the panes show (below); and the differences from HEAD that the panes show (A-24). Nothing is read twice at once (a read that is still on its way is the read of that period), a read that finds what is held draws nothing (the selection, the scroll and the open folders stay), and a read that fails says nothing and keeps what is shown.
- When the list of Orca shows another git branch for the worktree than the pane knew (a switch made outside the app, seen by the read of A-6 or the stream of A-12), the tree, the history and the files and differences that the panes show are read again at once, in the same quiet way, while the file pane is shown; the branch the app itself switched to (A-25) was read at the switch and is not read a second time. After the app's own create, delete and move the folders are read again, as above, and after a switch of the branch (A-25) the tree is read again. The read takes the root first, alone, and when it is read every open folder again, side by side, at most 16 at a time on this machine and 8 on a saved environment (Orca's own window reads 16, 8 for a paired server); a folder that was read and is closed is dropped, so that it is read when it is opened next. The pane is drawn again once for the reads that end before the next frame, and not at all for a read that finds the entries it holds (the same paths and kinds in the same order, and as many in the folder) or for ignore marks that are the ones shown. A read of the clock that finds the entries it holds does not ask `orca_files_ignored` either: the marks are asked for again at the next read that is not the clock's. Why the return of the focus and no watch: Orca's `files.watch` is a stream subscription, and this machine's Orca refuses streaming methods over its unix socket (A-12, `requirements/constraints.md`), so a change of the disk outside the app is seen only when the app reads again: at the return of the focus, and by the clock. Why side by side: five open folders took about 0.8 seconds one after the other (2026-11-10, issue #361); the root goes first so that the check of the worktree that a read may start (`orca worktree ps`) is not started by every read of the first round at once.

The editor. A file opens as a tab in the strip of the pane with the focus, after the tabs it has, with a file mark in place of the status icon; its title is the file's name. The tab is selected and its editor shows in the pane where a terminal would, with the cursor in the text. The editor has a heading with the file's path (relative to the worktree) and, at its right, the state of the save; under it a text area for the whole file (a textarea that takes the typing; the colors of A-21 are drawn in a layer under it, and a language the highlighter has no grammar for is plain text). The editor and the terminals are switched by their tabs. A Markdown file opens in a rich view in place of the text area, with a button for its source (A-26). A file that is open already is selected, not opened twice. A file that a pane shows is read again by the clock (above), and when its tab comes back into a pane after another tab was shown there (while the file pane is shown): only a file with no typing that waits for the disk (none unsaved, none being saved, no save refused or failed), and when the signature of the read is not the one held the text of the disk takes the place of the one shown, with the caret, the selection and the scroll where they were (the rich view of a Markdown file is drawn again and keeps its scroll). A file with unsaved typing is left as it is, so is one that was saved while the read was on its way (the signature held is no longer the one the read started with), and a read that fails says nothing. An open file keeps its text, its timer and its save while its tab is not shown, while another project or target is, and while the screen is not; closing the tab drops it.
- Reading. `orca_file_read(path, relative, environment)` answers the text (with LF line ends), whether the file has CRLF, the signature of what the disk holds, and the language of the file (`language` and `highlight`, A-21). It is refused, with a message shown in the place of the editor, when the file is binary, is larger than 384 KiB, is not UTF-8, or has line ends that differ (a CR that is not part of a CRLF at every line end, which a text area would change). The tab stays and can be closed.
- Line numbers. The text has a box of line numbers at its left (`.orca-edit-gutter`, `aria-hidden`), as wide as the digits of its last number (its width is set in `ch` when the count of digits changes, not measured from the numbers). The numbers start at 1, one for each line of the textarea (the empty line after a last line break has its own number; an empty text is one line), right-aligned, in a fainter color (`--muted`) than the text, with the same font family, size (A-20) and line height as the text, so each number is on the line it numbers. The box follows the vertical scroll of the text and stays where it is under the horizontal one; the wheel over it scrolls the text (by pixels, by lines of the text's line height, or by the height of the textarea, as the wheel event counts). A line is not wrapped (`white-space: pre`), so a number is one line. The numbers are not part of the textarea, so they are not selected and not copied, and they are not read aloud. They are kept in step with the text at the read and at each `input` event: only the numbers that came or went are written at the end of the numbers (one text node, not rebuilt), and nothing when the count of lines is the same, so a file of 384 KiB is not drawn again at each key. Why. The line of an error or of a diff could not be found in a file without counting (the owner's issue #378). Not in scope: wrapping a line and selecting a line by its number.
- The line of the caret (issue #885). While the text area has the focus and no range is selected, the line the caret is on has a faint band (`.orca-edit-line`, the color `--cursor-band` of `:root`) under the text and across the whole box of the text; it moves with the caret (keys and mouse) and with the scroll, it is not drawn while a range is selected (the color of the selection shows) and it goes when the focus leaves the text area, and the color of the text is not changed. Why. Every line had the same background, so the line the caret was on was hard to follow with the eyes; the rich view has the same band, one paragraph at a time (A-26).
- Saving. The file is saved by itself, and the screen has no save button. Once the typing has stopped for the time of the settings (below), the whole text is sent with `orca_file_write(path, relative, content, crlf, signature, environment)`; Orca writes it only if the disk still holds the text with `signature`, and Rust answers `saved` with the signature of what it wrote (the next save is made against that), or `changed`, `missing` or `not-utf8` when it refused. A CRLF file is written with CRLF. One save is on its way at a time; typing that came meanwhile is saved when it ends, by the same wait.
- The state at the right of the heading: nothing when nothing was typed; 「保存待ち」 from the first key until the save starts (each key restarts the wait); 「保存中」 while it is on its way; 「保存しました」 when it went through. The tab has a mark of unsaved typing (a dot, with the label 「未保存」) from the first key until a save went through with the text that is in the editor now; then the mark goes.
- A file that changed elsewhere. Before it writes, Orca compares the disk with the text that was read, so the editor never overwrites a change made elsewhere. When it refused, the heading says 「ディスクのファイルが変わっています」 (`changed`), 「ファイルが見つかりません」 (`missing`) or 「ディスクのファイルが UTF-8 ではなくなっています」 (`not-utf8`); nothing is written, the text stays in the editor and the tab keeps its mark, and no save is tried again for that tab (the user opens the file anew after closing it). Why this and not a read of the file's mtime before the write: `files.writeIfUnchanged` of Orca checks and writes in one step, so a change between the check and the write cannot slip through; the signature it compares is the one of Orca's `disk-baseline-signature.js` (two FNV lanes over the UTF-16 code units, and their count), which `orca_files.rs` computes of the text it read (`disk_signature`).
- A save that failed (the connection, a host that lacks `files.writeIfUnchanged`, a pairing that may not write) shows its message on a card (A-28), and the heading says 「保存できません」; the typing is kept, and the next key tries again. A text over 384 KiB is not sent.
- Closing the tab. A tab with unsaved typing is saved first (waiting for a save that is on its way, and saving what is typed), then closed. A save that cannot be made keeps the tab and shows its message (pressing the close button again tries the save again); a save that was refused (above) lets the tab close, as the heading has said the text is not saved.
- Closing the window. When the page is hidden for good (`pagehide`), the typing of every open file that can be saved (read, not refused, with typing not on the disk) is sent at once, without waiting for its timer, by the same call as a save. The window may close before Orca answers, so the answer is not shown.

The settings. The Orca tab of the settings screen has a card 「エディタ」 above the card of Ghostty (A-13), with the logo, the title and the `?` hint of the cards of the other integrations, and a row 「自動保存までの時間」 with a number field and 「ms」. The time is how long after the typing stops a file is saved: 100 to 3000 ms in steps of 100, 1000 at the start. A value out of the range becomes 100 or 3000, and one between two steps the nearer step (the upper one from the middle). `editor_settings` answers the saved settings and `editor_settings_set(autosaveMs)` saves a number and answers what it saved (so the field shows the value after the change). Both answer the range too (`autosaveMin`, `autosaveMax`, `autosaveStep`, held only by Rust), and the field's `min`, `max` and `step` and the range in the hint are drawn from them; the range is not in `editor-settings.json`. It is saved in `editor-settings.json` in the app config directory: `{"autosaveMs": 1000}`. A file that is missing or cannot be read gives 1000 (one that holds a number out of the range is brought into it). A failed save shows its message and the field shows the value held; a field that is not a number sends nothing. A change takes effect for the next key typed. The family and size of the text in the editor are chosen in the フォント tab (A-20), apart from the terminals; with none chosen the editor draws in `ui-monospace, Menlo, monospace` at 12.5 px, as before.

The calls. `orca_files_list(path, dir, ignored, environment)` takes `ignored`, whether the list marked `dir` as ignored, and answers the entries of `dir` from one `files.readDir`. `orca_files_ignored(path, paths, environment)` takes the paths of the entries of one list (at most 5,000, each checked like a file's path below) and answers those of them that `git.checkIgnored` names, in the order asked (a path that was not asked for is never answered; a check that fails answers none). The four file commands (`orca_files_list`, `orca_files_ignored`, `orca_file_read` and `orca_file_write`) take `environment` (where the worktree's row is: the name of a saved environment, or null for this machine) like A-10 and A-16. Orca is called as A-10 does it: `files.readDir` (with `limit` 5000), `files.read`, `files.writeIfUnchanged` (with `expectedExecutionHostId`, which Orca requires for a change, and `diskPrecondition` with `requireLosslessUtf8` true), `files.createFile` and `files.createDir` (`{worktree, relativePath}` with `expectedExecutionHostId`, answered `{ok: true}`; `files.createFile` makes the missing folders of the path and fails with `EEXIST` when the name exists, and never overwrites; `files.createDir` fails with "already exists" when anything is there), `files.delete` (`{worktree, relativePath, recursive}` with `expectedExecutionHostId`, answered `{ok: true}`; `recursive` is true for a folder only, it is `rm` with `force` so it deletes for good and a path that is not there is no error), `files.rename` (`{worktree, oldRelativePath, newRelativePath}` with `expectedExecutionHostId`, answered `{ok: true}`; it refuses a target that exists with "already exists", and makes no folder: with a folder of the target missing it fails with `ENOENT`, so on `ENOENT` Rust first asks `files.stat` for the source: when that answers `ENOENT` too the source is gone, 「ファイルが見つかりません」, and nothing is made; else (a `files.stat` that fails any other way does not stop the move) it makes the folders of the target from the worktree down, one `files.createDir` each, where "already exists" is no failure, and calls `files.rename` once more; when none was missing, 「ファイルが見つかりません」, and nothing is tried again. A `to` that is `from` with only the case of its name changed, in the same folder (`a.md` to `A.md`), is two `files.rename`, through a name of its own in that folder (`.tsunagiai-rename-` and 16 random hex digits), because on a disk that ignores case Orca finds the target there and refuses it as taken; when the second fails the entry is given its name back, and the reason shown is the second's) and `git.checkIgnored` (at most 2,000 paths a call), over this machine's unix socket and a paired server's WebSocket; the worktree must be one of the target's list (`orca worktree ps`), and an SSH host's worktree has no file calls (a message says so). The three reads (`orca_files_list`, `orca_files_ignored` and `orca_file_read`) do not start the CLI for each call: the worktree is confirmed against the list once, and the path and its `hostId` are believed for 60 seconds after, for that path (compared exactly, as the list is) of that target alone. Reads that find nothing believed at the same time (the clock reads the tree and each tab shown together) confirm once: the first asks the list and the others wait for its answer, a refusal included. The confirmation goes earlier when the screen's own read of the target's list no longer holds the worktree (a changed `hostId` is taken in at once, and the time is not renewed), when the environment is no longer a saved one, and when a call made with it fails; a path that was never confirmed, and an SSH host's worktree, are refused as before. `orca_file_write`, `orca_file_create`, `orca_files_delete`, `orca_file_move` and the download (A-22), like A-10, ask the list each time. Why: the start of `orca worktree ps` was about 120 ms of the 130 ms a folder took (2026-11-10, issue #362, measured in #360). The relative path is refused, in Japanese, when it is empty (a file), absolute, has a `.` or `..` or an empty element, a control character, or more than 4,096 bytes. Orca refuses the same, and a path outside the worktree. No token is in an error, a log or a return value, and the text of a file is in no log.

Checked against. The names and arguments of the calls were read in the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.253 and tried on this machine's Orca (a list, a read, a write in a throwaway file of a worktree: with the signature of the text read, with a stale one, of a file that is gone, and of a file whose bytes are not UTF-8); they are not a public interface of Orca. A saved environment (WebSocket) was not tried: it uses the same calls as A-10. `files.createFile` and `files.createDir` were read in the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.258 (the runtime method table, where they have the permission `workspace`, `files-mutation-params.js` and the handlers `createFileExplorerFile` and `createFileExplorerDir`) and were not run on a real Orca, so the exact text of its refusal for a name that is taken, and what it answers on a saved environment, are not checked (Rust takes `EEXIST` or "already exists", in any case, as a taken name). `files.delete` was read in the same `app.asar` (`files-mutation-params.js`, `deletePath` of the operation ledger) and was not run on a real Orca either. `files.rename` was read there too (the method table, permission `workspace`; `FileRename` of `files-mutation-params.js`; `renameFileExplorerPath`, and `renameNoClobber` of the operation ledger, which checks the target with `lstat` and then calls `rename`, with no `mkdir`) and was not run on a real Orca, because it changes a worktree: the text of its failure for a missing folder (Rust takes an error that holds `ENOENT`), the answer of `files.stat` for a source that is gone (Rust takes an error that holds `ENOENT`, as A-10 does), the rename that differs in case only (its two calls were never run on a real Orca, on a disk that ignores case or on one that does not), and a saved environment are not checked.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the path checks, the path of an entry, the order of the names and the folders first, the reading of a page and of a bare array of entries, the icon of each file of a page (none for a folder), the icon of a name (a Ruby file, a language, a whole name, an extension, a name with none, and the tables against the files of `src/vendor/jetbrains-icons/`), the list of a folder (one call, no mark), the ignored marks asked after it (in the order asked, only the paths asked for), the list of an ignored folder (no check), the chunks of the check, the paths of the check (inside the worktree, at most a list), a failed check, the confirmed worktrees (believed for the target and the exact path, the time up, forgotten after a failed call, dropped by a list read again, dropped with an environment that is no longer saved; calls made at the same time for one key running once and sharing the answer, another key and the next call running their own, and a call that waited for a run that panicked running its own), the signature against Orca's own values, the line ends and the refusals of a text (too large, truncated, not UTF-8, mixed line ends), the answer of a save (saved, each refusal, an old host's empty answer), what is sent to Orca by a read and a save (with a fake Orca), the check of the name of a new file or folder (trimmed, one name, no `/` `\` or control character, at most 255 bytes) and of the path made of it, the call that makes a file or a folder (the method, the host and the path sent), a name that is taken (`EEXIST` and "already exists"), any other failure and an `{ok: false}` answer, the root of the worktree and paths outside it refused for a delete, the call of a delete (`recursive` for a folder alone), its failures, the plan of a delete of several (a folder and the rows under it as the folder alone, a name that only starts like the folder's, a path named twice, the order kept, the root, a bad path and an empty list refused) and the delete of several one after another that stops at the first failure with what is gone and its message, the paths of a move (the root, a path outside the worktree, a bad part, the same place and a place inside the source refused), its call (both paths and the host), the folders of the target made after `ENOENT` and the one more rename, a source that is gone, a place that is taken and its other failures, the messages of failures, a CRLF file written back, the limits of the time and its steps, and the settings file (saved, read back, broken, out of the range); none touches the real settings folder or a real Orca. The E2E spec `orca-files.e2e.ts` covers the button and its place (in the upper row of the file pane while it is shown, in the corner pane while it is hidden, and always one), the pane and the divider, the heading and the root read, the folders and the ignored mark that comes after the list (and a check that fails, and an answer that comes late for another worktree), the folder read at the first opening and drawn from what was read after it, the read again at the return of the focus (the root and the open folders, side by side, at most 16 at once, dropping a closed folder, and nothing while the pane is hidden or a read is on its way) and the absence of the reload button, the read by the clock (a folder that did not change is not drawn again, a new file shows, a file shown takes the text of the disk and keeps its caret, a file with unsaved typing is not read, a failed read shows nothing), a file read again as its tab comes back into the pane, the tree and the file read again when the list shows another branch, a folder in an ignored folder, another tab's worktree, a saved environment, the messages of a failed read, the double press and the tab it opens, a file open already, the typing kept across tabs, a file that cannot be read, the numbers of the lines (from 1, with the empty last line, in the font of the text and of the editor's setting, a fainter color), their growth and shrinking with the typing and the widening of the box with a digit more, a long text, their following the vertical scroll and not the horizontal one, the wheel over them, and their being out of the selection and aria, the automatic save with its states and the mark, the restart of the wait with each key, the signature of the last save, typing during a save, the save when the page is hidden, a refused save (`changed`, `missing`, `not-utf8`) that is not tried again, a failed save, closing a tab (with typing to save, with none, with a failed save and with a refused one) and two files at once, the menu of a folder (the two items that make something, the line, the two copies, the line, the red delete) and of a file (with the line and the red delete last) and of the empty part of the tree (at the pointer, two items), the confirmation of a delete (the name, the two buttons, the keys on 「キャンセル」, nothing called on 「キャンセル」, Escape or a press elsewhere), a file deleted (the arguments with no recursion, the folder read again, its tab closed, nothing saved) and a folder deleted (recursive, the tabs of the files under it closed and the others kept, its reads dropped), a failed delete (the message, nothing changed), the selection (a press selecting a row in the accent color with white text, ⌘ adding and taking off, Shift selecting a range through the rows of an open folder, a folder opened by a plain press and only selected with ⌘ or Shift, the rows of a closed folder leaving it, a double press opening a file, the right button selecting an unselected row and keeping the selection of a selected one, the menu of several rows being 「n 件を削除」 alone in red), the delete of several (the one confirmation with the count and no call before 「削除」 or after 「キャンセル」, one call with all the rows, the folders they were in read again once, the Delete key and `Backspace` asking and doing nothing with no selection or in a field of the tree, a folder and the rows in it sent together, a failure after some rows were deleted showing its message, a refused delete leaving the selection and the tree), the field as the first row of the folder with the keys in it (and at the root), Enter making a file (the arguments, the folder read again, the file opened) and a folder (nothing opened), Escape, a blank name and the loss of the keys (with and without a name), and the message of a name that is taken, the move (the item before 「削除」 on a file and a folder, the field in the row's place with the path and the name selected, Enter with the arguments and the two folders read again, Escape, a blank path, the same path and the loss of the keys, the message of a place that is taken, the drag onto a folder and onto the empty part of the tree with the mark of the folder, the closed folder that opens under the pointer, no call for a drop on its own folder, on itself, inside itself and on a file, and the tab of the editor taking the new path and saving its typing there), and the icon that Rust names for a file drawn as an image of the app's own and the line drawing kept for a file with none and for a folder; `settings.e2e.ts` covers the card, the saved value, a number typed, what is not a number, a failed save and read, and the hint.

## A-19 Search the Orca list

The list pane of the Orca screen (A-8) has a search field that opens in the row of the heading 「プロジェクト」. What is typed narrows the list itself, the projects, to the rows whose name holds it. It is all in the frontend, `ts/orca.ts`; no command, file or call to Orca is added.

Why. The projects grew in number and the one wanted took a scan of the whole list. The owner chose the layout (2026-11-08, Discussion #199, issue #200): layout B, a field that narrows the list, not a palette in the middle of the screen as Orca's own window has it, because the palette hides the work pane and the field leaves the terminals in view while the row is chosen. The field was always shown under the heading at first. The owner made it a magnifier in the heading's row, and the field shown only when it is pressed (2026-11-18, issue #967, layout A of the two he was shown: the row of the heading becomes the field, so that the list does not move; layout B opened the field under the heading and pushed the list down). The wish to find a row by the name of a worktree or a branch was left out: the rows of the list are projects, with no row for a worktree or an SSH host (AI decision, written in the issue).

The magnifier. The row of the heading (`#orca-projects-head`, 36 px high) holds the heading 「プロジェクト」 and, at its right end, the button `#orca-search-open` (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` "検索"; 24 px, with the magnifier). Pressing it, or Cmd+J (below), opens the field: the row is hidden (the heading and the button with it) and the field `#orca-search` takes its place with the same room (the field is 32 px high with a 2 px margin above and below), so the list below does not move. The focus goes to the field with its text selected.

The field. `#orca-search` (`role="search"`, hidden until it is opened) has a magnifier, the text field `#orca-search-input` (`aria-label` "プロジェクトを検索", the placeholder 「検索」, which is only the field's name) and, while something is typed, the button `#orca-search-clear` (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` "検索を消す"). The screen has no other text about the field. The text is kept in memory.
- The field closes, and the row of the heading is shown again, when the text is empty and the focus leaves the field (a window that loses the focus keeps the field as it is), and with Escape (below). While text is left in it the field stays open, with the list narrowed, even when the focus is elsewhere.
- Cmd+J (Ctrl+J elsewhere), with the Orca screen shown and with the focus anywhere, including a terminal or the editor, opens the field when it is closed, and moves the focus to the field and selects its text. No other key of the app is Cmd+J (no handler for it in `ts/`, no accelerator in `src-tauri`; Orca's own window uses the same key for its search).
- The match is a part of the row's name, with case ignored; the text is trimmed, and a blank text is no search. A character that a regular expression takes as its own (`.`, `(`) matches itself. Only the name is searched: the name of a project. The sessions under a project, the worktrees and the branches are not.
- The letters that match are bold (`<b>` in `.orca-project-name`), every place in the name where the text is found.
- The order of the rows is the list's own (the projects, in the order of A-8). A row that does not match is not drawn. If that is the selected project, its row goes and the sessions under it go with it (they are drawn only under a row). The selection, the tabs and the work pane do not change with the search.
- When nothing matches, the list says 「一致するプロジェクトがありません」 (while the first read is on its way it says it is reading, as before).
- The keys of the field. One matching row has a line in the accent color (`data-cursor="true"`): the first, until a key moves it; a change of the text puts it on the first again. Down and Up move it to the next and the previous row, stop at the ends, and scroll it into view. Enter selects the row (the same as pressing it: the project opens in the list and its tabs show) and keeps the text, so that the list stays narrowed; with no row it does nothing. An Enter or an arrow that confirms an IME conversion is not taken. Escape clears the text, closes the field and takes the focus out of it. The clear button clears the text and keeps the focus in the field (it stays open). A cleared field shows the whole list.

Why this is not a Rust function. The testing rules want logic that needs a test to be a pure Rust function, and this is the one place where that costs more than it gives: the match is `RegExp.test` of an escaped text on a name, as the filter of the worktree dialog of A-16 does with `includes`, and the bold range is what `split` returns for it. A command would be called at each key for a list the page already holds, and would need its own mock in E2E for a one-line comparison. The E2E spec checks the whole behavior on the real WebView (rows kept and dropped, case, the escape of a special character, the bold ranges), so nothing here is left to a reading of the code.

Unit tests (`cargo test`): none; nothing of Rust is added. The E2E spec `orca.e2e.ts` covers the magnifier in the heading's row, the field that takes the row's place with the list not moving, its closing (Escape, and the focus leaving it with no text, while text kept leaves it open), the opening and the focus by Cmd+J (also from a terminal), the narrowing by a part of a name with case ignored, the bold letters at every match, a special character that matches itself, the SSH heading shown only for a matching host, the rows that go with the project that does not match, the line that says nothing matches, the keys (the first row at first, Down and Up and their ends, Enter selecting and keeping the text, no row, Escape, the clear button), and a read in the background that keeps the text.

## A-21 Colors in the Orca editor

A file open in the editor of the Orca screen (A-18) is drawn in colors by its language: comments, keywords, strings, numbers, names and so on each have a color. The language is decided by Rust from the file's name, as Orca's own editor decides it, and the colors are drawn by highlight.js in the page. Rust is `src-tauri/src/orca_language.rs` (with `orca_language_associations.json`) and the answer of `orca_file_read` in `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs`; the frontend is `ts/orca-editor.ts`, with `src/vendor/highlight.min.js`, `src/vendor/highlight-languages.js` and `src/style.css`.

Why. The editor was a plain textarea, and a source file was read as one color. The owner asked that a file open in it be colored by its language, as Orca's own editor does (2026-11-09, issue #296, parts #298 and #299). Everything else of A-18 stays as it was: the automatic save, the mark of unsaved typing, the handling of a file that changed elsewhere, the font of A-20 and the 384 KiB limit.

The language. `orca_file_read` answers two more fields. `language` is the name Orca's editor gives the file (`rust`, `dockerfile`, `plaintext` when it has none), and `highlight` is the name the highlighter of the page knows it by, or null when it has no grammar for it (then the text is drawn plain, as before). The decision is Orca 1.4.254's `detectLanguage` (`out/shared/editor-language/editor-language-detect.js`, `monaco-filename-language.js` and `monaco-language-associations.json` of `app.asar`), written again in Rust as a pure function (`detect_language(path, content)`) with its tables copied. In this order, the first that gives a language wins:
1. The file's name as it is in Orca's table of names (`Dockerfile`, `Jenkinsfile`, `Makefile`, `Justfile`, `CMakeLists.txt`, `.gitignore`, `.env`, `.bashrc` and so on), then the name in lower case in the same table.
2. The extension (from the last dot of the name, in lower case; a name that starts with a dot, `.gitignore`, is all extension) in Orca's table of extensions (`.ts`, `.rs`, `.py`, `.sh` and so on).
3. Monaco's own associations: its file names (`Gemfile`, `Rakefile`, `Vagrantfile`), then the longest of its extensions that the lower-case name ends with (`.hh`, `.csx`, `.vb`; a later language wins an extension that two claim).
4. A name that starts with `jenkinsfile.` is `groovy`; `.env` and a name that starts with `.env.` is `dotenv`.
5. Otherwise `plaintext`. Only a name with no extension then takes the language of its `#!` line (the first 256 characters of the text): the interpreter with its directory, `.exe` and the digits and dots at its end taken off, `env` and its flags skipped, looked up in Orca's table (`bash` and the other shells, `python`, `node`, `ruby`, `perl`, `php`, `lua`, `pwsh`, `groovy` and so on). A name with an extension that Orca does not know (`notes.txt`) stays plain whatever its first line says.

Orca decides again at each key; the editor of this app decides once, when the file is read, so a `#!` line typed into a file that had none colors it the next time it is opened. The name Orca gives is turned into the highlighter's by a table in Rust (`highlight_name`): the same name for most, and a close grammar for some (HTML, Vue, Svelte and Astro as `xml`, Apex as `java`, CUDA as `cpp`, `shell` as `bash`, `bat` as `dos`, `proto` as `protobuf`, `dotenv` as `ini`, `jsonl` and notebooks as `json`, Markdown variants as `markdown`). A language with no grammar in the page (`zig`, `lean`, `hcl`, `csv`, `mermaid` and so on) has `highlight` null.

The drawing. The editor is two layers of the same text (`.orca-edit-body`). The textarea is on top with its text transparent, so the caret, the selection (`--hl-select`) and the typing are its own; under it a `pre` (`.orca-edit-hl`, `aria-hidden`, no pointer events) carries the same text in `<span class="hljs-…">` elements that highlight.js returns, with its text escaped. Both take the same font family and size (A-20), padding, line height and tab width, so the letters sit on one another; the layer is moved by the scroll of the textarea (`transform`) and follows it again when the tab is shown after having been out of the page. The line numbers of A-18 are a third part beside the two layers: the two layers are in a box (`.orca-edit-text`) at the right of the numbers' box, so the layers' position and the text's padding are as they were, measured from the left edge of that box. The layer is drawn when the file is read and after each `input` event, so it holds the text of the textarea (the text of an IME conversion in progress too, without its underline). Up to 30,000 characters the text is colored again once a frame while keys come (the layer is the old one for at most that frame). A longer one is drawn plain at once when it is read and colored 150 ms later; while keys come, the layer keeps its old text and colors and is drawn again, colored, at most once in 150 ms (coloring 384 KiB takes longer than a key). A read that fails shows no layer, as no textarea. Nothing of the colors is saved, sent or logged.

The colors. They are the variables `--hl-comment`, `--hl-keyword`, `--hl-string`, `--hl-number`, `--hl-title`, `--hl-type`, `--hl-attr` and `--hl-meta` of `:root` in `src/style.css` (the colors of GitHub's light theme, each at least 4.5 to 1 on the editor's white ground), and `--ins`, `--del` and their grounds for the added and removed lines of a diff. The editor's ground is `--surface` and the app has no dark appearance for it (the terminals follow the system's, A-14, A-15), so there is one set; a dark set would be a second block of these variables with a dark ground.

The library. highlight.js 11.12.0 (BSD-3-Clause; 25,014 stars, last push 2026-11-01 as read from the GitHub API on 2026-11-09; languages are added by registering a grammar), as the files of `@highlightjs/cdn-assets`: `src/vendor/highlight.min.js` as it is (the common build of 36 languages) and `src/vendor/highlight-languages.js`, 23 more grammars of the same version joined as they are (`dockerfile`, `powershell`, `dos`, `dart`, `scala`, `groovy`, `elixir`, `erlang`, `haskell`, `clojure`, `protobuf`, `cmake`, `nim`, `verilog`, `delphi`, `fsharp`, `julia`, `tcl`, `coffeescript`, `handlebars`, `scheme`, `twig`, `pgsql`). Both are classic scripts that `ts/vendor-load.ts` reads together the first time a file or a diff is colored, not at start-up (#414): until they are read the editor draws the text plain and the diff draws its rows plain, and both are colored once the read is done. A read of a group that fails in part is tried again at the next ask for the scripts that failed only (`ts/vendor-load.ts` keeps the read of each script); after one failed read the editor gives up the colors of that file while it is open, so the keys do not read again. highlight.js loads no resource from outside, and the paths are inside `src/vendor/`, so the CSP is not loosened. Monaco, which Orca uses, needs a bundler, web workers and a loader, and CodeMirror 6 needs a bundler; neither fits a page without one.

Checked against. The tables were read in the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.254 (`/Applications/Orca.app`, extracted with `npx @electron/asar extract`), and the expected answers of the unit test were taken by running Orca's own `detectLanguage` under node. An Orca update can change the tables; then they are taken again. No new call to Orca and no new saved file is added.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the decision of the language against 204 pairs of a path and a text for which Orca's `detectLanguage` was run (names, extensions in either case, Monaco's names and extensions, `Jenkinsfile.*`, `.env.*`, a path with `/` and `\`, empty and dotted names, and the `#!` lines: `env` and its flags, versions, `.exe`, a first line that is not the first, a name with an extension), the order of the decision, that every name of the highlighter is a grammar that the two vendor files hold, that every Orca language in the table of the highlighter is one that Orca decides, the language without a grammar, and the fields of the answer of a read. The E2E spec `orca-files.e2e.ts` covers the libraries read only with the first terminal and the first colored file (and `inbox.e2e.ts` that the start reads none of them), the layer and its place under the textarea, the tokens of a Rust file (keywords, string, comment) and the text that equals the textarea's, the colors and the transparent text of the textarea, a language with no grammar drawn plain, the text escaped, the colors drawn again at a key, the layer moved with the scroll, a long text drawn plain and then colored, and the colors as variables of the page.

## A-22 Download an Orca file

A file in the file pane of the Orca screen (A-18) can be copied to this PC: the right button on its row opens the menu with the item 「ダウンロード」, and a save dialog asks where to put the copy. Rust is `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs` (the command `orca_file_download`, which calls `download`); the frontend is `ts/orca-files.ts`.

Why. A file of a worktree on a saved environment (a paired server) could be read in the editor but not taken to this PC, and a file that is not text (an image, an archive, a build output) could not be seen at all. The owner asked for a download in the menu of the file pane (issue #243, part #320).

The menu. The item 「ダウンロード」 is the third of the menu of a file row (A-18), and the menu of a folder row has only the two copies. A pick closes the menu and calls `orca_file_download(path, relative, environment)` with the path of the worktree, the path of the file from it and the place of the worktree (the saved environment, or null for this machine), as the other commands of A-18 do. The page sends no place to save to.

The save dialog. Rust opens the system's save dialog (`tauri-plugin-dialog`, from the command, as a sheet of the window and without stopping the main thread) with the last part of the file's path as the first file name. The place to save is what the dialog gives and nothing else, so the page cannot make the app write to a path of its choice. A cancelled dialog is not an error: the command answers `null`, and the page shows nothing. The page has no permission of the dialog plugin (no permission of the plugin is in any capability; `capabilities/default.json` holds only the window's drag, A-23): only the command opens it.

The copy. After the dialog, Rust asks Orca the size of the file (`files.stat`), then calls `files.readChunk` (or `files.readPreview` on a host that lacks it, and on a host that lacks `files.stat`, which gives no size to hold the preview to) and writes the file to a temporary file beside the place picked, which is moved onto it when every byte has come (the copy is whole or absent; an old file at the place stays as it was when the copy fails). The temporary file is a new file named `.{name}.part` (`.{name}.{n}.part` when that name is taken, and the name is cut at a character so that the whole fits 255 bytes), so a file or a link of the owner's with that name is never written to or deleted. The file is read 384 KiB at a time, and the reading stops with 「ファイルを読めません」 when Orca sends more bytes than the size said, so an Orca that never says it has reached the end cannot fill the disk; a preview is held to the size too (3 times the size for a text, because a byte that is not UTF-8 comes back as 3). There is no other size limit, and a file that is not text (binary) is copied as it is. The copy is not marked as downloaded (no `com.apple.quarantine` attribute), because it is the owner's own worktree file that the owner copies by hand. On a host that lacks `files.readChunk`, the file is read by `files.readPreview`, and a text file then comes back as a UTF-8 string, so a text that is not UTF-8 is not the same bytes as the original; a binary file is exact. The command answers the name of the file saved, and the page says 「{name} をダウンロードしました」 in a notice; the full path is not shown or logged. A failure (the connection, a file that is gone, a place that cannot be written, an SSH host, which has no file calls as in A-18) shows its message on a card (A-28), with no notice. The relative path is refused as in A-18. The bytes of the file are in no log, no file of the app except the copy, and no return value.

The same command works on this machine's worktree and on a worktree of a saved environment.

Checked against. `files.stat`, `files.readChunk` and `files.readPreview` are not a public interface of Orca. They were tried on Orca 1.4.254 on this machine (unix socket); a saved environment (WebSocket) was not tried, and uses the same calls. An Orca update can change them.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover `download` (the chunks until `eof`, an Orca that never says `eof` stopped at the size, the size from `files.stat` and its failures, a host that lacks `files.stat` or `files.readChunk`, a preview held to the size, the temporary file removed on a failure, a temporary name that is taken or a link in its place left alone, a name of 255 bytes, the old file kept, the paths refused) and the first name of the dialog. The dialog cannot be opened in the E2E build, so the E2E spec `orca-files.e2e.ts` mocks the command and covers the item on a file row only, the arguments of the call (a file in an open folder too, and no place to save), the notice with the name, nothing shown when the dialog is cancelled, and the message of a failure.

## A-24 Orca file diff

The right button on a file of the file pane of the Orca screen (A-18) has the item 「差分を表示」, which opens a tab of the work pane with the difference between the file at HEAD and the file in the working tree (what is not committed yet is in it), side by side or in one column, as the diff of an editor does. Rust is `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs` (the command `orca_file_diff`, which calls `git_diff` and `parse_diff`) and `src-tauri/src/orca_diff.rs` (the comparison of the lines); the frontend is `ts/orca-diff.ts` (the tab), `ts/orca-files.ts` (the item) and `ts/orca.ts` (the tab among the tabs).

Why. The owner asked to see the uncommitted diff of a file by the right button, as an IDE does (2026-11-11, issue #461; parts #462 and #463). The layout was chosen by the owner: side by side as the first view, and one column by a button of the tab. The difference between commits is A-27's: the same tab, opened from the history.

The menu. 「差分を表示」 is the fourth item of the menu of a file row (A-18), after 「ダウンロード」, with a line icon at its left; the menu of a folder row does not have it. A pick closes the menu and opens the tab (below).

The tab. It is a tab of the work pane (A-8) like a file of the editor (A-18): it joins the strip of the pane with the focus, after the tabs it has, with a diff mark (`DIFF_SVG`) in place of the status icon, its title is the file's name, it is selected, and it is closed with its close button (which asks nothing: it holds no typing). Its id is `diff:{n}`, and A-17 keeps it in the layout as it keeps `file:{n}`: the layout holds the id only, and the tab is in memory only, so after a restart the id is dropped from the layout. It is not a session, so it has no row in the list of A-8. A diff that is open for the file already (the same worktree and path) is selected and read again, and no second tab is opened. The tab shows in the place of the editor of its pane, and the tab strip, the split and the move of tabs (A-17) work on it. A diff of a file of a commit (A-27) is the same tab, with the short id of the commit after the path in the heading and after the file's name in the title.
- The heading (`.orca-diff-head`, the heading of the editor with the buttons) has the path of the file from the worktree, the count of the blocks of changes (「変更 {n} 件」, or 「変更はありません」 when there is none; nothing while it is read or when it failed), and four icon buttons (`.icon-btn`): 「前の変更」 and 「次の変更」, 「1 列で表示」 (「左右に並べて表示」 while it shows one column; `aria-pressed` is true while it shows one column), and 「読み直す」. A block of changes is a run of rows that are not the same. The two buttons of the changes are off when there is no block, and 「前の変更」 is off at the first block and 「次の変更」 at the last. The button of the view and the buttons of the changes are off while the diff is not drawn (read, failed), and the reload button is off while it is read.
- Side by side (the first view, `data-view` "side"). Each row has the line of HEAD on the left, the two numbers of the lines (HEAD's, then the working tree's) in the middle, and the line of the working tree on the right. A line that is only in one file has a blank on the other side (a gray ground and no number). Lines that were changed pair up, in order, on one row; when one side has more lines in a change than the other, the rest stands alone beside blanks. A removed line (or the left of a changed one) has a red ground, and an added line (or the right of a changed one) a green ground (the variables `--diff-del-bg` and `--diff-ins-bg` of `:root`). Long lines wrap.
- One column (`data-view` "inline"). Each row has the number of the line of HEAD, the number of the line of the working tree, a mark (`−` for a removed line, `+` for an added one, nothing otherwise) and the line. The removed lines of a change come first, then its added lines. A removed line is on the red ground and an added line on the green ground. The choice of the view is one for all the diff tabs and is kept in memory only; it is side by side at the start of the app.
- Colors. The text is colored by the language of the file (A-21: Rust names it, `language` and `highlight` of the answer, from the file's name and the first line of its text) with the highlighter of the editor, with the same colors. HEAD's text and the working tree's are each colored as a whole, so a token of several lines (a comment, a string) is colored on each of its lines. A language with no grammar is drawn plain, with the text escaped. A long text (over 30,000 characters in both) is drawn plain at once and colored 150 ms after, as the editor does.
- Moving. When the diff is first drawn, the rows scroll to the first block of changes. 「次の変更」 and 「前の変更」 scroll the rows to the block that follows or precedes the one last moved to (the row at the middle of the rows), and a block that was moved to stays the one the buttons count from. Changing the view scrolls to the block last moved to. A diff read again keeps the place in the rows. A difference from HEAD that a pane shows is read again by the clock of A-18 (every 10 seconds while the Orca screen and the file pane are shown), and when its tab comes back into a pane: the rows are drawn again only when the answer is not the one held, and a read that fails keeps what is shown. A file of a commit (A-27) does not change and is not read again. A tab that is taken out of the page (another tab of its pane is selected) and put back keeps its place.
- A reason. When Rust refuses (below) or the call fails, the reason takes the place of the rows, as a refused read takes the place of the editor (A-18), and no card is shown.

The call. `orca_file_diff(path, relative, environment)` takes the same arguments, and checks the worktree against the target's list and the relative path, as `orca_file_read` does (A-18: the worktree is believed for 60 seconds, an SSH host's worktree is refused, a failed call makes the next one confirm the worktree again). It calls `git.diff` of Orca's runtime with the worktree, `filePath` the relative path, `staged` false and `compareAgainstHead` true, over this machine's unix socket or a paired server's WebSocket. It answers the lines of HEAD and of the working tree (with LF; the rows hold their numbers), the rows of the two views, the blocks of changes (the row each starts at in each view) and the language of the file (the one of `orca_file_read`, from the working tree's text, else HEAD's). A file git does not know has no text at HEAD, and one that is not in the working tree any more is its text at HEAD against nothing.
- The reasons. A diff is not returned, and a reason in Japanese is, when Orca says the file is binary (the kind `binary`, or one side binary or an image), when Orca marks the diff too large to draw (`tooLarge`, `largeDiffRenderLimit`) or answers `diff_too_large` (over a paired server, a diff over about 4 MiB), when a side is over 384 KiB, when a side is not UTF-8 (Orca decodes bytes that are not UTF-8 into U+FFFD, as A-18), and when the answer lacks the text.
- The comparison. The lines of each text are compared with Myers' algorithm (the `similar` crate, which works in linear space; a deadline of 2 seconds gives a coarser, still correct, answer). CRLF is taken as LF before the comparison, so a change of the line ends alone is not a change. A line is its text and whether a line feed ends it, so a file that only lost or gained its last line feed differs (as `git diff` says). A change that replaces lines is a row of paired lines (side by side) and the removed lines before the added ones (one column).
- No text of the files is in a log or a file of the app, and `historyToken` is only Orca's handle of a list, held in memory, not a credential.

Checked against. `git.diff` is not a public interface of Orca. It was read in the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.255 (the schema `GitDiff` in `git-params.js` and the handler of `git.diff`: a method of the permission `workspace`, with the result `kind`, `originalContent`, `modifiedContent`, `originalIsBinary`, `modifiedIsBinary`, `isImage`, `tooLarge`, `modifiedDeleted` and `largeDiffRenderLimit`), and a saved environment (WebSocket) was not tried. An Orca update can change it.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the lines of a text and the rows (the same text, added and removed lines, changed lines that pair up and the rest of the longer side, a file with no line at HEAD or in the working tree, CRLF, the last line feed, the blocks of changes in both views, and two texts of 12,000 lines compared in time) and the answer of Orca (a new file, a deleted one, the reasons, the sizes, the arguments of `git.diff` and `diff_too_large`). The E2E spec `orca-files.e2e.ts` mocks the command and covers the item on a file row only, the arguments of the call (a file in an open folder too), the tab and its title, the two views and the button between them, the blanks and the grounds, the colors of the tokens (a comment of two lines on both), the plain text for a language with no grammar, the buttons of the changes (the rows scroll), a diff with no change, the reason in place of the rows and the reload after it, the second pick selecting the tab and reading again, and the close of a tab.

## A-25 Switch the branch from the Orca file pane

The branch in the heading of the file pane of the Orca screen (A-18, 「{repo} ・ {branch}」) is a button. Pressing it opens, right under it, a list of branches with a filter field, and picking a branch switches the worktree of the pane to it. Rust is `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs` (the commands `orca_local_branches`, `orca_remote_branches` and `orca_switch_branch`); the frontend is `ts/orca-branch.ts` (the list), `ts/orca-files.ts` (the heading and the button) and `ts/orca.ts` (which worktree the pane shows, and the read of the list of Orca after a switch).

Why. The heading showed the branch but could not change it. The owner asked to switch the branch from there (2026-11-11, issue #438; parts #439, #440 and #441). The layout was chosen by the owner: layout A, the button in the heading with the list right under it, the tree staying in view.

The button. The branch is a `button` (`.orca-branch-btn`, `aria-expanded` while its list is open); the part before it stays text. The branch is the git branch the worktree is on (`branch` of `orca worktree ps` without `refs/heads/`, `OrcaWorktree.gitBranch`), not Orca's name of the worktree (`displayName`, `OrcaWorktree.branch`, the name in the heading): a worktree made with a name keeps that name after a switch (Orca 1.4.256), so the two can differ. When the name ends with the branch the button is that end of the name; otherwise the button follows the name, after 「 ・ 」 (「{repo} ・ {name} ・ {branch}」). It is a button only for a worktree that the list of the screen holds, whose name in the heading is the list's, that is on a git branch (a detached HEAD, or a `branch` that is not `refs/heads/...`, gives none); elsewhere (a worktree the list lacks, or one on no branch) the heading is the plain text it was. The heading is drawn again only when the name or the branch changes, so the button stays while its list is open.

The list. It is a dialog (`role="dialog"`, `aria-label` 「ブランチ」) of the same make as the list of worktrees of the plus (A-16: the class `orca-dest-pop`, a filter field with the focus, a scrolling list under it), opened under the button and closed by a pick, a press elsewhere and Escape. It has two sections with a heading each, 「ローカル」 and 「リモート」, and a row is a button with the name of the branch (`data-kind` `local` or `remote`, `data-name` the name). A heading is a band (`.orca-dest-sec`: 13px, bold, `--accent-text` on a `--soft` background with 6px corners) with a line icon at its left (the branch icon for 「ローカル」, a cloud for 「リモート」), its name (`.orca-dest-name`) and, at its right end, the count of the section (`.orca-dest-count`); it stays at the top of the list while the rows scroll (`position: sticky`, inside its own section, so the next heading takes its place). The count is drawn once the answer of its section has come (not for a read that failed), and it is the number of rows that match the filter, so with an empty filter it is the number of all the rows of the section (the remote ones included, folded or not). The layout was chosen by the owner: layout A (2026-11-15, issue #652).
- Local. The branches of `orca_local_branches(path, environment)`, in its order (the one checked out first), read when the list opens. The filter narrows them by the text (a part of the name, case does not count). Every local branch is shown. The branch the worktree is on has `aria-current="true"` and a check mark at its right; pressing it only closes the list. A branch another worktree has checked out (`inUse`) is drawn faint and is disabled: it cannot be picked, because git cannot check a branch out in two worktrees.
- Remote. The branches of `orca_remote_branches(path, repo, query, environment)`, named as Orca names them (`origin/feature/a`), where `repo` is the key of the project of the worktree (`OrcaProject.key`) and `query` the text of the filter, trimmed. It is asked once when the list opens (an empty text) and again 250 ms after the typing stops, and the rows are narrowed by the text at once as well, so a typing key does not leave the old rows until the answer. An answer that comes after a newer question, or after the list closed, is dropped. While the filter is empty, only the first five rows of the answer, in the order Rust returns them (below), are shown (the constant `REMOTE_FIRST` of `ts/orca-branch.ts`). When there are more, a line is drawn under them (`.orca-dest-more`) and, centered on it, a small round button (`.orca-dest-more-btn`: 12px gray text, a `--chip-line` border, a `--bar` background, ends rounded, and a chevron at its right that turns over while it is open) whose text is 「ほか {n} 件を表示」 ({n} is the number of rows left); it has `aria-expanded`. Pressing it shows the rest under the first five and changes the text to 「たたむ」, and pressing it again folds them. With five rows or fewer there is neither the line nor the button. While the filter has text, every row that matches is shown, with no line and no button, and clearing the text brings back the folded state (an opened one is closed by the text). The button is not a row: it picks no branch and does not close the list.
