The lines of the places are read when the answer is in, four places at a time, each once: a file in the worktree by `orca_file_read(path, relative, environment)` and one outside it by `lsp_read_outside(environment, project, worktree, server, path)` (`server` is the server that was asked, `project` the key of the project of the Orca screen). A row draws, under its place, the 3 lines before and after the line of the definition (fewer at the ends of the file), read only, each with its number, the line of the definition marked (`data-hit`), and the spinner while its read is on its way. A read that fails is drawn in the row as the message, and the other rows are as they were.
- Open. 「開く」 opens the place as a tab of the editor (A-18) in the pane of the editor that asked: the pane that holds the tab of the file the press was made in (the tab joins that pane's tabs and is selected there, as a tab dropped on the middle of a pane is, A-17); when that tab is gone, the first pane that is not the definition tab's. The caret goes to the line and character of the definition, with the line in the middle of the pane. A file of the worktree is the editor of A-18 as it is: one that is open already is selected where it is and the caret moves in it. A file outside the worktree is a read only tab of the same editor: its text is read by `lsp_read_outside` (the path is the server's absolute path, and the server is the one that gave it), its tab has a lock in place of the file icon (and the mark 「gem」 when it is a Ruby file), its heading has a lock, the path and, for a Ruby file, 「gem」 in the place of the state of a save, the language and the colors being those of the file's own name (`language` and `highlight` of `lsp_read_outside`), the textarea is `readOnly`, and nothing is saved, written or read again (no autosave, no read by the clock, no read at its return into a pane); it closes with no question. It is in no list of the tree's open files, its menu has no copy of a path, and a move or a delete of a file does not touch it.

The language server in the file pane (issues #701, #961, #975 and #976). The owner chose the layout (issue #696, option 1, #957, layout A, and #973, case A: a card for each language, the languages that the worktree has found by the app, and the fields only on request; the part is the language server alone since #962, with the button 「言語サーバー」, `#orca-files-mode-server`, `SERVER_SVG`, in the place of 「定義」 in the top row): the third part of the pane, beside the files and the history, `#orca-server`. It is read only while it is shown, and the clock and the return of the focus read nothing for it. The part is `#orca-lsp`: a first row with 「言語サーバー」 and the reload (`#orca-lsp-reload`, `RELOAD_SVG`, `aria-label` 「再読み込み」), under it the select of the ssh host (a saved environment only), and under that the cards, one under the other (`#orca-lsp-cards`, `.orca-lsp-card` with `data-name`, the language, and `data-kind`: `server` for a language that the repository has a server of, `found` for one that it has none of). There is no field for the name of a server, no chip of languages and no plus: a card is one language, and a server is added by 「使う」 (below), by `tsunagiai-mcp` (A-1) or by Claude Code through it (the pane of "Claude Code sets the servers up" below, #976).
- How a language is drawn. Rust answers it: `lsp_language_looks()` answers a `LspLanguageLook` (`language`, `title`, `icon`) for every language of A-21 (`plaintext` too, each once, sorted), read once with the settings. The `title` is the display name of the table (「Ruby」, 「Rust」, 「TypeScript」, 「JavaScript」, 「Go」, 「Python」), and any other language is its own name; the `icon` is the name of the JetBrains icon of that language (`orca_icon::language_icon`, the table of A-18: `ruby`, `typeScript`, `go` and so on), none for a language that has none. A card draws the icon as an image of `src/vendor/jetbrains-icons/{icon}.svg` (`.orca-lsp-ic`), and the line drawing of a server (`SERVER_SVG`) when there is none.
- The cards of a server. The head row of a card (`.orca-lsp-card-head`) has the icon, the title of the language (`.orca-lsp-name`), the dot and the state in a word (`.orca-lsp-state`: 「索引中」, 「動作中」 or 「停止中」) at the right, and the menu (`.orca-lsp-menu`, `aria-label` 「メニュー」). The dot is yellow while indexing, green while running, gray when stopped and red when stopped by a failure (`data-state` is the state, `data-reason` the reason that Rust told with a stopped state, `notStarted`, `containerDown` or `failed`, and none otherwise; only `failed` is red). Under the head one line (`.orca-lsp-summary`), 「{the name of the server} ・ {where it runs}」: the name is the `name` of the settings, and the place is 「compose の {service}」, 「ホスト」 or 「方法なし」, left out (with its 「 ・ 」) while the way is not read. Nothing else is shown of a server, and no card opens or closes. The state of each server is the state of that server: it is read once with the settings (`lsp_status(environment, worktree, server)`, for each server) and follows the event `lsp-status` by its `server` (the screen listens once, for every worktree; the event of another worktree than the one shown is kept for it and not drawn). It is not read by the clock. The words of a card are changed in place, so that the fields are not drawn again by a change of the state.
- The menu. The menu of a card has four rows, in this order: 「再起動」, 「止める」, 「削除」 and 「手で直す」. The first two wait (disabled) while a press of a restart or of a container is on its way. 「手で直す」 opens the fields of that card under it, and the same row closes them; only one card has its fields open. The fields are 「サービス」 (the select `#orca-lsp-service` of the candidates, only when there are two or more: the one used is chosen), 「起動コマンド」 (`#orca-lsp-command`), 「worktree」 (the path the worktree has where the server runs, the mount, `#orca-lsp-mount`), 「自動で起動」 (a check box, `#orca-lsp-auto`, `autoStart`) and 「自動に戻す」 (`#orca-lsp-reset`). The fields are filled with what was decided (below). Each card has the line of its message (`.orca-lsp-note`), which shows the first of: the message of the last press of that card that failed (a save, a stop, the restart, the container), the message of the stopped state (`failed`, `containerDown`), and the message of the reading of the way (`lsp_launches`) that failed; none is no line. A refusal of Rust is its message, in Japanese, in the line of the card that was pressed; a message that no card holds (the settings could not be read, the languages could not be found, a save of the ssh host) is the line under the heading (`#orca-lsp-error`).
- The reading. Read when the part is shown for a worktree (not before, so no `docker` or `ssh` is run for a worktree whose language server is not looked at), by `lsp_settings(environment, project)`, then, for each server of the settings, `lsp_status(environment, worktree, server)`, once for all the servers `lsp_launches(environment, project, worktree, servers)` and, apart from them, `lsp_detect` (below); another worktree draws them again, and an answer that comes late for an older worktree is dropped. A read or a save that ends while the owner is typing in one field does not change the text of that field (the field that has the focus); the other fields show what was read. 「起動コマンド」 and 「worktree」 (the mount) hold what `lsp_launches` answered, which is the saved value when there is one, else the value that was decided (A-34 above); while the saved value is not set and a value was decided, the field has the mark 「自動」 (`.chip`, `#orca-lsp-command-auto` and `#orca-lsp-mount-auto`) at its side, and a value saved by hand has none. 「ssh の接続先」 is shown only for a worktree of a saved environment (A-33), is a select (`#orca-lsp-ssh`) and holds `sshHost`: its choices are an empty one (not set) and the Host names that `ssh_hosts` answers, read with the settings (when the field is shown, for a saved environment only, and not by the clock), and a saved `sshHost` that the list does not have is a choice too, the chosen one, so that it is never dropped by a redraw. `ssh_hosts` (`ssh.rs`, no argument) reads this Mac's `~/.ssh/config` and answers the names of its `Host` lines in the order they come, all of the names of a line, each once: a name with `*`, `?` or `!`, and a name that `ssh::check_host` refuses, is left out; the keywords are read without case, with a blank or `=` before the argument, a word in double quotes is one name, and a `#` that starts a word ends the line. An `Include` is read where it stands (a path from `~` or from `~/.ssh`, `*` and `?` in a name matched against the names of the folder in order, a name with a leading dot only by a pattern with one), down to 4 levels below the file itself, and a file that is not there or cannot be read is skipped; no file is an empty list. Nothing else of the file is read, kept or logged. A read of the settings that fails leaves the cards out, with the message in the line.
- Finding the languages. When the part is shown for a worktree, and at each press of the reload, `lsp_detect(environment, project, worktree)` is called (it is not read by the clock, and not by the watch of the settings); a change of the ssh host calls it again, because the host is the route of the read. The languages of its answer that the settings have no server of are drawn after the cards of the servers (JavaScript is not drawn while the settings have a server of TypeScript: `lsp_server_for` answers that server for a JavaScript file that has no server of its own, and it is told of the files of JavaScript on the disk too, so one typescript-language-server process serves both; 「使う」 on the card of JavaScript, while TypeScript has none, saves a server of its own), as cards of dotted line (`data-kind="found"`, `data-found` the state), each with its icon and title and one line: for `found` 「{the name of the server} ・ {where it would run: 「ホスト」 or 「compose の {service}」}」 with the buttons 「使わない」 (`.orca-lsp-skip`) and 「使う」 (`.orca-lsp-use`), for `missing` 「サーバーがありません」 with the button 「Claude に頼む」 (`.orca-lsp-ask`, below), and for `containerDown` 「コンテナが止まっています」 with the button 「コンテナを起動」. 「使う」 saves the server of the table for that language as one more server after the others (`lsp_settings_set`: `language`, the `name` found, 「自動で起動」 on, and no `command`, `mount` or `service`, so that it keeps following what the app decides), then reads its way and its state; the card becomes the card of a server. 「使わない」 only hides the card while this worktree is shown (it saves nothing: another worktree shows it again, and so does the reload). 「コンテナを起動」 of a language found saves the server as 「使う」 does, because the container is started for a server of the repository, and then starts the container and the server as the button of a server does (below). A failure of `lsp_detect` is its message in the line under the heading, and the cards of the servers stay.
- Claude Code sets the servers up (#976, the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #973). The heading has the ✦ of Claude (`#orca-lsp-ask`, `.icon-btn`, `CLAUDE_SVG`, the color `--claude`, `aria-label` 「Claude に設定を頼む」) before the reload, and the card of a language of `missing` has 「Claude に頼む」. Both open the pane of Claude Code of A-36 under the cards (`#orca-lsp-claude`: a head with 「Claude」 and the ✕ `#orca-lsp-claude-close` (`aria-label` 「閉じる」), and the terminal `#orca-lsp-claude-term`, 360 pixels high), `showClaudeCode` with the id `lsp-claude`, the owner `orca-lsp`, `builtinTools: true` (so that it can read the worktree) and the first instruction that Rust builds: the screen asks `lsp_claude_request(environment, project, worktree, language)` (`language` is none for the heading, the language of the card for 「Claude に頼む」), which checks the worktree against Orca's list as the commands of this part do (`check_worktree_of_project`), reads the ssh Host saved for the repository, and answers `lsp_claude_prompt(environment, project, worktree, ssh_host, language)`, a pure function (`lsp.rs`). The text asks Claude Code to look into the worktree (a worktree of a saved environment by `ssh` to the Host, and, when the Host is not set, to ask the owner), to ask the owner before it installs a server that is not there, to read the settings with `lsp_settings` and write them with `lsp_server_save` with the environment and project of the quote as they are (`local` for this machine), to leave `command` out for a server that the app finds by its table (#974) and write it only for another one, and to say what it set; `language` limits it to that language. The environment, the project, the worktree, the ssh Host and the language are lines of a quote (`> 環境: …`) that the text calls data and not an instruction, and are in no other place of it; a value that is empty, over 4096 characters, a control, hidden or a line separator, a worktree that is not absolute, an ssh Host that `ssh::check_host` refuses and a language that is not one of A-21 are refused in Japanese (the line under the heading, or the line of the card that was pressed), and no pane is opened. The Host is quoted only for a saved environment. Every write of the pane asks the owner in the terminal (A-36: `--permission-mode manual`). The pane is of one request (the worktree and the language): the same request pressed again changes nothing, and another one (another language) closes the pane (`closeClaudeCode`, which ends the process) and starts a new one; a press for another worktree than the pane's does so too, because the pane is asked for from the worktree that is shown. The ✕ closes it the same way. The part not shown (the files or the history chosen), the Orca screen left, the work pane not shown, or another worktree shown only hide it (`hideClaudeCode`): the process goes on, and the same pane is shown again when the part of that worktree is. It is closed only when Orca's list, read well, has not the worktree any more (at each drawing of the screen: `syncServers`, with `worktreeGone` of `ts/orca.ts`). The stop of the servers ("The stop" below) is not its rule: the file pane shows the worktree of the selected project with no tab of it open, and the pane stays then, with its process. The cards follow what Claude saves by the watch of the settings: a server saved for a language whose card was dotted makes it the card of a server at the next read (a language the settings have is not drawn as found), with its way read. The pane shows no help text. Unit tests (`cargo test`, `lsp.rs`, `lsp_claude_prompt`): the quote of this machine (the three lines and no host or language line, the values nowhere else, the owner asked before an install, the tools and the values of the quote named), of a saved environment with its Host (quoted once, the way to enter by it) and without one (no line, the owner asked), the Host ignored for this machine, a language limiting the text, a value that could leave its line (empty, a line break, U+2028 and U+2029, zero-width and bidi characters, a control, over the limit), a relative worktree, a refused Host and a language that is not one refused in Japanese, and text that reads like an instruction staying one line of the quote.
- Deleting. 「削除」 of the menu asks as A-18 does (`confirmDelete`, under the button of the menu, naming the title of the language), and, once it is answered, the settings are saved without that server and what ran for it (the servers this screen started or found running for that language, and the one of the worktree shown when its state is not stopped) is stopped with `lsp_stop`. A repository may be left with no server. A language that the worktree has is then a card of dotted line again, once it is found again (the list found is the one of the last `lsp_detect`).
- The save. A field that is left (the `change` of the field) is saved as it is, through `lsp_settings_set` with the whole settings of the repository, the one server changed (by its language) and the others as they are, and the way of that server is read again (`lsp_launches` with that language) so the fields show what Rust decided with it; a change of the ssh host reads the way of every server again and finds the languages again. The saves go one after another, each on what the one before it saved. A text field left as it was (the command or the mount equal to what `lsp_launches` gave) saves nothing; an empty one is saved as not set, which brings back what is decided and the mark. A save that Rust refuses shows its message in the line, and the fields show what is saved. The server that runs is not touched by a save: the restart takes the new settings.
- The watch of the settings. While the part is shown (and the page is visible), `lsp_settings` is read every 2 seconds (`periodMs("lspSettings", …)`, shortened in E2E by `timers({ lspSettings })`), so that a change made by `tsunagiai-mcp` (A-1) comes to the screen. A read that equals what the screen holds changes nothing; a read that differs is drawn (a server that is new or changed has its way read, one that is gone is stopped and dropped), unless a save of this screen is on its way or was made meanwhile (the read is then older). The text that is being typed in and the fields that are open (while their server is there) stay. The interval stops when the part is hidden, when the Orca screen is left or its work pane is not shown, when another worktree is shown and when the project is gone from the list (the pane has no worktree then), and a read that fails says nothing.
- 「自動に戻す」 saves the server's settings with the command, the mount and the service not set, and the rest (「自動で起動」, the name and the ssh host) as they are; it is disabled when none of the three is set.
- The restart and the stop. 「再起動」 of the menu calls `lsp_restart` for that card's server (not `lsp_stop` and `lsp_start` one after the other, which would lose the places outside the worktree that the server gave, so that a row or a tab of a gem read again after the restart still works), and keeps the state it answers (the later states come by the event). The rows of the menu and the buttons of the container wait (disabled) while a press of a restart or of a container is on its way. 「止める」 calls `lsp_stop` for that card's server, and the card is `stopped` with the reason `notStarted`; the screen then holds the server as stopped by the owner, so a file of its language that is opened later does not start it (see "The start"), until 「再起動」 or the button of the container is pressed (they take the hold away). The hold is in memory, for the worktree and the language, and goes with the deletion of the server and with the app. A failure of `lsp_stop` is the message in the line of the card.
- The container. While the state of a server is `stopped` with the reason `containerDown`, the button 「コンテナを起動」 (`.orca-lsp-container`) is shown in its card. It calls `lsp_container_start(environment, project, worktree, server)` and, when that has answered, `lsp_start`. A failure shows its message in the line of the card, starts no server and leaves the button. The container is never started by anything else.
- The start. A file of a worktree whose language a server takes (`lsp_server_for`, asked once when the file is read in the editor; a file outside the worktree is not, and none starts nothing) starts that server for its worktree, once for each worktree and server: `lsp_status` is asked, and `lsp_start` is called, both with the language of that server (its key), only when it says `stopped` with the reason `notStarted` and the settings of the repository (`lsp_settings`) have 「自動で起動」 on for that server. A server that runs (indexing or running) is left as it is, and one that ended (`failed`, `containerDown`) is not started again by the next file: the restart and the container button do it. A file whose language no server takes starts nothing. The worktree is then the screen's to stop.
- The stop. The screen calls `lsp_stop` (with the language of the server) for a worktree whose server it started or found running, once the list of Orca of its place was read well, when the list has not the worktree (its path in that place), or when no tab of the worktree is open: no Claude session or terminal of Orca, no file (one outside the worktree that a definition named included), no difference, no definition tab, in any project. The check is made at each drawing of the screen, so a tab closed and a list that changed are both seen. A list that failed or was not read yet stops nothing, and the app's own end stops every server (above). A place that the screen does not list now (a saved environment other than the one it shows, when it is not this machine) has no session or terminal tab open, so its servers need no list: they stop when no file and no difference of the worktree is open. This is so for every server that was started, whatever its languages. A server stopped so is started again by the next file of its languages that is opened.

Not here. No restart by itself of a server that died, and no watching of the disk by the clock (the files are looked at before a question only). The lines of a row are not colored. A definition is not asked from a file outside the worktree.

Unit tests (`cargo test`, `lsp.rs`, `lsp/protocol.rs`, `lsp/server.rs`, `lsp/outside.rs`, `lsp/container.rs`, `lsp/watch.rs`): those of #697 (above), those of the path that may be read (an absolute path of names; a relative one, one that starts with `-`, an empty one, the root, a control character, `.`, `..` and an empty name, and a path over the limit refused) and of the command (`docker compose exec -T {service} cat -- {path}` in compose, `cat -- {path}` on the host, the service and the path quoted, none with no way or no service, no command for a refused path), of what `cat` printed (the text, a failure with its last line, ssh's own failure, a file at the limit and one byte over it, not UTF-8), and of the whole read against a stand-in run (the command and the route of the way the server was started by, on this machine and through the ssh Host of a saved environment, the limit asked for, one run only, a refused path or no way running no `cat`, a failed run told), and, for the frame, the length in bytes, two frames in a row, pieces, a header that is not case sensitive, a body that is not JSON dropped with the next frame still read, and a length over the limit, a header with no length, with a sign, with two lengths, too long or too many, and a stream cut in a header or in a body being errors; the messages (the shapes, `initialize` with UTF-16 and progress, a response, a request, a notification and what is none of them, an error message cleaned and cut, the answers to the server's requests); the progress (a server with none until the grace is over, one token or two or a number, the grace not ending a progress that began, what cannot be read, the token limit); the paths (a URI made and read back with characters that need escaping, a URI that is no path, the dots and double slashes, a relative path below the mount and the refused ones); the answer to a definition (a `Location`, a list of both kinds, a path outside the mount, `..` out of the mount, the mount itself, a root mount, nothing and unreadable entries, a place twice, the limit, the unit of the position); the end of a process (a container that is down, a failure with its last line, ssh's own failure, the end of the standard error kept, a container's words said before the last line not taking a failure for a container that is down); and, against a stand-in server over a pipe and a stand-in `/bin/sh` process, the handshake and the states, the answers to the server's requests, a server with no progress, the text sent as `didOpen` and the file closed once it is answered (and not while another question about it waits), a notice of changed files sent as it is before the question that follows, an error of the server, a question before `initialized`, `shutdown` and `exit` in order and a server that does not answer them, a server that ends, a frame that cannot be trusted, a refused `initialize`, a dropped channel, the registry (the question of whether a definition exists asked of a server that is indexing, stopped and not there refused with its reason, answered true and false for a running one with `null` and an empty list as none, a path outside the worktree and a text too large refused, and no outside path kept by it; one start for one worktree, two places apart, a died server and its start again, a stop, a definition's paths and the remembered outside paths with the way they are read by, those paths kept by the server that replaces a stopped one and by the restart (the old server stopped, the new one started in its place, a restart with no server starting one), the listing of the changed files run once for each question with the first look and the next one told, a look that met a folder it cannot read (the code 1, no `--end`) still telling what it listed and keeping no list of all the files, one that ended well with a failing code keeping the list, a script that did not run or a run that fails telling nothing and keeping the time and the files known, and the first look being the start for a file that is gone by the first question, a refused path and a too large text, `close_all`, a task that ignores the stop) and the process (a container that does not run, a failure, a program that is not there, the standard input closed). Those of the container (#701): the command (`docker compose up -d {service}`, the service quoted, none for the host, for no way and for no service), the end of it (well, a failure with the last line of its message, ssh's own failure), and the whole start against a stand-in run (the way read first and then the start in the worktree by the route of this machine and through the ssh Host of a saved environment, the service of the settings the one started, no start with no compose service, a failed start and a run that cannot be made told). Those of the files that changed on the disk (`lsp/watch.rs`): the minutes since the last look, the command (the two listings, the folders left out, the end line, one script), what it printed (the recent files and all of them, no end, a cut last line, a line that is no path, and whether the script ran: the line between the listings), and what is told (the first look, a known and a new recent file, a new file with an old time, a gone file, no full list, nothing changed, each file once and at most the limit, the notice's URI and number, a path that makes no URI). Those of the Gemfile.lock read only when no service mounts the worktree and the server takes `ruby`. Those of #959 (`ssh.rs`): the lines of a config text (the keywords without case, `=`, a tab, a comment, a quoted name, several names, `HostName` and `Match` not being a `Host`), the names that ssh takes (a pattern, a negation and a name that `check_host` refuses left out, a name twice once, the order), the files of an `Include` (the order of the glob, a hidden file, a path from `~`, a file that is not there, one that includes itself) and the depth, and no file. Those of #958 and #974: the file of version 3 (the shape, a repository with no server, version 1 read as one `ruby-lsp` and version 2 read as one server of each language, both written as version 3 at the next save, a broken file or another version refused and not replaced), the checks of a server (the name: control character, 64 characters counted as characters, trimmed, an empty one the name of the table or the language, one name for two servers being no matter; the language: not a language of A-21, the same one in two servers, the same one in two repositories being no matter), `server_for` (the language, or none), the table (every language one of A-21, no quote in what goes into the script, ruby with nothing to ask), the script (the marks, the lock, each command once, no mark in the script of a start), what it printed (the lines it knows and the others left out), the languages found (by the mark, the first command of the table, `missing`, ruby only with a lock that lists `ruby-lsp`, a service of compose with its mount and `containerDown`, the shape of `LspFound`), the reading of the worktree for `lsp_detect` (the host once and a service once more, the host alone without a service, a container that is down and another failure of it, the host of a saved environment, a bad worktree refused before anything is run), the command of the table for a server with none (the first that the host has, one of `node_modules` before one of the PATH, none, in the service of compose, a container that is down, any other failure, a place asked once for all the servers, no question for ruby, for a command of the settings and for a language that the table lacks), the settings applied (no command from the table, the command of the table run in the service or on the host, the service and the mount as for ruby-lsp, the command of the settings used), a server that the settings do not have refused before anything is run, the container of a server that is not ruby's, `orca_language::is_language` and `extensions_of` (the tables read back, a name that a shell reads left out), the command of the files that changed for the extensions of a server, the `languageId` of `didOpen`, the key of two servers of a worktree kept apart, the messages that name the server (by its language), and the event with `server`. Those of #975: the title of a language (the six of the table, and any other language by its name), the look of a language (its title and its icon, none for a language with none), `orca_icon::language_icon` (the icon of a language, none for a language without one), every language of the table having an icon, and `lsp_language_looks` answering every language of A-21 in its order. No test runs `docker`, `ssh` or ruby-lsp, or touches the real config folder.

Unit tests of the screen's Rust (`cargo test`, `lsp/view.rs`): the name from any of its characters, between names and past the end, a constant path, `@`, `@@`, `$`, `?` and `!`, the `!` of `!=`, a sign alone, the UTF-16 units of a position, and letters that are not ASCII; and the place of a definition (a file of the worktree and the top of it, a gem with its version and folder, the top of a gem, a name with a `-` or a digit, a platform and a prerelease, a gem from git, a path that names no gem, one order for each location). The E2E spec `orca-definition.e2e.ts` mocks the commands and covers the arguments of the press (the place of the mouse, not the caret; the editor's typed text; the line of the name; the server that `lsp_server_for` answered for the language of the file), the default menu left out, the definition tab (split off below the pane of the editor with the title 「定義 ・ {name}」, the heading and the count, the next press put in the same tab wherever it is and no second tab, the tab closed with its button and the pane that closes with it, the file pane left as it was), the rows with their paths, places and marks, every row open and its lines read, the lines around the definition and their reading, a row that fails, the opening of a place (a file of the worktree, one open already, one outside with the lock, `gem` and no save, in the pane of the editor and not in the pane of the tab), the words of a refusal, the spinner, no place, no Command key, no character, no name, a language that no server takes, and a language other than Ruby that one does; and the underline (`lsp_definition_exists` asked with the arguments of the press after the mouse stayed on a name with the Command key, and asked of nothing without the key; the underline of the name's place and width at the foot of its letters, with the pointer, and no tab or `lsp_definition`; nothing drawn for no definition or for an error that is not kept; the key going down over a name the mouse is on, and going up; one ask while the mouse keeps moving; the same name not asked again until the text changes, after leaving it and coming back and after the key up and down, and another name and the changed text asked; the mouse leaving and the scroll taking it away); and, for the language server, what is read and when (nothing while the part is not shown; the state and the way of each server), the card of each language (its icon and title, and the line of the name of the server and where it runs, no field open, none of the name, the chip of the language and the plus), a language with no icon drawn with the line drawing, the menu with its four rows and the fields opened only by 「手で直す」 (and closed by the same row, one card at a time), the state of each server and its change by the event (and not by the event of another worktree), the message of a failed reading, the service choice only with several candidates, the mark 「自動」 and its absence for a saved value, the arguments of each save (the command, the mount, the service, 「自動で起動」, the ssh host with a saved environment), a save that changes only its own server, no save for a field left as it was, an emptied field saved as not set, a refused save, 「自動に戻す」 and when it is disabled, the stop (`lsp_stop`, the state, no start by a file opened later, the restart that gives it back, a failure), the languages found (`lsp_detect` read with the part and by the reload, again after the ssh host; the dotted cards for `found`, `missing` and `containerDown`, none for a language that has a server; 「使う」 saving the server of the table with nothing replaced and reading its way and state, its refusal; 「使わない」 hiding the card without a save, and the reload showing it again; a failure of the reading in the line under the heading; 「コンテナを起動」 of a language found saving the server and then starting the container and the server; 「Claude に頼む」 of `missing` and the ✦ of the heading asking `lsp_claude_request` and starting the pane with the text, the refusal in the card's line and under the heading, the ✕ closing the process, the same request left alone and another one closing the old pane first, the pane hidden with the part and shown again with no new start, and a server saved elsewhere replacing the dotted card at the next read), the delete after its question with the stop of what ran, the restart of the card that was pressed (one call of `lsp_restart`, the rows of the menu waiting, no call of `lsp_stop` or `lsp_start`), the container button and its failure, the start when a file is opened (of the server that takes its language, not with 「自動で起動」 off, not for a file that no server takes, not for a server that runs or ended, once for a worktree and a server), the text of the field that is being typed in left as it is when the save of another field ends, the read of the settings at an interval (a change drawn, the typed text and the open fields kept, no read while the part is hidden or once the Orca screen is left, the files that are open looking for their server again after a save), no question of the underline to a server that is indexing, the places of one file read once and the mark 「gem」 for a Ruby file only, and the stop when the last tab is closed (a definition tab too), not while another tab is open, and when the worktree is gone from the list; the language server is shown by the button 「言語サーバー」 and read only then; `orca-files.e2e.ts` covers the three buttons.

## A-37 Orchestration on the development screen

The side menu's Orca button is the development screen. It shows Orca (A-6) and the orchestration, where one Claude Code, the orchestrator, splits the owner's work into children that run in Orca (the owner's decision of 2026-11-16, issues #791, #795 and later ones). This requirement holds the screen, how the orchestrator starts, the tools that start and end the children, and the state of a child. The flow pane is #796, the children's terminals are #797, and #803 puts the two in one right pane. The terminals of the children moved to the tabs of the middle pane and the right pane became a column of cards (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #909 and #914). The formations and the projects are in one list at the left of the screen, with no switch of a mode (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #909 and #913).

The button. The sidebar button `nav-orca` has the `aria-label` 「開発」 (the count and the working yellow of A-6 are as they were, so the label is 「開発（確認待ち {n} 件、作業中 {m} 件）」 while a session waits or works) and the icon `CODE_SVG` of `ts/icons.ts`. The names inside do not change: `Screen` is still `"orca"`, `MenuScreen::Orca` and `side-menu.json` keep their names, so a saved order stays as it is.

The list of both. The development screen has one list at the left, the list pane `#orca-list` of A-6, which holds the formations under the heading 「フォーメーション」 and, under the heading 「プロジェクト」, the projects with the sessions of A-8 (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #913). There is no switch of a mode: the footer (A-35) has the bell alone, and no `#footer-modes`, no `#footer-mode-orca` and no `#footer-mode-formation`, and the counts that those two buttons showed (#840 and #841) are not shown anywhere any more. What the list has selected decides what is at its right: a row of a formation shows the panes of A-37 (the leader and the flow, below) in place of the work pane of Orca, and a project or a session shows the work pane (`#orca-work` with the file pane, A-6). The development screen is shown with the work pane at each start of the app, as the Orca mode was. The selection is one: a formation is selected by pressing its row (it is deselected when a project or a session is selected, and the row of the project is marked only while no formation is), and the selection is in memory only. "Orca の画面で開く" (A-9) selects the project and the session, so it shows the work pane, also when the list does not hold the session yet (no formation stays selected). A session that was started from the plus of the work pane and waits for its tab (A-16) is not selected once a formation is selected meanwhile, so the screen is not taken back to the work pane. The list can be closed (A-23) while a formation is shown: the button that opens it again is then at the left end of the head of the leader's pane (`#formation-list-toggle`, before the tabs), because the tab strip of the work pane, which has it otherwise, is hidden. While a formation is selected the keys and the clock of A-6 act as they do while the work pane is hidden: the list is read as it is while the screen is shown (the projects, the sessions and the counts of the sidebar's button go on), and no Orca terminal, editor or file pane is shown or read; ⌘J still takes the search field.

The panes of a formation (`#formation`, `ts/formation.ts`). Two panes at the right of the list, in place of the work pane while a formation is selected: the middle pane (`#formation-orchestrator`, the `aria-label` 「端末」) and the right pane (`#formation-flow`, 「流れ」, below). The middle pane has a header band `#formation-head`, which holds the tabs (`#formation-tabs`, `role="tablist"`: the leader's tab first, then a tab for each child, below) and, at its right end, the restart button (`#formation-restart`). Under the band are the words of a failure of the leader's terminal (`#formation-term-error`) and the terminal of the selected tab: the leader's in `#formation-term` while the leader's tab is selected, and a child's in `#formation-teammate-term` while a child's tab is selected (the one that is not shown has `hidden`, and its terminal is hidden, so the two never share the height). The band has no title and no name of the formation: the leader's card of the right pane has the name. The two are the children of `#formation`, which is a part of `#orca` that takes no box of its own (`display: contents`) and is hidden while no formation is selected; the work pane and the file pane are the children of `#orca-session`, which is hidden while one is (#822, #830, #913; the layout is the owner's decision of 2026-11-17, #817, and of 2026-11-18, #914). With no formation selected (at each start of the app, with an empty list, after a project or a session is selected, and after the selected formation is deleted) the leader and the flow are not started or read.
The width of the list is the Orca screen's (A-6: 19% of the screen at first, 15% to 50% of it, the divider `#orca-split`), the same for a formation as for a project. The right pane is 260px wide (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #914) and the middle pane takes the rest. There is no divider between them: the width is fixed, and the divider `#formation-flow-split` of #817, which put the right pane at 37% of the screen, is gone.
The tabs, the cards and the terminals below are those of the formation that the list has selected (the paragraph on the list).
The right pane is shown whenever a formation is selected, with the leader's card (the name of the formation and the tag 「リーダー」) before any child is read or recorded (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #914); with no child it holds that card alone, and the leader's tab is in the middle pane from the start. The middle pane takes the width between the list and the right pane.
A read that fails keeps the last read's children, so it changes neither pane.
The screen shows no help text (`frontend.md`).

The orchestrator's pane. The leader's terminal of a formation of this Mac (a formation belongs to one PC, "Where the orchestrator runs" below) is the Claude Code pane of A-36 with `kind: "formation"`: `showClaudeCode(host, {id: "formation:{name}", kind: "formation"}, "formation-screen")`: the pane id is `formation:{name}` (`formationPaneId`), the name of the formation whose orchestrator it is, the one that the list has selected (none at each start of the app, and then the pane is not shown and starts nothing; #820, #822), and Rust takes a pane of such an id for that formation's orchestrator (a name that is no formation's name, by `check_name`, is refused). Claude Code's own tools are on. The screen gives no project: `claude_code_start` takes none, and Rust reads the projects saved with the formation (`formation.json`, below) and turns them into the first instruction (below), at each start (a restart too). Showing the panes with a formation selected starts the process of the shown formation with the size of the host, once (with none selected, the pane is hidden with `hidden` and no process starts); selecting another formation in the list hides the terminal of the last one (its process goes on and ends nothing) and shows the one of the selected formation, starting its process at its first show. Selecting a project or a session, or leaving the screen, hides the pane (`hideClaudeCode`) and ends nothing; showing it again draws the same process. A process that ended by itself is begun anew when the pane is shown again after a hide (A-36), and the screen has no `onEnd`. The restart button (`#formation-restart`, #802) is at the right end of the header `#formation-head`: an icon button with `RELOAD_SVG` and the `aria-label` 「リーダーを再起動」, no text and no dialog. Pressing it calls `closeClaudeCode("formation:{name}", "formation-screen")` for the shown formation and then `showClaudeCode` as the first show does, so the process that runs is ended (`claude_code_close` with the id and the number of its start) and a new one is started (`claude_code_start` with the id `formation:{name}`) with the size of the host; it works after the process ended by itself (`Claude Code が終わりました` is drawn) too, because the screen of the ended process is cleared as a new pane's is. The new terminal takes the keyboard, as a first show gives it, and the new process begins a new conversation (it reads its state again from its formation's folder, `formations/{name}/`: `CLAUDE.md` and `children.json`). The button is off from the press until the new start has answered, so a press in between starts nothing. Only the orchestrator of the shown formation is started again: the other formations' orchestrators, the children, their record (`children.json`) and Orca's commands are not touched. The tab of a child hides the leader's terminal the same way (`hideClaudeCode`; the process goes on), and the leader's tab shows it again. The restart button is off while a child's tab is the selected one, because it restarts the leader, whose terminal is not in view then. Nothing else closes the pane (`closeClaudeCode`) while the app runs.

Where the orchestrator runs (the owner's decision of 2026-11-17, issues #857, #858 and #859). A message by SendMessage reaches only a Claude Code session on the same PC, so a formation belongs to one PC and its orchestrator runs on that PC, with its children. The PC of a formation is the environment of its projects (`formation::formation_environment`: `local` is this Mac, any other name is a saved environment, and a formation with no project is this Mac's); `formation_create` refuses projects of two environments, with the two environments in the message, and nothing is made (`check_one_environment`; a formation made before this keeps what it has, and its PC is the environment of its first project). `formation_list` answers each formation's `environment`.
A formation of this Mac is as the paragraphs here say: its orchestrator runs in the pseudo terminal of A-36, and nothing of it changes. The orchestrator of a formation of a saved environment runs in a terminal of the Orca of that environment, and the app only makes the terminal and shows it (`formation_orchestrator(name, restart)`, `formation::start_orchestrator_with`): it runs `orca terminal create --worktree global-floating-terminal --title {name} --command {command} --json --environment={environment}` (#879): Orca's floating terminal, which belongs to no repository and opens in the home folder of that PC, so the orchestrator's terminal is not in a project's repository, and `--worktree` is what Orca asks of a terminal it creates on a saved environment. It reads no `orca repo list` and no repository of the formation. The command (`orchestrator_command`) is one `exec sh -c` sentence typed into the terminal's login shell, whichever shell it is, which its `exec` replaces, so the terminal ends with the process that runs in it (#943); it runs the `tsunagiai-mcp` that the app put in `remote/` of that PC's config folder (`formation_remote::program_path`, "The server on the PC" below; no other place is searched: not the installed app, not `~/.cargo/bin`, not the PATH), first with `--serve --bind {host}`, which starts the server of that PC unless one of this version runs there (its output and a failure of it are not shown and stop nothing), and then, in place of the `sh`, with `--formation-start {name}` and one `--project {repo}` for each repository of the formation's projects (#858: it writes `CLAUDE.md`, `mcp.json`, the launch script and the settings in `formations/{name}/` of the config folder of that PC, and starts Claude Code there, so the children are launched and recorded on that PC); `{host}` is the host of that PC's Orca endpoint, which must be an IP address (an environment reached by a name is refused with its reason, because the server binds to the address it is reached at). Where the program is not there it prints `tsunagiai-mcp が置かれていません` and opens a shell in its place (`exec "${SHELL:-/bin/sh}"`; an `exit` would close the terminal with the words unread), so the terminal stays (the consent of the owner places it, "The consent" below). The name has passed `check_name`, so the sentence holds no character a shell reads. The repositories follow the `sh -c` script as its arguments (`"$@"`), each in single quotes of its own (a quote in a name is closed, escaped and opened again), and each has passed what `formation::first_prompt` checks (1 to 200 characters, none a control or a hidden one, 50 projects at most), and holds no `\` (a login shell such as fish reads a `\` inside single quotes as an escape, so a name with one is refused), so a name that `tsunagiai-mcp` would refuse is refused by the app first. `tsunagiai-mcp --formation-start` makes the projects the first instruction of Claude Code (`claude [prompt]`, as the pane of this Mac does), in the words of `formation::first_prompt` with each project's environment written `local`, because on that PC every project is of `local`; with no `--project` it passes none (#882). The app does not wait for Claude Code (`orca terminal wait`) and sends it nothing (`orca terminal send`): Claude Code first asks whether to trust the folder (its default is No, exit; the folder `formations/{name}/` is new), a question that `orca terminal wait --for tui-idle` never sees answered (`blockedReason: agent-trust-workspace`) and that the owner answers in the terminal the screen shows, as in the pane of this Mac (A-36: nothing here answers it). Once the terminal is made, the handle is written in `formations/{name}/formation.json` of this Mac (`orchestratorTerminal`; a file written before it has none, and a formation of this Mac never has it); a terminal whose handle cannot be written is closed, so none is left running with no record. A file of v0.82 also has `pcRepoPath`, the folder of the first project's repository: it is read as before and not used, and the next write of the file leaves it out. The command asked again finds the recorded handle in `orca terminal list --json --environment={environment}` and answers it with nothing made; the list is asked with no `--worktree`, because Orca refuses `--worktree global-floating-terminal` as a selector (`selector_not_found`, measured on Orca of a saved environment) and lists the floating terminals when none is given. A handle that is no longer listed (or is orphaned) is replaced by a new terminal, and stays in the file until the new one replaces it, so that the children it launched stay in the screen's reads when the start fails. With `restart` the recorded terminal is closed first and a new one is made, which begins a new conversation; a terminal that cannot be closed and is still listed stops the restart with that reason (`今のリーダーのターミナルを閉じられません`), because a new terminal beside it would run a second orchestrator. A formation of this Mac is refused (`この Mac のものです`), and so is one that is not there. One start of a formation runs at a time (the screen asks again while one is on its way), and another formation starts meanwhile. A failure (Orca does not answer, the terminal cannot be made, the handle cannot be recorded, a project name is refused) is one line, `リーダーを起動できません（環境「{environment}」）: {the first line of what Orca printed}`. A PC without the placed program is not a failure of the start: the terminal shows `tsunagiai-mcp が置かれていません` and stays, which the owner reads there (#882, #891).
The screen shows that terminal as it shows a child's: in `#formation-term` of the middle pane, streamed when the Orca screen streams that environment (A-12) and mirrored otherwise (A-8), with `showTerminals` under the owner `formation-screen`. The pane asks for it when the formation is shown and the screen holds none (the handle is kept in memory and Rust finds the recorded one again), asks once for a formation at a time, and starts no pseudo terminal. A failure is drawn in `#formation-term-error` (`role="alert"`, above the terminal, in the style of the failures of the other panes) and is tried again when the formation is pressed in the list again or the restart button is pressed. The restart button of such a formation calls `formation_orchestrator(name, true)`, drops the terminal it showed and shows the new one; it is off until that has answered. When Claude Code ends, the terminal ends with it and leaves that Orca's list (#943): the stream says it ended, or a read of the mirror says the terminal is not listed and the mirror reads no more (`TermSource.onGone`). The screen keeps the last screen it drew, and the next time the formation is shown (pressed in the list again, or the leader's tab selected again) it drops the handle and calls `formation_orchestrator(name, false)` again, which finds no listed terminal and makes a new one, so Claude Code starts anew, as the pane of this Mac does. A terminal that is still listed is shown again and nothing is asked. Deleting the formation (`formation_delete`) closes the recorded terminal in that Orca right after the orchestrator's process of this Mac is ended and before the children are closed (a close that fails, because the owner closed the tab or the environment is gone, stops nothing), and the screen drops its terminal. The files of the orchestrator on that PC (`CLAUDE.md`, `mcp.json`, the launch script and the settings) are not read or removed by the app; the record of its children is read and changed through the server of that PC (the next paragraphs).
The record of a formation of a saved environment (the owner's decision of 2026-11-17, issue #860; the way it is read was replaced by the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, issues #888 to #894). The children of such a formation are launched and recorded by `tsunagiai-mcp` of that PC, in `formations/{name}/children.json` of that PC's config folder, each in that PC's own Orca (`local` there); this Mac writes no `children.json` for it, and reads and changes that file through the server that `tsunagiai-mcp` runs on that PC (the paragraphs below; `formation::pull_pc_children`, `drop_from_pc_record`). Orca's runtime RPC `files.*` is not used for it: it reads only the folders registered in Orca, and the config folder is refused with `Access denied: path resolves outside allowed directories` (measured on 2026-11-17), so no right to read it is asked and no file is written through Orca. A formation made before its orchestrator ran on its PC may hold children in this Mac's own `children.json` (its old orchestrator ran here): they are the formation's too, and every read, close and deletion below takes them with the PC's (the PC's first, then those of this Mac's record that the PC lacks, by name), each child closed from the record it is in (`formation::with_own`, `through_record`).
The server on the PC (`src-tauri/src/formation_server.rs`, `formation_remote.rs`; issues #890 and #891). The app puts `tsunagiai-mcp` of its own version on the PC and runs it there as a server, with a WebSocket of its own. It does so through a terminal of that PC's Orca, the floating terminal (`global-floating-terminal`, titled `tsunagiai-server`): the app types one `sh -c` sentence, reads the screen of the terminal for the line the program prints, and closes the terminal. The placement (`formation_remote::place`) runs only on macOS with Apple Silicon (`uname -sm`). It fetches `tsunagiai.app.tar.gz` of the GitHub Release of this app's version into a temporary folder (the repository is the one of the updater's endpoint in `tauri.conf.json`), takes `tsunagiai-mcp` alone out of the archive, checks with `codesign --verify --strict` that it is signed and, with a requirement on the Team ID, that it is signed by the Team ID of this app's own signature (an app with no Team ID, and a development build, whose version is `0.0.0` and has no release, are refused with their reason), and only then puts it in `remote/` of that PC's config folder (`$HOME/Library/Application Support/jp.mysis.tsunagiai/remote/`) by a rename, writes the file `version` beside it and runs it with `--serve --bind {host} --client-key {key}`. A failure of a step prints a line that begins `TSUNAGIAI-SERVE-ERROR` with its reason, which is the message the screen shows.
`tsunagiai-mcp --serve --bind {host} [--client-key {key}]` makes its key pair in `remote/` (`server.key`, secret, mode 0600, in a folder of mode 0700, and `server.pub`), pins the app's public key (`client.pub`, from `--client-key`, or the one pinned before; with neither it is an error), starts the daemon (`--serve-run`) unless one of this version, at this address and with this key already runs (it ends that one and starts again when any of the three differs), and returns at once with the line `TSUNAGIAI-SERVE {the server's public key} {version}`, which the app reads from the screen of the terminal. The daemon listens on `{host}:47823` only (`formation_server::PORT`; `{host}` is the address that the PC is reached at, never all addresses), writes its process, version and address in `pid`, and serves until a client sends `stop`. `--serve-stop` ends the server that runs and prints `TSUNAGIAI-SERVE-STOPPED`. The server uses no launchd and does not start at login: the orchestrator's launch command starts it (above), and the app starts it once when nobody answers (below). The version it answers is the content of `version`, which the placement wrote (`0.0.0` when it is not there). No other file of the PC is read or written by the server than the `formations/{name}/children.json` that its requests name.
The connection (`formation_server::Client`). Each side holds the other's static public key. The app's own key pair is in the keychain (service `jp.mysis.tsunagiai.formation`, user `client-key`; it is made at the first use), and its public key goes to the PC in the placement. The server's public key is read from its ready line and pinned in `formation-remote.json` of the app's config folder, for each environment by its name, with the version (`{"environments": {"{name}": {"key", "version"}}}`, written through `write_atomic`). A server answers only one who holds the secret key of the pinned public key, and the app believes only a server that holds the pinned one. After the WebSocket opens, each side sends one message, its version and an ephemeral X25519 public key; the two keys of the two directions are derived with HKDF-SHA256 from the Diffie-Hellman result of the ephemeral keys and that of the static keys together with a hash of the transcript, so that one who does not hold a static secret key derives other keys and its first frame does not open. A key of low order is refused. Every message after that is one binary frame sealed with XSalsa20-Poly1305 under the key of its direction, with a nonce made of the direction and a counter, so that a frame that is replayed, reordered, changed or sent back does not open (a frame that does not open ends the connection with no answer). A frame carries 1 MiB at most before it is sealed, the handshake has 10 seconds, a connection silent for 60 seconds is closed, and the server serves 16 connections at a time. A secret key is in no message, log or error text. The requests are JSON, `{method, params}`, with the answer `{ok, result}` or `{ok: false, error}`: `version` (the server's version), `children.read` (`formations`: up to 200 names, each of the shape `formation::check_name` accepts; for each name the children of its `children.json`, none when the file is not there, or the reason it could not be read), `children.drop` (`formation` and `names`: the record is read again and only those names are taken out, so that a child that the orchestrator added meanwhile stays; nothing is written when the record is not there or holds none of them; the number taken out) and `stop` (the server ends after it answers).
The way to the server (`formation_remote::connect`). The app connects to `{host}:47823` with the pinned key and asks the version. When nobody answers it starts the server on that PC once, with `--serve --bind {host}` and no key (which keeps the pinned one, and prints the line that is pinned again), and tries again. When the server's version is not the app's it places the program again once (the placement starts the server too) and tries again. When it still fails the read fails with the reason, and the words that a question of the firewall may be waiting on that PC's screen, where the owner presses 「許可」 and tries again: the PC's firewall may refuse a program it has not allowed (measured on 2026-11-18: a new server was not reached from outside, an allowed program was), so the owner presses 「許可」 once, on that PC's screen, when the server first starts (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18), and the app changes no setting of the firewall. A start or a placement is not made again on the same PC within 30 seconds, whatever came of it (a screen that reads every few seconds would otherwise open a terminal at every read), and one is made at a time for each PC. The screen's reads (the children of A-37's flow pane and the list of formations) do not wait for a start or a placement: they give up after 5 seconds (`SAVED_PS_TIMEOUT`), and when a start or a placement is needed it runs in the background (none is begun while one runs on that PC) and the read answers at once that the PC's server is being started (「その PC のサーバーを起動しています」) or placed again (「その PC のサーバーを入れ直しています」). The connection and the handshake of such a read wait 3 seconds at most (`SCREEN_HANDSHAKE`), shorter than those 5 seconds, so that a PC that takes the connection and says nothing (a firewall that waits for an answer) is heard as silent, and reaches the start and the words of the firewall; those of a close and a delete wait 10 seconds. A close and a delete wait 30 seconds at most, the connection included (`PC_RECORD_WAIT`), and fail with that reason while a start or a placement is running. An environment with no pinned key is refused with `環境「{environment}」の PC にサーバーを置くことを、まだ許可していません。…` and nothing is done on its PC.
The consent (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, issue #893; `ts/formation-consent.ts`). The program is put on a PC only after the owner agrees to it, once for each saved environment, and the consent is the pinned key of that environment in `formation-remote.json` (`formation_consents` lists the PCs that have it, with their versions). When the owner makes a formation (`ts/formation-new.ts`) whose projects are all in one saved environment that has no consent, a dialog replaces the one that makes it and asks 「「{environment}」に tsunagiai-mcp を置きますか？」 with four lines: that `tsunagiai-mcp` of this app's version is put in that PC's config folder from the GitHub Release, that it listens on the port 47823 there, that the owner first presses 「許可」 on the question of the firewall on that PC's screen, and that the consent is taken back in the Orca tab of the settings. 「同意する」 calls `formation_consent(environment)`, which places the program, starts it and pins its key (it blocks while the program is fetched there, so it runs in a thread of its own; the buttons are off meanwhile); a failure shows its reason on a card (A-28), keeps the dialog and agrees to nothing. 「やめる」, Escape and a press elsewhere make no formation. This Mac needs none, and a mix of environments is refused by `formation_create` with its own reason before any dialog is shown. Rust checks the consent too: `formation_create` refuses a project of a saved environment with no consent (`環境「{environment}」には、tsunagiai-mcp を置くことへの同意がありません。…`), before anything is made. A formation made before the consent existed asks for it when its orchestrator is about to start (`ensureConsent` before `formation_orchestrator`, also at a restart): when the owner declines nothing is started and nothing is shown, and pressing the formation again asks again.
Taking the consent back (issue #893; `ts/formation-consent-settings.ts`). The Orca tab of the settings (A-3) has a card 「サーバーを置いた PC」 (`#formation-consent-items`, with a `?` hint) that lists the PCs that have the consent, read each time the tab is shown: a row for each, with the environment's name, `tsunagiai-mcp {version}`, the status 「置き済み」 and the button 「取り消す」; with none it says 「サーバーを置いた PC はありません」. 「取り消す」 calls `formation_consent_revoke(environment)`, which answers the consents that are left. It is refused, with the formations named, while a formation of that PC is left (`環境「{environment}」には、フォーメーション（…）が残っています。削除してから取り消してください`), because its orchestrator and children run there and its record is read from that server. Otherwise it runs one command in a terminal of that PC's Orca that ends the server (`--serve-stop`) and deletes `remote/` of that PC's config folder (the program, `version`, the keys and `pid`), and then forgets the pin. A stop that fails keeps the pin, so the revocation can be tried again, and says why (`サーバーを止められませんでした: …`); an environment that is gone from `orca environment list` cannot be reached, so only the pin is forgotten. The button is off while it runs, and a failure is shown on a card (A-28). Nothing else of the PC is deleted: the folders of the formations and their records stay there.
The reads of the screen. Every child of the record is named by the formation's environment from here (`child_from_pc`: on its PC a child is in `local`, and from here that PC is the saved environment), so its terminal, its `worktree ps` and every command of Orca go to that Orca, as for any child of a saved environment. A formation whose `formation.json` has no `orchestratorTerminal` never had its orchestrator started on its PC, so that PC has no record of it and is not asked (a file of v0.82, which also has `pcRepoPath`, is asked the same way: that folder is not used). The flow pane (`formation_children`) draws these children, and `formation_list` counts them (its `readError` has the reason when the record of a PC cannot be read, and that formation counts the children of this Mac's record alone): the list reads the records of the formations of one saved environment with one `children.read` over one connection, the environments side by side, and `formation_children` asks the same call for the formations of its environment, so the two made at the same moment run once (`orca::Flights`); `orca environment list` is read at most once for a list, and only when a record could not be read, and a failure is the message of `formation_children`: `環境「{environment}」の子の記録を読めません: {the first line}`. A PC that was taken off `orca environment list` cannot be asked: its record reads as none, so that its formation is still listed and can be deleted.
`formation_close_child` and `formation_delete` close the children of that record in that PC's Orca as they close any child (the same checks, the same confirmation, the same `force`), working on a copy of the record in a scratch config folder that is deleted afterwards (`through_record`). Then the names of the children that were closed are taken out of the record they were in: of this Mac's `children.json` for a child it holds, and of the PC's `children.json` for the others: the server of that PC takes only those names out of what it holds now (`children.drop`: it reads the file again, so a child that the orchestrator added meanwhile stays) and writes it. Nothing is written when the file is not there or holds none of the names. It is written also when the work failed half-way, because the children it closed are closed, and a record that cannot be written is an error also when every child was closed (`子は閉じましたが、その PC の children.json から外せませんでした`, or `この Mac の children.json` for this Mac's). The deletion of the formation deletes this Mac's folder `formations/{name}/` once both records are written and read again with no child in either: a child that the orchestrator of the PC added while the children were closed stops the deletion, and this Mac's folder stays (`子を閉じている間に、リーダーが子を足しました。…もう一度削除してください`).
A child is launched on the PC of its orchestrator only: `formation_plan` and `formation_launch` of `tsunagiai-mcp` refuse any environment but `local`, the PC it runs on, before anything is read, made or written, and say why (`SendMessage は同じ PC の中でしか届かない`; `formation::check_children_here`). The tools that only read take any environment, and `formation_close` closes a child that is in the record whatever its environment is.

How the orchestrator starts (`src-tauri/src/claude_code.rs`, `formation_claude.md`). Claude Code runs in `formations/{name}/` of the app config directory, the folder of its formation (#819; the folder of a formation named `formation` is `formations/formation/`), which holds four files that the app writes at each start: `.claude/settings.json` (the project settings, which the script below reads: they are written as `{}` each time, so a permission or a hook that the orchestrator added to them in auto mode does not stay for the next start), `CLAUDE.md` (the orchestrator's instructions, written from `formation_claude.md` with the formation's name where the text says `{name}`: it is named after its formation, and its job is to reach the purpose of the owner's instruction and finish the work (#851): on an instruction it investigates first, with `formation_environments`, `formation_worktrees`, `formation_files`, `formation_read` and `formation_git`, sets the purpose, the goal and the plan itself (how many children, their roles, places, order and how they work together), tells the owner in short and goes on without waiting for approval; it runs on the same PC as its children and places a child only in `local`, the PC it runs on, because SendMessage reaches only that PC (it can read the other environments, not launch into them, #860); it starts the children with `formation_launch`, choosing for each a new worktree, a worktree of an existing branch, or an existing folder (the repository's own folder or a listed worktree, for a child that only reads, or for a branch that the own folder has open), and the name of a new branch from the repository's own conventions; it tells the ones to come with `formation_plan`, instructs them by SendMessage with a request to tell the orchestrator by its own name (the formation's) when the work ends or a decision is needed, answers the children's questions itself after it has investigated, and asks the owner, in text with what it found, the options and its recommendation and not with AskUserQuestion, only for a product or specification decision that the material does not settle, an operation that cannot be undone (a force push, a history rewrite, a write to production, a publication), or what investigation does not settle and would change the purpose; it chooses another place or branch itself when a launch is refused; it reads the projects that the first instruction quotes as data and starts nothing before the owner's word, and puts text from outside (a mail, a Slack message) in a quote as material to read; it checks that the goal is reached itself before it reports the result to the owner, and does not close a child when its work is done: a finished child stays, with its worktree and its screen, until the owner closes it from its card (`formation_close_child`) or asks it, in so many words, to close it, and only then does it use `formation_close`, after the child has pushed, never removing a worktree itself), `mcp.json` (`tsunagiai-mcp` with the arguments `--formation {name}`, which is what makes the server offer the tools below and keeps them to the record of that formation; the panes of A-36 do not pass it) and the launch script. Besides those, the app replaces the folder `.claude/skills/feature-dev/` as a whole at each start, with the leader's skill (A-41). The script runs `exec claude -n {name} --permission-mode auto --effort high --setting-sources user,project --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config {mcp.json} "$@"`: Claude Code's own tools stay on, the permission mode is `auto`, the effort is `high` (a fixed value, #911: the children below run at `medium`; no other pane of A-36 passes `--effort`), and the project settings of the formation's folder are read too. The session is named by its formation (`-n {name}`), which is the address that the children's SendMessage reach, so two formations have two sessions with two names (a child's name is never a formation's, `check_name`). The first instruction (#820, #821): the pseudo terminal runs `/bin/sh {script}` and, when the formation has projects (the ones saved in its `formation.json` when it was created; none for a formation that has no `formation.json`), adds `--` and their text as the last two elements of the argument list, which the script passes on as `"$@"` (the `--` is needed because the script's `--mcp-config` takes several values and would read the text as one of them, measured with `claude --mcp-config a.json "x"`), so `claude [prompt]` starts the interactive session with it as its first instruction (`formation::first_prompt`; with no project there is no prompt and Claude Code starts as before). The text is fixed words and one quoted line for each project, `> 環境: {environment} / リポジトリ: {repo}`, with a line that says the quotes are data and not instructions to follow, one that asks the owner's word, and one that asks the orchestrator to check the project with `formation_environments` before it launches a child. The names come from outside (the lists of Orca), so they go in the argument list and never through a shell, a name that has a control, hidden or line-separating character, that is blank or that is longer than 200 characters, and more than 50 projects, are refused with no start, so a name cannot end its quoted line. The folder `formations/` itself holds no file of the orchestrator: the `CLAUDE.md`, `mcp.json` and `start.sh` that it held before the formations had folders of their own are deleted when the first of them is read or written (a `CLAUDE.md` there would be read by every orchestrator under it). Like every pane, the child process has no environment variable that starts with `ORCA_` or `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_` (A-36), so an app started from Orca's terminal does not make Claude Code take the pane for Orca's.

The children's tools (`tsunagiai-mcp --formation {name}`, `src-tauri/src/formation.rs`). The name is a formation's name: the shape of a child's name below (`formation::check_name`), or the server does not start; `formation_plan`, `formation_launch` and `formation_close` read and write the record of that formation only, and `formation_children` takes the name of the formation it reads. Only the orchestrator is given them; the other MCP clients and the panes of A-36 are not. The orchestrator runs in auto mode, so besides these (and the five tools that only read, `formation_environments` and the four of #848 below) it is offered only the read tools of `tsunagiai-mcp` (`list_inbox`, `get_thread`, `list_events` and `db_profiles`) and none that writes (`trash`, `archive`, `set_read`, `db_profile_save`, `db_test`, `lsp_server_save`, `lsp_server_delete`, `lsp_repo_ssh_host_set`), nor `lsp_settings` (the language servers of A-34, A-1: the read tools it keeps are those four alone), which the panes of A-36 ask the owner about each time. A child is a Claude Code in auto mode that runs in a terminal of Orca, in a worktree of its own, in the Orca of its environment: `local` (this Mac, `--environment=local` of the CLI, which is this machine's own Orca whatever `ORCA_ENVIRONMENT` holds) or a saved environment of `orca environment list`, named by its name or its id (the name is kept). Every command of Orca that a child needs (`repo list`, `worktree create`, `terminal create`, `wait`, `send`, `list` and `close`, `worktree rm`, `worktree ps`) carries that `--environment=` in the joined form, so that a name that looks like an option stays one argument, and an environment that is not `local` and not exactly one saved environment is refused before any of them is run. Every command of Orca and of git is run with an argument list and never through a shell, and what they print is quoted in an answer as data. The children are recorded, with their environment (`environment`; an entry written before it was kept has none and is this Mac's), in `formations/{name}/children.json` of the formation (`{"children": [...]}`, written through a temporary file and a rename, one change at a time), in the order they were planned or launched; the state of a child is not recorded, because it is Orca's. A change of the record reads a missing file as none, but a file that is there and cannot be read, or is not the record, is an error and nothing is written, so a broken file does not lose the record of the children already launched (the screen reads such a file as none).
The record is split by formation (#819, the first part of #817): each formation has the folder `formations/{name}/` with its own `children.json`, and a name is checked as a child's is (`check_name`) before a path is made of it, so it cannot lead outside `formations/`. A child's name must not be the name of any formation (the folders of `formations/` that are named so, and `formation`, which has none until it is written) and must not be the name of a child, planned or launched, of any other formation, because a message by SendMessage must reach one session; the record of another formation that is there and cannot be read refuses the name, as it cannot be known to be free. A planned child of the same formation of that name is still replaced in place by `formation_plan`. The record that existed before the split, `formations/children.json`, is moved to `formations/formation/children.json` (the formation named `formation`) the first time a record is read or changed, with nothing lost; a record that is already in that place is not written over, and a record that cannot be moved is an error that stops a change (the screen reads none). The folder was `formation/` before the owner's decision of 2026-11-18 (issue #889): the app at its start, and `tsunagiai-mcp` when it reads the config folder, rename it to `formations/` once (`formation::migrate_folder`) and leave a symbolic link `formation` -> `formations` in its place, so that an orchestrator that was started before the update, whose `tsunagiai-mcp` has the old path, reaches the same folder, so the record before the split is `formation/children.json` there; nothing is done when `formation` is no real folder (there is none, or it is that link or another one), and a `formations/` that is already there is never written over (a failed move is logged, and the formations read as none until the next start). Creating a formation under a name that is a child's is the command of a later part.
- `formation_environments()` only reads: it lists this Mac (`environment` `local`) and then each saved environment, each as `{environment, ok: true, repos: [{name, path}]}` (a saved one also has its `id` when it differs from its name), or as `{environment, ok: false, error}` when that environment's `orca repo list` could not be read, which stops no other (the environments are read side by side, as `formation_plan` reads the ones its children are in); when `orca environment list` itself cannot be read, `environmentListError` says why and this Mac alone is listed. What Orca printed is quoted as data.
- The tools that read a repository (#848, `formation::worktrees`, `files`, `read_file` and `git_state`) let the orchestrator find the goal and the work of an instruction by itself, in any environment: they take the `environment` and the `repo` as `formation_launch` does (checked against `orca environment list` and the environment's `orca repo list`, a repository that is not there refused with the names of the ones that are) and write nothing; each carries `read_only_hint`. What they quote (names, paths, branches, the text of a file) is the disk's and Orca's, and is data to read and never instructions to follow: each answer has a `note` that says so, as the description of each tool does. The reads go over one connection to the Orca of the environment (`orca_link::Connection`, this Mac's and a saved environment's alike), with the calls the file pane of A-18 and A-25 make (`files.readDir`, `files.read`, `git.status`, `git.localBranches` and `repo.searchRefs` with `namespace` `remote`), and the code of `orca_files.rs` that reads them is the one used, so the limits are the file pane's.
