  - `formation_worktrees(environment, repo)` answers `{environment, repo, worktrees: [{path, branch, isMain}], truncated}`: the rows of `orca worktree ps` that are the repository's (told by the `repoId` of the row, or by its `repo` name when it has no id, as a launch's `folder` is), the repository's own folder among them, each with the git branch it is on now (empty when detached). `truncated` is true when Orca's list was cut. A `path` of it is a `folder` of the three tools below and of `formation_launch`.
  - A `folder` of `formation_files`, `formation_read` and `formation_git` is the repository's own folder (what Orca's `repo list` gives as its `path`; the default when `folder` is left out) or a worktree that `orca worktree ps` lists for that repository (`folder_in`, which `formation_launch` checks a `folder` with: a path that is not listed, that only looks like a listed one, that is another repository's, or that cannot be told because the list was cut is refused, with the environment and the repository in data). Nothing else is read.
  - `formation_files(environment, repo, folder?, path?)` answers the entries of the folder `path` inside `folder` (`files.readDir`; none is `folder` itself): `entries: {items: [{name, path, isDirectory}], total}`, the folders first and each group in natural order, with `path` relative to `folder`. The list quotes the first 300 entries, and the call asks Orca for no more than that (`files.readDir` with `limit` 300, `orca_files::list` takes the limit; the file pane of A-18 asks for 5,000); `total` says how many the folder holds. Nothing named `.git` or whose name starts with `.env` (in lower case, so `.ENV` too) is listed or counted, and a `path` with such a name in it is refused (`.github` and `.claude` are listed), because the orchestrator runs in auto mode and reads text from outside too, and a token in `.git/config` or a value in `.env` must not reach its context.
  - `formation_read(environment, repo, folder?, path, offset?, limit?)` answers `{path, content, offset, lines, totalLines}`, the text of the file as the editor of A-18 reads it (`files.read`): a file over 384 KiB, one that is not UTF-8 or is binary, one whose line ends are mixed, and one that is not there are refused with the reason, and no content is returned. A `path` with a name `.git` or one that starts with `.env` in it (`.env`, `.env.local`, `.envrc`) is refused before Orca is asked, as for `formation_files`. The answer quotes at most 2,000 lines (`READ_LINES`): `offset` (the line to start from, 1 is the first; the answer's `offset` says where the lines start) and `limit` (at most 2,000) choose the range, `lines` is how many are quoted and `totalLines` how many the file has, so the next range is `offset` + `lines`; an `offset` past the last line is refused.
  - A `path` of the two is relative to `folder` and is checked before Orca is asked (`orca_files::check_relative`): it is not empty for a file, and has no absolute path, no `.` or `..` element, no empty element (`a//b`) and no control character, so it cannot lead outside the folder; one that does is refused and nothing is asked.
  - `formation_git(environment, repo, folder?, query?)` answers `changes: {items: [{status, path}], total}` (`git.status`: the changed and untracked files), `upstream` (the `upstreamStatus` of `git.status` as Orca gives it, when it does), `localBranches: {items: [{name, current, inUse}], total}` (`git.localBranches`, `inUse` being a branch that another worktree has checked out) and, with a `query`, `remoteBranches: {query, items: [{name, localName}], total}` (`repo.searchRefs` of the repository by its id, in the `remote` namespace, which is how a launch finds a branch that only `origin` has; an empty `query` lists the first ones; a text with a control character or over 2,048 bytes is refused before Orca is asked). Each list quotes its first 300. An answer of `git.status` with no list of files is an error, and not an empty list.
- `formation_launch(environment, repo, name, branch?, folder?, agent?, prompt)` (`environment` is `local`, the PC of the orchestrator: any other is refused with the reason, "The record of a formation of a saved environment" above, and nothing is read, made or written) makes a new worktree in the repository `repo` of that environment (its name in that environment's `orca repo list`, its id or its path, one repository exactly; a folder is not one; one that the environment's list lacks is refused, and the answer's `data` holds the `environment` and the names of its repositories as candidates) on the new branch `branch` (#824, below; with no `branch` Orca names the branch, and with `folder` no worktree is made, "A folder that is there" below), makes a terminal in it that runs `claude -n {name}` in auto mode at the effort `medium` (`--command "claude -n {name} --permission-mode auto --effort medium --plugin-dir '{config}/formation-plugin'"`, the effort a fixed value, #911, and the plugin of A-41 written before anything is made), waits for Claude Code to take input, sends `prompt` as the first instruction, and records the child (with its `agent`, and `created`, whether tsunagiai made the worktree, "A folder that is there" below). `name` is a lowercase letter or a digit and then lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, at most 40 characters, not the name of any formation (the name of its orchestrator's session, which a message to the child must not reach), not the name of a child of another formation (planned or launched, so that the address of SendMessage is one session), and not the name of a child of this formation that is launched already; the prompt is 1 to 20,000 characters with no NUL and not blank, and it is sent with the hidden and control characters taken out as A-9 does (C0 controls other than the line break and the tab, DEL, zero-width, bidi and tag characters) and the C1 controls (U+0080 to U+009F) too, which the count and the blank check read as well. A planned child of that name is replaced in place. The answer is `{ok: true, name, environment, repo, agent, path, branch, handle, paneKey, created, closedShells}`; with `agent: "codex"` the terminal runs a Codex instead, and no plugin is written (A-41, #920); a step that fails after the worktree was made answers `{ok: false, error, step, data, note}` and leaves the worktree, which `formation_close` removes once the child is recorded (a child that works in a `folder` is only detached).
  - The branch (#824, #849). `branch` is optional and has no form: any name that `git check-ref-format` accepts and that is no option (`orca::check_ref`, which trims it; a blank one is none; `formation::target_of`), for example `feature/WON-2482`. The ticket-and-kind form that #824 required (`formation::check_branch`) is gone, so the orchestrator may name the branch as the work needs, with or without a user name. A branch that git refuses is refused before Orca is asked, with nothing made and nothing recorded. With a `branch` the worktree is made by `worktree.create` with `branchNameOverride` and `branchIntent` ("The start and the branch" below); with none, no intent is sent and Orca names the branch (its answer is recorded as the child's branch, and the check that the branch is the one asked for is not made); the name of the worktree is the child's, and its place is under the repository ("The place" below).
  - A folder that is there (#849). With `folder` (an absolute path; refused together with `branch`, and when it is not absolute, before Orca is asked) no worktree is made, and the repository's settings in Orca and its git folder are not touched: the child works in the folder, which must be the repository's own folder or a worktree of that repository in `orca worktree ps` of the environment (`formation::folder_in`: a row of exactly that path whose `repoId` is the repository's id, or whose `repo` is its name when the row has no id). A path that the list lacks (also when the list was cut, `truncated`: it cannot be told) and a folder of another repository are refused, with nothing recorded. The terminal is made in it as for a new worktree (`orca terminal create --worktree path:{folder}`, with `claude -n {name}`), the plain shells that Orca opens in a worktree it makes are not closed (the folder was there, and its terminals are the owner's), and the child is recorded with `created: false` (`Launch::created`; a record written before the field has none and is read as `true`, as a child tsunagiai made). Its `branch` in the record and the answer is the git branch that `orca worktree ps` names for the folder (empty when it is detached). The preparation of the repository (`prepare_repo`: its worktree base and `info/exclude`) is not made, because no worktree is. Closing such a child ("Closing" below) removes nothing.
  - The place (#842). A child's worktree is made in the repository it works on: `{repository}/.claude/worktrees/{the repository's folder name}/{child}`, where Orca puts one more folder, the repository's name, under the base because its setting of nested workspaces (`nestWorkspaces`) is on (measured with Orca 1.4.261). The tool makes it so by the repository's "place of worktrees" in Orca (`worktreeBasePath` of `orca repo list`, which `orca project setup-update --setup={repository id} --worktree-base-path=.claude/worktrees --json --environment=...` sets; the relative path is joined to the repository's folder by Orca, and the worktree stays Orca's own: `orca worktree ps` lists it, so the flow pane, the close and the terminals work as for any worktree). The setting is made in two places: `formation_create` sets it for each repository of the formation's projects (the repositories of each environment are read once; a setting that Orca refuses takes the formation back, its folder is deleted and the message says so, and names the repositories whose setting was changed before it, which is not taken back: Orca keeps them as they were set), and `formation_launch` sets it, before the worktree is made, on the child's repository when the list it has just read does not have it (so that a repository the formation was not made with is set too); a repository whose base is `.claude/worktrees` already, or the absolute path of that folder in the repository, is left as it is and nothing is written. No other setting of Orca is changed: neither `nestWorkspaces` nor `workspaceDir`, which every other repository's worktrees keep using. The setting of the repository is Orca's, so it holds for the worktrees that A-9 (the hand-over) and A-16 (the plus of the work pane) make in that repository too: they are made in the same folder from then on (the worktrees made before stay where they are). The tool checks the answer: a worktree whose path is not under `{repository}/.claude/worktrees/` (or holds a `.` or `..` part) is closed, removed again with its branch as for another branch above, and refused with the path and the folder named; a repository whose path the list lacks is not compared. For a repository of this Mac the tool also keeps the folder out of the repository's `git status` before the worktree is made: it appends the line `/.claude/worktrees/` to `info/exclude` of the repository's common git folder (`git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir`, so a repository whose `.git` is a file or that is a linked worktree leads to the same file), makes the file and its folder when they are missing, writes nothing when a line of it names the folder already, and never changes the repository's `.gitignore`; when that cannot be done the launch is refused before anything is made, and the answer names the step (`git の追跡外の設定`). A repository of a saved environment is on another machine: its file is not written, and `.claude/worktrees/` shows in its `git status` unless that machine ignores it. The close is as before: `inspect_child` takes a worktree for the child's only when `orca worktree ps` lists it and it is not the repository's own folder (`isMainWorktree`), so a worktree under the repository is closed like any other, and a folder under the repository that Orca does not list (the folder `.claude/worktrees/{repository}` itself, `src`) is never removed, forced or not.
  - The start and the branch (#850). The worktree is made through Orca's runtime RPC `worktree.create`, not through `orca worktree create`, which sends no branch intent and makes `{branch}-2` in silence when a name is taken. The call goes over one connection to the Orca of the environment (`orca_link::Connection`; this Mac's unix socket, or the saved environment's WebSocket), so a repository of a saved environment is decided as one of this Mac, by what its own Orca says. With a `branch`, the tool first reads the repository's branches as the branch button of A-25 does: `git.localBranches` with `worktree: "path:{the repository's own folder}"` (the `path` of `orca repo list`) and `repo.searchRefs` with `{repo: "id:{repository id}", query: "origin/{branch}", namespace: "remote"}`; then the params are `{repo: "id:{repository id}", name: {the child's name}, noParent: true, branchNameOverride: {branch}, branchIntent, baseBranch}` and `branchIntent` is
    - `open-existing-v2`, with no `baseBranch`, when the branch is a local one: Orca opens it at its own commits (also when it is ahead of `origin/{branch}`, which is its upstream);
    - `open-existing-v2` with `baseBranch: "origin/{branch}"` when only `origin/{branch}` has it (what an earlier child pushed and `formation_close` closed: `worktree rm --delete-branch` leaves `origin/{branch}` and deletes the local branch): Orca makes the branch of that name at the pushed commit, with `origin/{branch}` as its upstream (nothing is fetched, and commits that were not pushed are not taken over);
    - `create-exact` otherwise, with `baseBranch` set to the tool's `base` (a ref such as `origin/develop`, checked by `orca::check_ref` as `branch` is) when there is one, and none when there is not: Orca starts from its own default, which is `origin/HEAD` of the repository (`origin/main`, `origin/master`, `main`, `master` when it is not set) unless the owner set a base ref for that repository in Orca.
    The branch is the child's (`Launch::branch_made`, `Intent::makes_branch`) for `create-exact`, for `open-existing-v2` with a start (Orca makes the local branch from `origin`), and when Orca names it; it is not the child's when a local branch was opened as it is. The record keeps it (a record written before the field has none and is read as made), and a close, and the removal of a worktree that was made on the wrong branch or in the wrong place, run `worktree rm` with `--delete-branch` only for a branch that is the child's, so that a branch that the repository had (`main`, the owner's working branch) and its upstream are never deleted.
    With no `branch`, no intent is sent and Orca names the branch (as before). `base` is for a branch that is new: with a branch that exists, with no `branch` and with `folder` it is refused before anything is made. Orca never makes the branch under another name: it refuses a branch that another worktree has open (`Branch "…" is already checked out at "…"`) and a taken name (`worktree_create_exact_name_taken`, nothing created), and the tool answers `{ok: false, error, step, data}` with the code and Orca's whole message (the 1,000 first characters; `orca_link::MESSAGE_LIMIT`) in `data.output`, with nothing left behind. Measured on this Mac's Orca 1.4.261 with a throwaway repository (#850): each of the three cases made a worktree on the branch asked for, none made `-2`, and its `status.get` has the capabilities `worktree.open-existing-branch.v2-request.v1` and `worktree.create.exact-name.v1`. The tool still compares the branch Orca answers with `branch`, and when they differ (an Orca that ignores the intent) it closes the terminals of the worktree it just made and removes it, with its branch when the branch is the child's (`worktree rm`, `--delete-branch` only then, no `--force`; it holds nothing), and answers `{ok: false, error, step}` that names both branches. A worktree that cannot be removed is left and named.
  - The orchestrator's `CLAUDE.md` leaves the place and the branch of each child to the orchestrator: it says what the three places do, that one branch cannot be open in two places, and that a refused launch is tried again another way before the owner is asked (#851).
- `formation_plan(children)` records children that are launched later, each `{name, environment, repo, agent?}` (`agent` is `claude`, the default, or `codex`, and is the agent that `formation_launch` takes when it is given none, A-41; `environment` is `local` as for `formation_launch`, and another is refused with nothing planned; the repository is checked against the environment's list as a launch does, and the name of the repository is recorded): a planned child of the same name is replaced in place, a new one goes to the end, and a name that is launched already is refused with nothing changed. The screen shows a planned child as planned.
- `formation_close(name)` is used by the orchestrator only when the owner asks it, explicitly, to close a child (#912): a child whose work is done is not closed by it, and stays, with its worktree, its branch and its screen, until the owner closes it from its card (`formation_close_child`) or asks the orchestrator to. It closes the child's terminals and removes its worktree, with its branch when the branch is the child's (`Launch::branch_made`, "The start and the branch" above), and its entry of the record. A child recorded with `created: false` (it works in a folder that was there, "A folder that is there" above) is not that: only its terminal is closed and its entry dropped (`Closing::Detach`), and the folder, any worktree and any branch are never removed, whatever git says of them (nothing is looked at, so a change, an untracked file or a commit no remote has does not stop it; the answer is `{ok: true, name, folderKept: true, terminalClosed}`). Its terminal is closed only when Orca's list says that it is the child's (`terminal_is_childs`, as for a worktree that is gone, below): for a Claude Code child `orca worktree ps` lists an agent of the recorded `paneKey` under the recorded path (`has_agent_at`), and for a Codex child, which `ps` has no row for (A-41), `orca terminal list` of its environment (`all_terminals_args`) lists the recorded handle, not orphaned, with a `worktreePath` equal to the recorded path (`is_childs_terminal_listed`, as the state of the child is read); the record is a file the orchestrator can write, so a handle that the list does not confirm (one that is listed under another path, such as the owner's own terminal or the leader's, or under none, as a floating terminal) is left alone and `terminalClosed` is false, and a list that cannot be read confirms none. The deletion of a formation closes its children through the same check. A worktree that tsunagiai made is not removed (also with the owner's force) while another child of the same formation, in the same environment, is recorded with `created: false` at the same path (`folder_holder`): the close is refused with the child's name in data and nothing is removed, and closing that child first lets it go on. The deletion of a formation closes the children with `created: false` first, so that it is not stopped by this. The record is still not trusted for what it does ask of Orca: the handle must be a terminal handle, the path absolute, the environment this Mac or exactly one saved one, and `orca worktree ps` of it must be readable, as for any child (a folder that is gone from the disk is dropped as a worktree that is gone is, and a saved environment that is gone as before). The check that the worktree is listed and is not the repository's own folder is for the worktree that tsunagiai made only. Every command of Orca goes to the child's environment (`--environment=`), and the environment of the record must be this Mac or exactly one saved one (by its name or its id), or nothing is done. When `orca environment list` was read and has no saved environment of that name or id (the owner removed it in Orca), there is nothing of the child to ask for: no command of Orca or git is run, nothing is deleted, its entry is dropped, and the answer is `{ok: true, name, environmentGone: true, terminalClosed: false}`; a list that cannot be read, or a value that fits two saved environments, refuses as before. A worktree with a changed or untracked file, or with a commit that no remote has, is not removed (no `--force`), nothing else is done and the answer says why; on this Mac a repository with no remote (`git remote` prints nothing) has no commit to ask for, so only the changed and untracked files are looked at. A child of a saved environment (#811) has these looked at through that environment's Orca, because its worktree is on another machine: the runtime calls `git.status` (the changed and untracked files, and the upstream's `hasUpstream`, `upstreamName` and `ahead`) and `git.history` with `limit` 1 (its `remoteRef`), over one connection of A-7 (`orca_link.rs`; they are not a public interface of Orca, read in the `app.asar` of Orca 1.4.261 and tried on this machine's Orca over its unix socket, and a saved environment's was not tried). Any file in `entries` is a change; a branch is clean of commits only when it has an upstream, `git.history` names that upstream as a branch of a remote (`remoteRef` of the same name whose id starts with `refs/remotes/`), and `ahead` is 0. A branch with commits ahead of its upstream is kept, though another remote may have them (`git rev-list HEAD --not --remotes` is not asked). What cannot be told stops the close with `{ok: false, error, step, data}` and nothing deleted, and is never read as none: the answer lacks `entries`, `upstreamStatus` or its fields, the call fails, or the branch has no upstream (Orca cannot tell a repository with no remote from a branch that was never pushed, so a child of a saved environment on such a branch is not closed, and the owner decides). A child that is only planned is dropped from the record. The record is a file the orchestrator can write, so nothing is deleted on its word alone: the recorded path must be absolute and the handle must be a terminal handle, and, for a child tsunagiai made, `orca worktree ps` must list a worktree of that path that is not the repository's own folder (`isMainWorktree`), or the close is refused, with nothing deleted and the entry kept; the branch is not compared, because a child may switch it. When the worktree is gone (the owner removed it in Orca), `orca worktree ps` of the child's environment is read, the child's terminal is closed only when Orca's list says that it is the child's, as above (an agent of the recorded `paneKey` is listed under a worktree of that path, or, for a Codex child, its handle is in `orca terminal list` under the recorded path; when it is not, or the list cannot be read, no terminal is touched and `terminalClosed` is false), its entry is dropped, and the answer is `{ok: true, name, path, worktreeGone: true, terminalClosed}`, so that the name can be used again. This Mac's worktree is gone when the recorded path is no folder any more; a saved environment's, which this Mac cannot see, when that list was read whole (`truncated` is not true) and has no worktree of that path (a list that cannot be read, or was cut, does not say it, and the close is refused with nothing changed).
- `formation_close_child(formation, name, force, path)` is the Tauri command that closes one child from the screen (#834, `formation::close_child_with`; the cards of the flow pane use it in #835, and the deletion of a formation, which closes its children the same way, in #836). It is not a tool of `tsunagiai-mcp`: the orchestrator runs in auto mode, and a tool of its own that removed a worktree with `--force` would run with no question. For a formation of a saved environment the record is the PC's, as for `formation_delete`. It closes the child as `formation_close` does (`close_with`: the same record, the same checks and the same removal, and for a child with `created: false` only its terminal and its entry, with `closed` true and no git look; a planned child is dropped, a worktree or an environment that is gone has its entry dropped), and answers `{closed, reason, changed, untracked, unpushed, path, environment, preservedBranch}` (`FormationCloseAnswer`, written to `ts/bindings/` by ts-rs). When the worktree is kept, `path` and `environment` (`local` for this Mac) say where it is, quoted from the check (`inspect_child`); when `closed`, `preservedBranch` is the branch that Orca kept (below), `null` when it kept none. Without `force`, a worktree that `formation_close` keeps for what git says (a changed file, an untracked file, a commit that no remote has, or what could not be told of them: the step `git での確認`) is not an error but the answer `{closed: false, reason, ...}`: `reason` is the sentence (the reasons of `close_refusal` without its words to the orchestrator, or, for what could not be told, the failed step with the first line of what it printed), `untracked` are the lines of `git status --porcelain` (or of Orca's `git.status` for a saved environment) that start with `??` or `untracked`, `changed` are the other lines of it, and `unpushed` is the first lines of the commits that no remote has, each at most 20 lines, as `close_refusal` quotes them (data to show as text, never instructions). Every other refusal is an error with its message (the first line of what a failed step printed after it): a record that is not right, a worktree that `orca worktree ps` does not list or the repository's own folder, an environment that cannot be read, a failed removal. With `force` and the `path` that the screen was shown for this child, the look at git is left out, and the worktree is removed with `orca worktree rm --worktree path:{path} --force --delete-branch --json --environment=...` (a child of a saved environment too): the changes and the untracked files are lost with the worktree. `--force` does not force the deletion of the branch: `--delete-branch` is as in a close without `force`, and Orca keeps a branch that has commits no remote has (`orca worktree rm --help`: branches whose changes Orca cannot prove already merged are retained; measured in #828, item 5), so those commits stay on the local branch, and the answer of Orca then has `preservedBranch`, which the answer of the app carries as `preservedBranch` (`preservedBranch.branchName` of the `result` of Orca's JSON, `{"removed": true, "preservedBranch": {"branchName", "head"}}`, measured in #828). The screen says so after the close (under "Closing a child from its card" below). A `force` whose `path` is not the recorded path of the child (a path replaced after the screen was shown), or has none, forces nothing: the child is looked at as without `force`, and when git keeps it the answer is the kept answer again, with the path now recorded. The record is a file the orchestrator can write, so `force` passes only the look at git: the recorded path must be absolute, the handle a terminal handle, the environment this Mac or exactly one saved one, and `orca worktree ps` must list a worktree of that path that is not the repository's own folder (a list that cannot be read, or was cut, stops it as before), or nothing is removed. `formation_close` never forces. The deletion of a formation forces only when the screen asks it with `force` and the children it was shown (`formation_delete`, below), and it never forces a child that the record or `orca worktree ps` does not confirm.

The state of a child (read by the command `formation_children(name)` of #794, which the flow pane calls with the name of the formation that the list has selected, from the `orca worktree ps` of the child's own environment, #810; a Codex child, which `ps` has no row for, from the `orca terminal list` of its environment, A-41). The command reads the record, finds the environment of each launched child as `formation_launch` does (an entry with none is `local`; `local`, or exactly one saved environment of `orca environment list` by its name or its id, which is read only when a child is in a saved environment; any other value is refused and never reaches the CLI, because the record is a file the orchestrator can write), and runs `orca worktree ps --environment=<name>` (the joined form) once for each environment that has a Claude Code child, and `orca terminal list --json --environment=<name>` (the terminals of every worktree, the floating ones too) once for each environment that has a Codex child, side by side, however many children are in it; `orca environment list` is read at the same time as this Mac's `ps`, and the `ps` of a saved environment waits 5 seconds at most (this Mac's and the list's wait 15), so that one environment that answers slowly does not hold back the others, and one that does not answer in time is one that could not be read. A Claude Code child is joined only to the agents of its own environment's `ps`, and a Codex child only to the terminals of its own environment's list. Each row of the answer holds the child's `environment` (`local`, or the saved environment's name, also when the record holds its id), which the screen reads its terminal by. For a formation of a saved environment the record is the PC's `children.json`, read through the server of that PC ("The record of a formation of a saved environment" above), and its children are named by that environment. An environment that cannot be read (its `ps` fails, `orca environment list` fails, or its value is refused) leaves its children 作業中 with their `handle`, branch and environment and no time, reply or tool, because a child is not taken for finished unless Orca says so, and the children of the other environments are drawn from what was read; when no launched child could be read, the read fails with the first reason, so that the screen keeps its last list and says why. A planned child is 予定. A launched Claude Code child is found in its environment's `orca worktree ps` by its `paneKey`: it is 承認待ち when its `state` is `waiting`, `blocked` or `permission`, or its `programStatus.state` is `blocked`, or its `state` is `working` while its `programStatus.state` is `idle` (it was interrupted and waits for input); 作業中 for any other `working`, and any state that is not known yet; and 完了 when its `state` is `done`, `failed` or `stopped` (which end a turn too), or when `ps` no longer lists it (its terminal was closed), and then it has no terminal `handle` and no tab. A done child stays in the list (a card with 完了, and, while its terminal is listed, its terminal, which can be selected) until the owner closes it from its card (below) or asks the orchestrator to close it (#912): nothing closes a child because its work ended, and a child whose `claude` ended and whose terminal is gone keeps its card with 完了. A launched Codex child is found by its terminal `handle` in its environment's `orca terminal list` (not orphaned, and with the `worktreePath` of the child's recorded path: a handle listed under another path is not the child's): 作業中 while the terminal is listed, and 完了, with no `handle` and no tab, when it is not (A-41); it is never 承認待ち. The first instruction is never given to the screen, because Orca fills it with the last message sent to Claude, which may be the result of a tool.

The right pane (`#formation-flow`, `ts/formation-flow.ts`, the `aria-label` 「流れ」, 260px wide). Its heading (`#formation-flow-head`) has 「流れ」 (an `h2`) and, at its right end, 「自動で追う」 (`#formation-follow`, below). It shows no count of the children and has no button that closes it: it is shown whenever a formation is selected, and there is no strip of 40px (`#formation-strip`), no mark of a child and no button that opens the pane (the closed pane of #901 is gone with #914). Under the heading is the view of the flow, a column of cards, which takes the rest of the height of the pane.

The view (`#formation-flow-body`) is a column of cards. At the top is the leader's card (`#formation-flow-root`, a button with the name of the formation that is shown and the tag 「リーダー」, which selects the leader's tab); under it, joined to it by a line at their left (`#formation-flow-list`, a left border of 1.5px and a margin of 16px), the children's cards one under the other, in the order `formation_children` gives (launched ones in the order they were started, planned ones after them). The view scrolls vertically inside the pane when the column is taller, and the heading stays. A launched child's card is a button with its name, the time its state has lasted (`elapsed` of `ts/orca.ts`: 「7 分」, and 「25 分前」 for a done child; none when the time is unknown), a dot and the name of its state (作業中, 承認待ち, 完了), and the first line of its last reply; the card of a done child also has a × (`.formation-card-close`, below). The dot and the frame take the colors of Orca: `--agent-working` (a turning ring), `--agent-question` (the card also has a tinted ground) and `--agent-done`. A planned child's card is not a button: a dashed frame, faint text, a hollow dot and 「予定」, with no time and no reply, and when the child is launched the card is drawn again in the same place as a launched one. A read that fails shows its words in `#formation-flow-error` above the tree, keeps the tree of the last read, and the text goes at the next read that works.
The pane is read while a formation is selected: `formation_children` is called, with the name of the formation that is selected in the list, when it is selected and then every 3 seconds, one call at a time (a tick that finds a read in hand starts none), and nothing is read while another screen is shown (`hideFormation` stops the clock). Only the shown formation's children are read; when another formation is selected, the children, the selection and the terminals of the last one are dropped at once (the pane keeps the leader's card, with the name of the new formation, until the first read of it answers), the new formation is read at once, and an answer of a read of the last formation that comes after the switch is dropped (that read is not cut short: the new formation is read when it has ended, so that switching again and again never runs the reads side by side). While no formation is selected, the same function reads `formation_list` alone (not `formation_children`) at the period of the Orca list's read (`shownMs` of `ts/orca.ts`, 10 seconds), for the rows of the list; the two ways share one clock and one read at a time, so they never read together. `formation_children` and `formation_list` are called together on each tick of the flow, and both ask Orca for the same `orca worktree ps` of each environment (and the same `orca environment list`): the calls made at the same time for the same arguments are run once in Rust (`orca::Flights`, `formation::screen_read`) and take the same answer, so Orca is asked once for each period, however many formations there are. A read draws the tree again in place only when what is drawn changed, and keeps the scroll and the focus.
With no child, the pane holds the leader's card alone, and the words of a failed read are shown above it.
The terminal of a child (`#formation-teammate-term`, #797, #803 and #914). It is under the header band of the middle pane, in the place of the leader's terminal, and it is shown only while the tab or the card of a child that has a `handle` (a terminal) is selected; a planned child, and a child whose terminal is closed, have none. It shows the terminal of the selected child from the Orca of the child's environment (#810, #839), the way the Orca screen shows a terminal (A-8, A-12) and the pane of A-9 shows a session's: a child of a saved environment that the Orca screen streams (its list is streamed, A-12) has its terminal streamed (`orca_stream_terminal`), so the screen has its colors and the terminal takes the size of its host (the size is sent with the subscription, with each change of the host and with the keys, `orca_stream_viewport`), and the keys typed go through `orca_stream_input`; any other child is mirrored, a child of this Mac, whose Orca cannot stream (`method_not_supported`), with its screen read every second (`orca_terminal_read` with the handle alone, no colors and no size sent), and a child of a saved environment that is not streamed with its screen read through `orca_terminal_read` with its `environment` (every 2 seconds), and its keys going through `orca_terminal_input` (with the `environment` for a child of a saved environment); in both ways the owner answers a child's permission or question here. The way is chosen by `streamsEnvironment` at each read of the children, so a terminal changes from a mirror to a stream, and back, when the Orca screen starts or stops streaming that environment. Orca streams one saved environment at a time, the Orca screen's target, so a child of another saved environment is mirrored. No new command is added: the commands check the environment or the handle. Leaving the terminal (another tab is selected, the leader's included, another formation is shown, a project or a session is selected, the screen is left, a hidden window, or the child is gone from the list) ends the stream (`orca_stream_terminal_close`), so that Orca gives the terminal back its own size, as the Orca screen does when its tab is left. Only the selected terminal is read. These terminals have an owner of their own (`formation-session` in `showTerminals`), so the Orca screen's terminals and these do not take each other; a child that is gone from the list has its terminal forgotten and read or streamed no more, and selecting a project or a session, or leaving the screen, stops the reads and the streams (the terminals of the Orca screen come back at the next show of the work pane). A file dropped on these terminals is not put in a worktree (no path is known). The terminal fits its new size when it appears or goes, or the middle pane changes its size (the size of its host is watched, A-8).
The tabs and the cards (#914). The leader's tab (`#formation-tab-leader`, a button with `role="tab"`, the name 「リーダー」 and the tag 「high」, `.formation-tab-effort`) is always there. While the leader of the shown formation waits for input (#915; the same `FormationEntry.waiting` as the row of the list) the tab has `data-state="waiting"` and shows the mark ● (`#formation-tab-leader-mark`, `.formation-tab-mark`, in `--agent-question`) before the name, as a child's tab does; otherwise the mark is hidden and `data-state` is empty. After it, in `#formation-tabs-mates`, is a tab for each child in the order of the cards (`.formation-tab` with `data-state` and `data-name`): the mark of its state (`.formation-tab-mark`: ◐ for 作業中 in `--agent-working`, ● for 承認待ち in `--agent-question`, ✓ for 完了 in `--agent-done`, ○ for 予定), its name and the tag 「medium」. A child that has a terminal has a button (`role="tab"`, `aria-selected`, `data-child`). A planned child, and a done child whose terminal is gone (no `handle`), have no button: the tab is shown and a press selects nothing (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #914), and a planned child's tab has a dashed frame. A done child's tab and card have a × (`.formation-tab-close` and `.formation-card-close`, the `aria-label` 「{name} を閉じる」) and stay until the owner presses it or asks the orchestrator to close the child (#912). The strip of tabs scrolls sideways when it is long. Pressing a tab or a card selects it: the leader's tab or card shows the leader's terminal, a child's shows that child's terminal, the terminal takes the keyboard, and 「自動で追う」 is turned off. One tab at most has `aria-selected="true"`, and the card of the selected child has `aria-pressed` (the leader's card has it while the leader's tab is selected); the mark stays through a read, and a selection that moved (the follow moves it too) brings its card into the view of the column and its tab into the view of the strip, without moving the keyboard's focus. The leader's tab is selected at each start and when another formation is selected, and the selection moves to it when the selected child is gone from the terminals (it was closed, or it left Orca's list). The restart button is off while a child's tab is selected.
Closing a child from its card (#835, `ts/formation-flow.ts`). A right press on a card (`contextmenu`), a planned child's too, opens a menu (`openMenu`, at the pointer, anchored on the name of the card) with one item, 「削除」 (the danger color and `DELETE_SVG`), and prevents the browser's menu; a card whose close is on its way has none. The item opens a confirmation under the name (`confirmChildDelete(button, name, created, onConfirm)` of `ts/hint.ts`, `role="alertdialog"`, 「{name} を削除しますか？」 with a body that says the worktree and the branch go and that a child with a change, an untracked file or a commit no remote has is not closed, or, for a child whose `created` is false (`FormationChild.created`, from the record's `Launch::created`: it works in a folder that tsunagiai did not make), the body 「ターミナルを閉じて、一覧から外します。フォルダ、worktree、ブランチは消しません。」, because nothing of it is removed and git is not asked, so no second confirmation follows for it, 「削除」 in the danger color and 「キャンセル」, which has the keys first); 「キャンセル」, Escape and a press elsewhere close it and call nothing. 「削除」 calls `formation_close_child(formation, name, false)` with the name of the formation that the list has selected and the name of the child; the card is `aria-busy` until it answers, and a second close of that child is not started meanwhile. The × of a done child's tab and of its card opens the same confirmation (`confirmChildDelete`), anchored on the name of the tab or of the card, and then calls the same `formation_close_child`; the confirmation of the force that follows a kept worktree is the same, and the tab of the child is `aria-busy` while the close is on its way, as its card is.
- `closed` true: the child is taken off the pane at once (a read that began before is dropped, and the children are read again), and when it was the selected one, the selection moves as it does when a child is gone from the list (to the leader's tab, or to the child that 「自動で追う」 selects): the terminal of the closed child is released and read no more. When the answer has `preservedBranch` (Orca kept the branch for commits that no remote has), `#formation-flow-error` says 「「{name}」のブランチは、どのリモートにもないコミットがあるので、ローカルに残しました（{branch}）。」; the words stay through the reads of the children, and go at the next close and when another formation is shown.
- `closed` false: the card stays, and a second confirmation opens under the name (`confirmChildForce`, `role="alertdialog"`, 「{name} を削除できませんでした」) with the `reason` of the answer, then for each of `changed` (「変更のあるファイル」), `untracked` (「追跡していないファイル」) and `unpushed` (「どのリモートにもないコミット」) that is not empty its head and its lines (`.pop-lost`; git's lines drawn as text, as they are with their leading spaces (`white-space: pre-wrap`, so that the status columns of `git status --porcelain` read right), scrolling when long), under the reason a line with where the worktree is (`.pop-lost-place`: 「この Mac」 or the environment, then the path, from `environment` and `path` of the answer), and the line that a forced close removes the worktree and loses the changes and the untracked files, and that commits no remote has stay on the local branch. 「強制削除」 in the danger color calls `formation_close_child(formation, name, true, path)` with the `path` of that answer, so that only the worktree that was shown is forced, and then goes on as above; 「キャンセル」, Escape and a press elsewhere close it and call nothing more.
- A failure (the command's error: the record is wrong, the worktree is not in Orca's list, Orca failed) shows its message in `#formation-flow-error` as a failed read does, keeps the cards, and goes at the next read that works.
An answer that comes after another formation was selected is not drawn. `formation_close_child` is called by the screen only, never by the orchestrator (above).
「自動で追う」 (`#formation-follow`, an icon button (`.icon-btn`) with the `aria-label` 「自動で追う」 and no text; `aria-pressed` is shown as a pale green ground (`--soft`) and a green icon) is at the right end of the heading of the right pane (`#formation-flow-head`), so it is shown with the pane. It is off at each start of the app and each time a formation is opened, so the middle pane stays on the leader's tab, where the owner types, and moves to a child's tab only after the owner presses the button on (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #914); it stays as it was while the screen is left and shown again, in memory only. While it is on, each read moves the selection, and with it the tab of the middle pane (`followTarget`): to the child that waits (承認待ち) and has waited the longest (the smallest `atMs`), else to the selected child when it works, else to the first child that works; it moves nowhere when no child waits or works. The move does not give the terminal the keyboard, so the keys the owner is typing to the orchestrator stay there. A press on a tab or on a card, the leader's too, turns it off, and the button turns it on (it moves the selection at once).
No count on the footer (#913). The footer's two buttons of the mode and the numbers on them (the children that wait for approval in all the formations, #841, and the sessions that wait for a check with the projects that work, #840) are gone with the switch. What remains of them is the sidebar's 開発 button (A-6: the count of the sessions that wait and the yellow that fades while one works), which `drawBadge` of `ts/orca.ts` draws as before. The children that wait are seen in the flow pane of their formation (above); the list's rows show no count.

The formations (#821, `src-tauri/src/formation.rs`; the list that draws them is #822, and the dialog that makes one is #823, below). A formation is a folder `formations/{name}/`, and the formations are the folders of `formations/` that are named as a formation is; none is in the list without its folder, and `formation` is a name like any other (the record that `formations/children.json` held before the split is moved into `formations/formation/` when it is found, A-37 above, so that formation is in the list from then on). What a created formation holds is in `formations/{name}/formation.json` (`{"createdAt": ..., "projects": [{"environment", "repo"}, ...]}`, camelCase, `createdAt` in epoch milliseconds, written through a temporary file and a rename as `accounts.json` is; a formation of a saved environment also gets `orchestratorTerminal`, the handle of its orchestrator's terminal, when that is made); a formation with no such file (a folder made before the file existed) has no project and no time. A file that is there and cannot be read is an error at the start of the orchestrator (the orchestrator does not start as if the formation had no project), and the list reads it as no project. The formations in the list (`#orca-formations`, `ts/formation-list.ts`, #822 and #913) are drawn from `formation_list` at the top of the list pane `#orca-list` of A-6, under the heading 「フォーメーション」 (an `h2`, `.orca-list-head`; the heading 「プロジェクト」 of the projects follows them). The button `#formation-new` (an icon button with `FORMATION_NEW_SVG`, a tree of a leader and three teammates with a plus, and the `aria-label` 「フォーメーションを作る」, no text) is in the header `#orca-header` of the list, level with the button that adds a project, and opens the dialog that makes a formation (below). `#formation-list-feed` has a row for each formation in the order the command gives, a button (`.formation-row`, of the height and shape of a project's row) with a ring before the name (`.formation-row-dot`, gray and hollow) and the name, with the number of its launched children at the right end (`.formation-row-count`, a small pill of the colors of the subagents' badge of a session, A-8, `aria-label` 「起動しているチームメイト {n} 人」; no time and no project, and no pill while the formation has no launched child; the number is `FormationEntry.children.length`, so a child that is only planned is not counted, and a done child is, as long as its entry is in the record). The row has 32 px of room at its left for the arrow of A-8 (below). While the formation's leader waits for the owner's input (`FormationEntry.waiting`, #915, below) the row has `data-waiting="true"`: the dot is filled with `--agent-question` (orange) with a pale ring of the same color around it, and at the right end of the row stands the label 「入力待ち」 (`.formation-row-waiting`, a small pill of a pale orange ground, the same word as Orca's list uses for a Claude that asks, `STATUS_LABELS.question`); a leader that does not wait leaves the gray ring and no label, and the row changes at the next read of the list, with the scroll and the focus kept. The row of the selected formation has `aria-pressed` and a ground of `--soft`, as the selected project has, and is the one the middle and the right pane show; a formation row opens nothing of its own: its children are under it (below). No row is selected at each start of the app, and the selection is in memory only; it is taken off when a project or a session is selected (`deselectFormation`, called by the one function that selects a project in `ts/orca.ts`), and the project's row is not marked while a formation is selected. While none is selected (at each start, with an empty list too, after a project or a session is selected, and after the selected formation is deleted) the leader and the flow panes (`#formation`) are hidden, no orchestrator is started, no `formation_children` is called, and the work pane of Orca is shown; a list with no row shows the heading alone and no other text (`frontend.md`). Pressing another row selects it: the middle pane shows that formation's orchestrator and the right pane its flow (above); pressing the selected row changes nothing. The list is read (`formation_list`) when the development screen is shown and then on the clock of `ts/formation-flow.ts`: 3 seconds while a formation is selected, and at the period of the Orca list's read (10 seconds) while none is, one call at a time; no other screen reads it. A row is drawn again in place only when what it shows changed, and the scroll and the focus stay. The block has at most 40% of the height of the window and scrolls inside. A read that fails, or `readError` of an answer, shows its words in `#formation-list-error` under the heading, keeps the rows of the last read, and goes at the next read that works. When the selected formation is no longer in the list, its flow is read no more and no row is selected (no other row is selected in its place). The screen shows no help text (`frontend.md`). A right press on a row opens the menu that shows its projects and deletes the formation (below). The row shows no project. The launched children of a formation are under its row (#946, the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, layout B). A formation that has some has the arrow of A-8 at the left of its row (`.orca-arrow`, the same button as a project's, `data-arrow="{formation}"`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-label` 「チームメイトを閉じる」 or 「チームメイトを開く」), which opens and folds its children; they are open at first, the fold is in memory only, and pressing the arrow selects nothing and keeps the count. Under the row, while it is open, there is a row for each launched child in the order of the record (`.formation-child`, a button, `data-child-of="{formation}"`, `data-child="{name}"`, indented as the sessions of a project are): the status icon of a session of A-8 (`statusIcon`: 作業中 is a working child, 承認待ち a waiting one with the question mark and 完了 a done one; `aria-label` is the word of the flow pane's card, `STATE_LABELS`), and two lines: the child's name (`.formation-child-name`, the heading of its tab and card) and, under it in the muted color, 「{project} ・ {branch}」 (`.formation-child-where`: the repository name of `FormationListChild.repo` and the branch of its worktree; the project alone while the branch is empty). Pressing the row selects the formation (as pressing its row does; nothing changes when it is already selected) and shows that child's tab (`showChild` of `ts/formation-flow.ts`, with the keyboard in its terminal, as a press on the tab does, and 「自動で追う」 turned off): at once when the children of the formation are read already, else with the first read that lists the child with a terminal; a child with no terminal (a done one whose terminal is gone) selects the formation and leaves the leader's tab. The list keeps the focus on the arrow or the row that has it when it is drawn again. The children are those of the formations of a saved environment too (the same rule as the counts, below).
The list that draws the rows is the answer of `formation_list` only: it asks Orca and the PCs for nothing more than for the counts. Five commands serve the screen:
- `formation_environments()` answers the environments (this Mac first, then each saved environment) with the repositories of each, `{environmentListError?, environments: [{environment, id?, ok, repos?: [{name, path}], error?, label?}]}`, read as the orchestrator's tool `formation_environments` reads them (`formation::environments_view`, the Orcas side by side; an environment that cannot be read has `ok` false and its `error`, and a list of saved environments that cannot be read leaves this Mac's alone with `environmentListError`).
- `formation_create(name, projects)` makes a formation and answers it as the list draws it (below). The name has the shape of a child's name (`check_name`), is not the name of a formation (`formation` too, when it has a folder) and is not the name of a child of any formation (a child's name is the address of its SendMessage and a formation's name is the session name of its orchestrator, the same rule as a child's name against the formations, from the other side); a wrong name is refused before Orca is read. When the formation is made, the worktree base of each project's repository is set to `.claude/worktrees` ("The place" under `formation_launch`). Each project is an environment (its name or its id) and a repository (its name or its path) of `formation_environments`, read for the call, and is saved by their names: an environment that is in no list (the message says so, and why when the list of saved environments could not be read), one whose repositories could not be read, a repository the environment lacks or has twice, the same project twice, a name that the first instruction would refuse (blank, hidden, a control, over 200 characters) and more than 50 projects are refused, and nothing is made. Every project is of one environment, because a formation belongs to one PC ("Where the orchestrator runs" above): projects of two environments are refused with `フォーメーションは一つの PC に属します。環境「{a}」と環境「{b}」にまたがるプロジェクトは入れられません（…）` after the names are resolved, and nothing is made. No project at all is allowed (the orchestrator then starts with no first instruction, and the formation is this Mac's). The folder is made with a call that fails when it is there, under the lock of the record, so two creations of one name make one; a folder that cannot get its `formation.json` is removed.
- `formation_list()` answers `{formations: [{name, projects, environment, createdAt, counts: {planned, waiting, working, done}, children: [{name, repo, branch, path, environment, state, created, handle, paneKey}], waiting}], readError}` in order of name (`environment` is the PC of the formation, `local` or a saved environment's name, and the children of a formation of a saved environment are those of its PC's record, read as `formation_children` reads it; `createdAt` null and no project, and `local`, for a folder that has no `formation.json`); a list with no folder is empty. The counts are the number of its children in each state of A-37 (the mapping of `formation_children`). `children` are the launched children of the formation (`listed_children`, a pure function: a planned child has no worktree and is not in it), in the order of its record, each with the `state` that the counts use, the `branch` and `path` of its worktree on its PC (`Launch::branch`, `Launch::path`) its `environment`, `created` (`Launch::created`: whether tsunagiai made the worktree) and the `handle` and `paneKey` of its terminal and agent (`Launch::handle`, `Launch::pane_key`, which the record keeps also after Orca lists the terminal no more); they come out of the same join to Orca's lists as the counts, so no read is added, and for a formation of a saved environment they are the children of its PC's record. A child whose Orca could not be read is working, as in the counts. `path` and `environment` tell which worktree is a child's when `created` is true, and `handle` and `paneKey` tell which sessions are a child's when it is false: the sessions of the projects under the list (A-8) and the worktrees that the plus (A-16) and 「Orca に渡す」 (A-9) offer leave the worktrees that tsunagiai made for the children out, and a child that works in a folder that was there leaves only its own sessions out of the projects, never the folder (#947, the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, #945), so that a child is seen only under its formation's row. What is counted is not changed: the sidebar button's count, the notices and the working mark of A-6 take the child's sessions in, so that a child that waits for input is noticed. A child's `environment` and the `environment` of a list item are the same PC (`local` is the item with none), so the child of a formation of a saved environment leaves the sessions of that environment's projects. A child that is closed leaves the record, and its worktree is a worktree of its project again. `waiting` (#915) is whether the formation's leader waits for the owner's input, read from the title of the leader's terminal: Claude Code writes the title of its terminal (OSC 0) with `✳` first while it waits at its prompt and with a spinner character (`◐`, `◑`) first while it works, which was measured with Claude Code 2.1.327 in a pseudo terminal and in the titles that `orca terminal list` gives (the title of a terminal that was made with `--title` follows the program's OSC title all the same, measured on the floating terminal of this Mac's Orca). `title_waits` (`formation.rs`, a pure function) is true when the title starts with `✳`; an empty title, one not written yet, and any other first character are not waiting, so a leader is taken for working unless its terminal says otherwise. For a formation of this Mac the title is the one the pane `formation:{name}` of A-36 last wrote: its reader (`claude_code.rs`, `TitleScan`) finds OSC 0 and 2 in what the pseudo terminal prints, also when a read cuts one in two, and keeps the last one while the process lives (nothing is saved; a pane with no process has none and does not wait). For a formation of a saved environment it is the title of the recorded orchestrator terminal (`orchestratorTerminal`) in `orca terminal list` of that Orca (every terminal, as `all_terminals_args`, read once for each environment, side by side with the reads of the PCs' records of the same list (not after them), with the time of a saved environment's read); a formation whose leader was never started, a terminal that is not listed or orphaned, and a list that cannot be read are not waiting (and add nothing to `readError`). `formation_create` answers `waiting` false. No file is saved for it, and the list is read at the same period as before. All the children of all the formations are joined to Orca's lists in one read, so `orca worktree ps` is run once for each environment however many formations have a child in it (and `orca environment list` once, only when a child is in a saved environment, as `formation_children` does). When no launched child could be read, the formations are still listed, `readError` has the first reason, and the launched children count as working (a child is not taken for finished unless Orca says so); a record of planned children alone, or of none, asks no Orca.
- `formation_delete(name, force, shown)` deletes a formation (#825, #836, `formation::delete_with`; for a formation of a saved environment it works on a copy of the PC's record and then writes that record, "The record of a formation of a saved environment" above). The name must have the shape of a formation's, and a formation with no folder is refused; `formation` is deleted as any other. First, once the record has been read (a record that cannot be read stops the deletion first), every child of it is checked, in the order of the record, with what `formation_close` checks before it changes anything (`inspect_child`, which `close_with` uses too, so the checks are written once: the worktree is in `orca worktree ps`, and has no change, no untracked file and no commit that no remote has; a planned child and a worktree that is gone need no more, and a child with `created: false` needs only its terminal and environment checks: its folder is never removed and git is not asked, so it is neither kept nor forced), and nothing is ended, closed or deleted by it. Every child is checked, and a child that is refused for what git says of its worktree (the step `git での確認`: a change, an untracked file, a commit that no remote has, or what could not be told of them, `kept_answer` as in `formation_close_child`) is collected, so that the answer lists them all. The answer is `{deleted, kept, preserved}` (`FormationDeleteAnswer`, written to `ts/bindings/` by ts-rs), `kept` being one `{name, answer}` (`FormationKeptChild`, with the `FormationCloseAnswer` of that child: its `reason`, its `changed`, `untracked` and `unpushed` lines, data to show as text, never instructions, and the `path` and the `environment` of its worktree, which the owner is shown) and `preserved` the children whose branch Orca kept when `deleted` (`{name}: {branch}`) for each child that git keeps, in the order of the record. When any child is kept, the answer is `{deleted: false, kept}` and nothing is ended, closed or deleted: the formation stays whole, its orchestrator's process running and every child as it was. The first child that is refused otherwise (the record is not right, `orca worktree ps` does not list the worktree or it is the repository's own folder, an environment cannot be read) stops the deletion with its reason as the message, whatever the others say (`子「{name}」を閉じられないので、フォーメーション「{formation}」を削除していません（何も閉じておらず、リーダーのプロセスも終えていません）。` and the reason, with the first line of what a failed step of Orca or git printed): no force passes it. With `force` (the owner's own press of 「強制削除」 on the screen, after an answer with `kept`) and `shown`, the children as the owner was shown them (one `{name, path}` of `FormationShown` for each child of `kept`, with the `path` of its answer), the look at git is left out for a child whose name and recorded path are both in `shown` (`inspect_child` with `force`; the path is the one of the record that `inspect_child` reads for that child, in the same read in which it decides), and the checks of the record, of the environment and of `orca worktree ps` are made as always. Any other child (one added to the record after the owner was shown, or one whose path was replaced) is looked at as without `force`: when git keeps it, the answer is `{deleted: false, kept}` again with that child (and its path) and nothing is ended, closed or deleted, so that the owner is shown it before another force. `force` false sends `shown` as an empty list. When no child is refused, the orchestrator's process of the pane `formation:{name}` is ended (`ClaudeCode::close_formation`, whichever start made it), and the deletion goes on only when that process has ended or has been sent the KILL (2 seconds of grace for the TERM, waited for on the thread of the command). The terminal of the orchestrator of a formation of a saved environment is closed right after, before any child: a terminal that cannot be closed and is still in `orca terminal list` of its PC stops the deletion with the reason, before any child is closed (`リーダーのターミナルを閉じられないので、フォーメーション「{formation}」を削除していません（子は閉じていません。リーダーのプロセスは終えています）。`), and one that is gone (not listed any more, or its environment gone from `orca environment list`) counts as closed. The process is ended first because: the tools of `tsunagiai-mcp` run in the process group of the orchestrator, in another process than the app, and the lock of the record (`RECORD`) does not hold them, so a tool that went on could launch a child or write `children.json` while the folder was being deleted. Then every child is closed, in the order of the record except that the children with `created: false` go first (a worktree that tsunagiai made is not removed while one of them works in it, `formation_close` above), as `formation_close` does it (`close_with`: a planned child is dropped from the record, a worktree that is gone has its entry dropped), and with `force`, for a child that `shown` names, with `orca worktree rm ... --force --delete-branch` as `formation_close_child` does (the changes and the untracked files are lost with each worktree; the commits that no remote has stay on the local branch, which Orca keeps and `preserved` names), and any other child without `--force`. A child that is refused only now (its state changed after the check) stops the deletion, with or without `force`, and its reason is the message (`子「{name}」を閉じられないので、フォーメーション「{formation}」を削除していません（閉じ終えた子は閉じたままです。リーダーのプロセスは終えています）。` and the reason): the formation and its folder stay, the children closed before it stay closed, and the formation has no orchestrator process, so that showing it again starts one (the terminal of the orchestrator of a formation of a saved environment is closed already, "Where the orchestrator runs" above). When every child is closed, the record is read once more under its lock, and a child that is in it stops the deletion (the folder stays, and the message says so); otherwise `formations/{name}/` is deleted with all it holds (`formation.json`, `children.json` and the orchestrator's files). A change of the record (`update`) that finds the folder of its formation gone is refused, and writes nothing: it never makes the folder again. The answer of a deletion that worked is `{deleted: true, kept: [], preserved}`, and a failure carries the message. `formation_delete` is not a tool of `tsunagiai-mcp`, and `force` is not an argument of any tool of it: the orchestrator runs in auto mode, and a tool of its own that removed worktrees with `--force` would run with no question.
- `formation_orchestrator(name, restart)` answers `{environment, handle}` (`FormationOrchestrator`, written to `ts/bindings/` by ts-rs): the terminal of the orchestrator of a formation of a saved environment, started or found as "Where the orchestrator runs" above says. A failure carries the one line the pane shows. It is not a tool of `tsunagiai-mcp`.
Deleting a formation (#825, `ts/formation-list.ts`). A right press on a row (`contextmenu`) opens a menu (`openMenu`, at the pointer) and prevents the browser's menu. At the top of the menu are the projects of the formation (`FormationEntry.projects`, the repository name of each, in the order saved), headings that cannot be pressed (disabled rows with `FOLDER_SVG`, in the monospace of the names), and under a line (`divider`) is 「削除」 (the danger color and `DELETE_SVG`), the only item that takes the keys; a formation with no project has the item alone, with no line; a row whose deletion is on its way has none (`formation` has one like any other). The item opens a confirmation under the name of the row (`confirmFormationDelete` of `ts/hint.ts`, `role="alertdialog"`, 「{name} を削除しますか？」 with a body that says the children are closed first and that a child that cannot be closed stops it (nothing is closed, and the orchestrator keeps running, when the check finds the child that cannot be closed; a child refused only when it is closed leaves the orchestrator ended), 「削除」 in the danger color and 「キャンセル」, which has the keys first). 「キャンセル」, Escape and a press elsewhere close it and call nothing. 「削除」 calls `formation_delete(name, false, [])`; the row is `aria-busy` until it answers, and a second deletion of that formation is not started meanwhile. An answer with `deleted` false (children that git keeps; nothing was closed, so the row, the selection and what is shown stay, and no words of failure are drawn and the list is not read again) opens a second confirmation under the name of the row (`confirmFormationForce` of `ts/hint.ts`, `role="alertdialog"`, 「{name} を削除できませんでした」): for each child that was kept, a line 「子「{child}」」 in the danger color, its reason, and git's lines under a head each (「変更のあるファイル」, 「追跡していないファイル」, 「どのリモートにもないコミット」, data from git drawn as text, the head of a kind that has none left out, as `confirmChildForce` does), then, under the reason, a line with where its worktree is (`.pop-lost-place`, as in the card's second confirmation), and at the end a line that says that a forced deletion removes the worktrees of these children and then the formation, that the changes and the untracked files above are lost, and that the commits that no remote has stay on the local branch; 「強制削除」 in the danger color and 「キャンセル」, which has the keys first (the popup scrolls when it is taller than the window). 「キャンセル」, Escape and a press elsewhere close it and call nothing. 「強制削除」 calls `formation_delete(name, true, shown)` with `shown` made of the name and the `path` of each child of that answer, the same way (the row `aria-busy`, one call at a time), and what follows is as after a deletion that works, or, for a refusal, as below. A refusal shows its message in `#formation-list-error` (it stays until another row is pressed, a formation is made or another deletion starts), keeps the rows and the selection, and reads the list again, because some children may have been closed. A deletion that works with `preserved` not empty says 「どのリモートにもないコミットがあるブランチは、ローカルに残しました: {preserved}」 in `#formation-list-error` (it goes as the words of a refusal do). A deletion that works takes the row off the list at once (a read of `formation_list` that began before it is dropped, as with a creation) and reads the list again. When the formation that is shown is the one deleted, no row is selected, as when the shown one leaves the list: the middle and the right pane are not shown (the terminal that was shown is hidden, and only while the development screen is shown does a deletion that ends change the panes: one that ends after the owner has left them shows no terminal), and the read of the deleted formation's flow stops. The terminal of the deleted formation's orchestrator is dropped from the screen (`closeClaudeCode`; Rust has ended its process already) whether the formation was shown or not, and the terminal of another formation that is shown stays shown without a new start.

The list of the dialog that makes a formation (#823, #837, `ts/formation-new.ts`). Under the filter the dialog lists the environments in the order `formation_environments` gives (`.formation-new-env` for the heading, the label or the name of the environment), each with its repositories as rows with a box (`.formation-new-repo`; the box is 16px square, the row is at least 32px high and the gap between the box and the name is 10px, #886). The heading of an environment is shown only while a row under it is shown: an environment with no repository has no heading, and the filter hides the heading of an environment with no repository left, by one rule that also draws the list when the answer first comes (a text typed in the filter before the answer narrows the first drawing too). Each change of the text in the filter (also its emptying) scrolls the list, the box that holds the rows, to its top (`scrollTop` 0), so that the rows that match are seen from the first (#942); the narrowing alone hides rows and does not touch the scroll position. An environment that could not be read is not shown, heading and all, and nothing of it is written in the dialog: its reason is Orca's own words and names no project to choose (#878; the owner's instruction, 2026-11-17). The same goes for a list of saved environments that could not be read: the dialog shows the environments that were read, and no note. The words of a failed creation, of a failed read of the environments and of a failed read of the consents are shown on a card (A-28), and each stays until its × is pressed, whatever is done next (before A-28 the next operation hid them, #944).

No new saved setting for the screen (the selection of the list is in memory); `formations/{name}/formation.json`, `formations/{name}/children.json` and the files of the orchestrator in `formations/{name}/` are the saved files of the tools. For a saved environment the app saves `formation-remote.json` in its config folder (the servers pinned by their public keys and their versions, which is also the owner's consent for each PC, #891 and #893) and keeps its own key in the keychain (service `jp.mysis.tsunagiai.formation`), and the PC has `remote/` in its config folder (the program, `version`, the keys and `pid`). The new communications are the fetch of the program from the GitHub Release, which the PC makes with `curl` in a terminal of its Orca, only after the consent, and the connection of the app to the server of that PC at the port 47823 ("The connection"). Besides them the commands read `orca environment list`, `orca repo list` and `orca worktree ps`, which the tools and `formation_children` read already, and `orca project setup-update` sets the place of a repository's worktrees (#842). The one file that is written beside the saved files above is `info/exclude` of a repository of this Mac, one line, when a child is launched in it ("The place"). The orchestrator is Claude Code and calls an outside service as Claude Code does.
