The consent has its own cases in the same spec: the dialog that asks for the consent of a PC when the projects of one saved environment have none (the lines that say what is put there, and nothing made yet), 「同意する」 calling `formation_consent` and then making the formation that was asked for, 「やめる」 and Escape making nothing and putting nothing on the PC, both buttons off while the program is put there, the words of a failed placement shown with the dialog kept, no dialog for projects of two environments (the refusal is Rust's) and the words of Rust's refusal shown, the same dialog when a formation made before the consent is opened (nothing started before the answer, the terminal made once the owner agrees, nothing shown when the owner declines), and no question for a PC that has the consent or for this Mac. The spec `settings.e2e.ts` ("settings: the PCs with the server of the orchestration (the Orca tab)") covers the card of the Orca tab: its empty note and the read of the consents each time the tab is shown, a row for each PC with its server's version, its state and its button, 「取り消す」 calling `formation_consent_revoke` with the environment's name and drawing the consents that are left, the button off while the PC is being cleared, the words of a refusal shown with the PC kept, and the words of a failed read.

## A-38 Add a project to Orca

The header of the Orca list (`#orca-header`, A-6) has a button that adds a project to Orca, a folder of this Mac or of a saved environment. The frontend is `ts/orca-add.ts` (the popup) and `ts/orca.ts` (the button and the read again of the list); Rust is `orca_project_add_here` (this Mac) and `orca_project_add(path, environment)` (a saved environment, `environment` always given) of `src-tauri/src/orca.rs` (issue #779 for the second), which run `orca repo add --path` and are not repeated here. The folder of a saved environment is chosen on its server, in the dialog of `ts/orca-browse.ts` (issue #782), over `orca_browse_dir(environment, path)` of `src-tauri/src/orca_browse.rs` (issue #780).

Why. The list had no way in: a project had to be added in Orca itself. The owner chose, of the layouts put to them, a button in the header and a small popup with the place and the folder, with no URL clone, no new project and no host to add (2026-11-16, issues #774 and #781). SSH hosts are not in the select: only this Mac and the target are, when it is a saved environment (the list reads only these two, A-33, so a project added to another environment would not show).

The button. `#orca-add` is an `.icon-btn` (a folder with a plus, no text, `aria-label` 「プロジェクトを追加」, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`) in `#orca-header`, at its right end; the button of the list (A-23) keeps the left. It opens the popup under itself and keeps `aria-expanded="true"` while the popup is open.

The popup. A `.menu` window (`.orca-add-pop`, `role="dialog"`, `aria-label` 「プロジェクトを追加」) with a field and a button, and no other text (`frontend.md`). The field is a labelled select (`aria-label` 「場所」): this Mac first, then the target of the last read of the list (`target`, A-33) by name, only when it is one of the saved environments of that read (A-6); no other saved environment and no SSH host is listed, so with this Mac as the target the select holds this Mac alone. The select starts on this Mac, each time the popup opens. The button is 「フォルダを選ぶ…」, with a folder icon, and has the focus when the popup opens. Escape and a press outside close the popup and add nothing.

This Mac. Pressing 「フォルダを選ぶ…」 closes the popup and calls `orca_project_add_here` with no argument. Rust opens the folder dialog of the system (the dialog plugin's `pick_folder` on the main window, waited for as the save dialog of A-22 is; the page gives no place and has no permission for the dialog), and a cancelled dialog answers `false` and adds nothing. A folder picked is added with the same checks and the same call as `orca_project_add` makes, with no environment (this is the only way a folder of this Mac is added: `orca_project_add` takes the name of a saved environment and nothing else), and the command answers `true`; the list is then read again at once (`orca_overview`, the same read as when the screen is shown), so the project is in the list when Orca has added it. A cancelled dialog says nothing and reads nothing. A failure of the dialog or of the add shows its message on a card (A-28), and the list is not read again.

A saved environment. Pressing 「フォルダを選ぶ…」 with a saved environment chosen closes the popup and opens the dialog of that environment's folders, and calls neither `orca_project_add_here` nor `orca_project_add` yet. The dialog has the form of the "browse the host's file system" of Orca itself.
- The frame. A `.scrim` over the dimmed screen with a dialog (`.orca-browse`, `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`, `aria-label` 「{environment} のフォルダを選ぶ」) in its middle, 420 px wide. The screen behind is inert while it is open. The heading is 「{environment} のフォルダを選ぶ」 with a cross (`aria-label` 「閉じる」) at its right end. There is no line that explains the dialog and no placeholder (`frontend.md`).
- The trail. A row with the button 「上のフォルダ」 (an arrow up), the button 「ホーム」 and the trail of the folder shown: 「/」 and each folder of its path, separated by a chevron, the last one bold and not a button, every other one a button that goes to that folder. The trail scrolls sideways to the folder shown when it is longer than the dialog. The paths are read as POSIX paths: the folder above is the path cut at its last 「/」, and a folder inside is the path, a 「/」 and the name. The button above is off at 「/」 and while no folder has been read.
- The field. A field with the `aria-label` 「フォルダを絞り込む」 and no placeholder, which has the focus when the dialog opens and after each folder read. A name typed narrows the rows to the names that hold the text (part of the name, case ignored, a character of a pattern meaning itself). A text that starts with 「/」 narrows nothing: Enter goes to that path. The field is emptied when a folder has been read.
- The list. The folders of the folder shown, in the order Rust gives (the order of their names), each a row with a folder icon, the name and a chevron. A press on a row goes into that folder (`orca_browse_dir` with the path of the row). The names come from the server, so they are drawn as text. A folder with no folder, or a name that no folder holds, shows 「フォルダがありません」. The list is 196 px high and scrolls.
- The keys. ArrowDown and ArrowUp in the field put a mark on a row (down from no mark goes to the first row, up to the last; the mark stays on the ends), and Enter goes into the marked row. Typing, and reading a folder, take the mark off.
- The path. The foot shows the path that 「フォルダを選ぶ」 adds: the marked row, and the folder shown when no row is marked. A path longer than the foot is cut with an ellipsis, and its title holds all of it.
- The read. Opening the dialog reads the home folder (`orca_browse_dir(environment, "")`), and 「ホーム」 reads it again. Each read gives the folder Orca opened (`resolvedPath`), which is the folder shown. An answer of an earlier read, or one that comes after the dialog closed, is dropped. A failed read shows its message in the list (in the color of an error) in place of the rows and keeps the folder shown in the trail, so the way back (the trail, up, home) stays; nothing can be added until a folder has been read, so 「フォルダを選ぶ」 and the path are off while the message is shown.
- The add. 「フォルダを選ぶ」 calls `orca_project_add(path, environment)` with the path above and the name of the environment (never twice: the button is off while it is on its way), closes the dialog, and reads the list again at once, as for a folder of this Mac. A failure closes the dialog and shows its message on a card (A-28), and the list is not read again.
- Closing. 「キャンセル」, the cross, Escape (not the Escape that cancels an IME conversion) and a press on the dimmed part close the dialog, add nothing and give the focus back to the button of the header.

The E2E spec `orca.e2e.ts` covers the button in the header (its label, its place after the button of the list, the popup under it), the select (this Mac first, then the target when it is a saved environment, none when this Mac is the target or none is saved, the start on this Mac), the folder of this Mac (the call of `orca_project_add_here` with no argument, the popup closed, the list read again with the new project drawn), a cancelled dialog that reads nothing again, a failure on a card (A-28), a saved environment that opens the dialog and calls neither command of this Mac, and Escape; for the dialog of a saved environment (#782), its heading, its aria attributes and no placeholder, the home folder read first with the environment and an empty path, the trail, the buttons above and home, the entry into a folder by its row and its path, the narrowing by name, the path typed with Enter, the marks of the arrow keys with Enter and the path that follows them, the call of `orca_project_add` with the path and the name of the environment (for the folder shown and for the marked one) and the list read again, the words of a failed add on a card (A-28) with no new read, the message of a failed read (of the first one and of a typed path) with the folder kept in the trail and nothing to add, a name drawn as text, and the four ways to close with the screen behind inert and the focus given back.

## A-39 Git changes in the Orca file pane

The tree of the file pane (A-18) shows which entries differ from HEAD: a file that git sees as changed or new, and a folder that holds one. Rust is `orca_files_changes` in `src-tauri/src/orca_files.rs`; the frontend is `ts/orca-files.ts` and `src/style.css`.

Why. Seeing which files a session has touched took `git status` in a terminal. The owner chose, of the layouts put to them, the color of the name and the icon with `M`, `U` and a dot at the right end, and no bold (2026-11-18, issues #898 and #900).

The call. `orca_files_changes(path, paths, environment)` takes the paths of the entries of one list (at most 5,000, each checked like a file's path, A-18; `environment` as for the other file commands) and answers `{path, mark}` for each of them that differs from HEAD, in the order asked, with `mark` `changed` or `new` (`OrcaGitMark`). It reads `git.status` of the worktree once. A file is `new` when git calls it untracked or added, and `changed` for every other status (modified, deleted, renamed, copied, a conflict). A folder is what its files are: it is marked by the files under it, `new` when they are all new and `changed` when both kinds are there, and an untracked folder that git does not list file by file marks everything under it `new`. An entry with no difference is not in the answer, and a path that was not asked for is never answered. A check that fails (a folder that is no repository, a call that fails) answers none.

The marks. A row that is marked has `data-change` (`changed` or `new`).
- The name and the icon take the color of the mark: `--warn` for `changed` and `--diff-ins-mark` for `new`. The name is not made bold. The color is drawn over the color of a file open in the editor (A-18), whose ground (`--soft`) stays.
- At the right end of the row (`.orca-file-git`, after the name, which is cut with its ellipsis before it) a file has `M` for `changed` and `U` for `new`, in the same color, and a folder has a dot (6 px, a filled circle with no text) in the same color. An entry with no difference has none.
- A selected row (A-18, the green ground and white text) stays white: its name, icon and mark are white, and its mark of the kind is still in the row (`data-change`).
- An ignored entry (A-18, faint and in italics) keeps that look, with the color and the mark on it.

When it is asked. After a folder is read and drawn, with the paths of its entries in the order of the list, as `orca_files_ignored` is, and at every read of A-18: the return of the focus, the clock of the Orca screen, the app's own create, delete and move, and a switch of the branch. The folder that the list marked as ignored is not asked, because what it holds is ignored (A-18). The clock asks for it also for a folder that holds the entries it held, because a file edited outside the app changes its difference and not the entries (the ignore marks are not asked for again there, A-18). A read of the tree (the root and then the open folders: the return of the focus, the clock and a switch of the branch) and the read again of several folders after the app's own delete or move, or after a scratchpad file is made (A-29), asks for all the folders it read in one call, once the last of them is read, so that the read runs one `git.status` and not one for each open folder (the clock reads every 10 seconds); it asks in more calls only when the paths of the folders pass 5,000. A folder read alone (opened, or read again by itself) is asked for alone, as soon as it is drawn. The rows are drawn when the folder is drawn, and the marks come with the answer: until then, nothing is marked. An answer replaces the marks of the entries of that folder (an entry it does not name loses its mark); a read again keeps the marks until the answer comes, so they do not blink; a check that fails marks nothing new, leaves the marks as they are, and shows no message. Only the last question of a folder is answered, and an answer that comes after the tree became another worktree's, or after the folder was dropped, is dropped. Nothing is drawn when the marks are the ones shown.

Unit tests (`cargo test`) cover the marks of a file, of a folder (by the files under it, and both kinds) and of an untracked folder, the statuses, the order of the answer, the paths of the check and a failed `git.status`; none touches a real Orca. The E2E spec `orca-files.e2e.ts` covers the question (its arguments for this machine and for a saved environment, after the folder is drawn, with nothing marked until the answer), the colors of the name and the icon, the `M`, `U` and the dot at the right end and no bold, an entry with no difference, the selected rows in white, an open file on its ground, an ignored entry with a mark, a check that fails (nothing marked, no message), the marks kept through a read again and replaced by the answer, the clock, a folder that is opened, a folder that is ignored (not asked), and an answer that comes after another worktree's tree took the place.

## A-40 Peek at a session of the Orca list in a modal

A right click on a session row or a project row of the Orca list (A-8) opens a modal in the middle of the screen with the terminal of that session, so that its state can be seen and keys can be typed without selecting its project. The frontend is `ts/orca-peek.ts` (the modal), `ts/orca.ts` (the right click and what the modal shows), `src/index.html` (`#orca-peek`) and `src/style.css`. No Tauri command is new: the terminal is shown through `showTerminals` of `ts/orca-terminal.ts` (A-12), with an owner of its own.

Why. To look at a session of another project, the project had to be selected, which changed the work pane and needed a second selection to come back (A-8). The owner chose a modal in the middle of the screen that shows only the session that was right clicked, with no tabs (2026-11-16, issues #740 and #741). The modal opens from a project row too, and a button 「開く」 moves to the project and the session (issue #742).

The right click. A `contextmenu` on a row `.orca-session` (A-8) opens the modal for that session and cancels the default menu. A row had no right click before, so nothing is replaced. It opens nothing, and still cancels the default menu, when the row belongs to the selected project (its terminal is in the work pane, and a terminal is shown in one place at a time), and when the session has no terminal (`handle` is null).

The right click on a project row. A `contextmenu` on a project row `.orca-project` (A-8) opens the modal for the first session of that project, the first of the sessions that the list shows under it, and cancels the default menu. A project row had no right click before, so nothing is replaced. It opens nothing, and still cancels the default menu, when the project is the selected one, when it has no session, and when its first session has no terminal. Closing the modal gives the focus back to the project row.

The frame. `#orca-peek` is a curtain of the same look as `.scrim` (the class is not used) over the dimmed screen with a box (`#orca-peek-box`, `role="dialog"`, `aria-modal="true"`) that fills it with a margin of 32 px on the four sides, in the frame and shadow of `#account-popup`. The screen behind is inert while it is open. The modal has no line that explains it (`frontend.md`).

The head. One row, from the left: the status icon of the session (A-8, with its `aria-label`), the name of the project in bold, the title of the session in the muted color (the same text as its row; cut with an ellipsis when it is long), the name of the saved environment in the `.orca-env` form of the list (A-33; only for a session of a saved environment), and, at the right end, the button 「開く」 (`.text-btn`) and a cross (`.icon-btn`, `aria-label` 「閉じる」) at its right. The head follows the list: a read that changes the status or the title draws it again.

The terminal. Below the head, and nothing else (no tab strip), the terminal of the session fills the modal, and takes the keyboard when the modal opens, so keys typed go to it as in the work pane. It comes the way the work pane's does: streamed when the session is in the saved environment whose list is streamed (A-12), mirrored otherwise. Its owner is the modal's own, so the work pane, its tabs and its layout stay as they were (no call of `orca_panes` and no change of the selected project or tab).

The button 「開く」. It closes the modal, as the cross does, and then selects the project and the session that the modal showed, as a click on that session's row does: the project is the selected one, the session is its selected tab, and the terminal is in the work pane. The modal's terminal is forgotten first, so that the work pane's is the one that is read or streamed.

Closing. The cross and Escape (not the Escape that cancels an IME conversion; it is not sent to the session) close the modal and give the focus back to the row. Closing gives the terminal back to the Orca screen, so its stream and its reads stop; the keys not sent yet stay, and the screen forgets the terminal when its session leaves the list (A-12). The modal also closes by itself when the list no longer holds the session, when its project becomes the selected one, and when the Orca screen is not shown; a process that reads at an interval stops when its target is gone.

The E2E spec `orca-peek.e2e.ts` covers the right click on a session row (the modal, the head: icon, bold project name, title, the button 「開く」 before the cross, no tab strip, the label of the cross), the right click on a project row (its first session), 「開く」 (the modal closed, the project and the session selected, the terminal in the work pane), the environment tag (only for a session of a saved environment), the terminal (mirrored on this machine, streamed in a saved environment, the keys typed reaching it), a row of the selected project, a session with no terminal and a project with no session (nothing opens), Escape and the cross (closed, the work pane and the selected tab as before, the terminal's reads stopped), and the close when the session leaves the list.

## A-41 Develop a feature with feature-dev in a formation

The leader of a formation (A-37) can run `feature-dev`: it takes a feature through the requirement, the design, the implementation (one for each repository) and the specification by giving each unit to a child, one after another. The decisions are in issue #918, and the parts are #919 to #929. This section holds what each part settles; a later part writes its own paragraph here.

Embedding and writing (#919). The leader's skill and the plugin of the children are files of `src-tauri/formation/`, embedded in the binary at build time (`src-tauri/build.rs` walks the folder and writes a list of `(path relative to its folder, contents)` for each of the two folders below, `formation_assets.rs`; no crate is added, and a file that is added, changed or removed is in the next build). The files are text, in English.

- `formation/leader/feature-dev/` is the leader's skill, named `feature-dev` (the leader runs it as `feature-dev`). `write_formation_files` (`claude_code.rs`) replaces `.claude/skills/feature-dev/` in the leader's folder `formations/{name}/` with it as a whole at each start of the leader: the files are written in a folder beside it that then takes its place, so a file that the skill no longer has does not stay and a failed write leaves no half-written skill. A link that the leader placed in the place of `.claude/skills/` or of the skill's folder is removed and not followed. The other folders of `.claude/skills/` stay. `tsunagiai-mcp --formation-start` goes through the same function, so a leader on another PC gets the skill too.
- `formation/plugin/` is the plugin that every child is given, named `feature-dev` by `.claude-plugin/plugin.json`: its skills (`skills/`, the unit procedures) and its agents (`agents/`). `formation_launch` of `tsunagiai-mcp --formation` makes `formation-plugin/` of the config folder on the PC it runs on hold it as a whole, in the same way, before it makes the worktree or the terminal, unless the folder holds this very plugin already (a file `.tsunagiai-plugin-hash` in it holds the SHA-256 of the plugin's paths and texts, and every listed file must be there with its length: nothing is written again then, so a launch does not write the 55 files again and the other children that read the folder never see it replaced). A folder that has to be written is made beside it and takes its place by a rename, the old one is moved aside by a rename first and removed after the new one is in place, and the files are not synced one by one (the folder is synced before the rename), and a child's command is `claude -n {name} --permission-mode auto --effort medium --plugin-dir '{config}/formation-plugin'` (the path in single quotes, `claude_code::sh_quote`). A failed write is the step 「プラグインの書き出し」 of the answer and nothing is made.

Unit tests (`cargo test`): the embedded plugin holds `plugin.json` with the name `feature-dev` and every embedded path stays inside its folder; a folder is replaced as a whole (a file the list lacks is gone, nested files are written, nothing of the writing stays beside it, a failed write keeps the old folder, a swap that cannot place the new folder gives the old one back, a link in its place is removed and not followed); the plugin is written as a whole in `formation-plugin/` of the config folder with its hash, a folder that holds this plugin is not written again, and one with a cut or missing file, another hash or none is; the leader's skill is replaced as a whole by `write_formation_files` at each write and the other skills stay; a link in the place of `.claude/skills/` is not written through; the child's command passes the plugin folder as one quoted word.

A Codex child (#920). `formation_launch` and `formation_plan` take `agent`: `claude` (the default) or `codex` (a value of `FormationAgent`; any other is refused when the arguments are read, and the schema of the tool says the two). `formation_launch` takes the agent it is given, else the one the child was planned with, else `claude`. A Codex child is started in the same terminal of Orca, in the same worktree or folder, as a Claude Code child: its command is `codex --sandbox workspace-write --ask-for-approval never` (no `-n`: the title of the terminal, which Orca shows as its name, is the child's name; no `--plugin-dir`, and `formation-plugin/` is not written for it, because the plugin is Claude Code's), and the first instruction goes the same way: the launch waits for the terminal to take input (`terminal wait --for tui-idle`) and sends `prompt` with `terminal send`, and the send counts as made when Orca accepted the text (a Claude Code child is also checked for the start of its turn, `turn_started`, which is not known for Codex). When `codex` is not on the PC, or is not signed in, the terminal is made and never takes input, so the launch ends as for a Claude Code that does not start: the answer is `{ok: false, error, step}` with the step 「子のエージェントの立ち上がり待ち」 and an error that says to check that `codex` is installed and signed in on this PC, and the child is already in the record, so `formation_close` closes it. The child's `agent` is kept in `children.json` (`Child::agent`, `claude` or `codex`; a record from before the field has none and is a Claude Code child) and is a field of the row that the screen draws (`FormationChild::agent`). Its state is read from `orca terminal list` of its environment, by the terminal's handle under the child's recorded path (`worktreePath`, so that a record that names another terminal's handle does not take that terminal for the child's, and does not close it), and not from `orca worktree ps` (#920): a terminal made with `--command "codex ..."` is listed by `orca terminal list`, but `orca worktree ps --json` has no row for its `paneKey`, with an `agentType` of `codex` or none (measured, 2026-11-18: Codex answers an instruction and the terminal stays running). A Codex child is therefore 作業中 while its terminal is listed (not orphaned, under its path) and 完了, with no `handle` and no tab, when it is not; it is never 承認待ち, because its approval policy is `never` and Orca tells nothing of its turns. The read of the children (A-37) asks `orca terminal list` once for each environment that has a Codex child, and `orca worktree ps` only for an environment that has a Claude Code child; a list that cannot be read leaves the Codex children of that environment 作業中, as an unreadable `ps` does. The screen marks a Codex child: its tab shows `codex` where a Claude Code child's shows its effort (a Codex child has none), and its card has the same word after its name; a Claude Code child has no mark on its card. No text on the screen explains anything.

SendMessage does not reach Codex, so a Codex child hands over its work in files. The leader's first instruction tells it to write its result to an absolute path that the instruction gives and, last of all, an empty file `{that path}.done`; the leader waits for that `.done` file on the local disk (a Monitor or an until-loop of Bash with a time limit), reads the result and passes it on to the child that asked for it. `formation_claude.md` says this to the leader. The review by Codex that the unit children ask for (`CODEX-REVIEW {target} -> {output path}`, sent to the leader) is the use of it; the skills that write the prompt and the wait are those of #921 to #929.

Unit tests (`cargo test`, `formation.rs` and `tsunagiai-mcp.rs`): the command of a Codex child and the arguments of its terminal (the title, the command, no plugin, no `-n`), the agent of a launch (the one asked for, else the planned one, else Claude), the record that keeps the agent through a plan and a launch and reads an old record as Claude, the send that Codex needs only accepted, the row of a Codex child that takes its state from the terminal list (working while listed under its path, done with no handle when not listed, orphaned or listed under another path, whatever `ps` holds), the close that closes the terminal of a Codex child only when it is listed under the recorded path and the read of the children that asks `ps` and the terminal list once for each environment that needs them, and the `agent` of the two tools (absent, `codex`, an unknown name refused). The E2E spec `formation.e2e.ts` covers the mark of a Codex child on its tab and its card.

The decision page (#921). A child that has a question or an approval for the owner writes the input JSON of a decision page (the input contract of the decision pages) to a file and sends the leader `PAGE {absolute path}` (SendMessage). The leader calls the tool `formation_page` of `tsunagiai-mcp --formation` with `child` (the child's name) and `path`. The tool only takes a child of the record that is launched, checks the page, and records it; it is offered to the orchestrator only, with the other formation tools. Drawing the page (#922), sending the owner's answer (#923) and the gate of the wording (#924) are other parts.

- The contract. `schema_version` is 1. `page_kind` is `open-questions`, `approval` or `code-review`, and `subject` is `requirement` or `design` for the first two and `code` for the third. `context_label` and `title` are required, `what` is required on `approval` and `code-review` and absent on `open-questions`, `roots` maps a name to an absolute directory, and `previous_reply` is optional. `open-questions` has `questions` (cards of `question`); `approval` has `approval_scope` and `quality_loop` `{rounds, must_fix_resolved}` (required) and `changes`, `needs_user_decision` (`nr`), `residual_must_fix` (`must-fix`), `risks`, `settled_decisions` and, on `design` only, `structural_proposals` and `adr` (optional); `code-review` has `changes`, `residual_must_fix` (`review-must-fix`), `unmet_criteria` (`unmet-criterion`), `needs_user_decision` (`review-decision`) and `pr` `{url, note}` (optional). A card has `kind`, `number`, `title`, `summary`, `options` (`question`, `nr` and `review-decision` only: `{key, label, detail}`, keys `A`, `B`, ... in order, the recommendation always `A`), `recommendation` and `recommendation_reason` (every kind but `unmet-criterion`, which has neither; the recommendation of the other kinds is one of their fixed choices: `fix` or `skip`, `approve`, `reject` or `hold`, `keep` or `decline`, `fix` or `accept`), and optionally `background`, `facts` `[{label, value}]`, `blocks` and `evidence`. The blocks are `paragraph`, `heading`, `list`, `callout`, `table`, `before-after`, `folded`, `diagram` (its first word is `flowchart`, `graph`, `sequenceDiagram`, `stateDiagram` or `stateDiagram-v2`) and `evidence`, nested up to six deep. The evidence is `excerpt` `{root, path, start, end, highlight, note}` (at most 400 lines), `diff` `{summary, name, before, after, note}`, `git-diff` `{summary, root, path, base, head, note}` (`head` null is the working tree) and `node` `{root, ref, note}`; at most 200 items. A key that the contract does not name, in any object, is a violation (the images, `scene`, `terms`, `hold` and the other parts of the original contract are not taken).
- The violations. The check returns every violation as `{path, message}`, `path` a JSON path from `$` (`$.questions[0].options[1].key`) and the message in Japanese, in the order the input is walked, and refuses the page with all of them: the answer is `{ok: false, error, data: {violations}}` and nothing is recorded. The leader sends them to the child to write the page again. The screen's read gives them one on each line.
- The boundary. Every `roots` directory, and every path that evidence, `approval_scope` or `extras` reads, is inside a repository of this PC's Orca (`orca repo list` of `local`) or one of its worktrees (`orca worktree ps`), by its real path: a `..`, an absolute path where a relative one is meant, an empty name, a `\` and a link that leads out are refused. A path is relative to its `root` (`deliverables` when none is given; a `root` that `roots` does not name is a violation) and stays inside it. `.git` and any name that starts with `.env` is never read, in a root, a path or a `ref`, as `formation_read` leaves them. The page's own file is inside the worktree of the named child (itself inside those folders, because the record names it and the orchestrator can write the record) or inside a `.feature-dev/` folder of a repository folder or worktree, never elsewhere.
- The resolution. An `excerpt` is the lines `start` to `end` of a UTF-8 file of 2 MiB at most (a line end does not make an extra line, a trailing `\r` is dropped), and a range past the end of the file or a `highlight` outside the range is a violation. A `node` has `ref` `{file}#{pointer}`: `{file}` is a relative path ending `.json` and `{pointer}` an RFC 6901 JSON Pointer; a token that meets an array and is not a number picks the element of the array whose `id` is that token (`design.json#/designs/DESIGN-02`, `requirement.json#/requirements/REQ-03`), and a number is an index; a node that is not found, or whose text is over 64 KiB, is a violation. A `git-diff` is `git --literal-pathspecs -C {root} -c core.quotepath=false diff --no-color --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-renames --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ {base} [{head}] -- {path}`, with the revisions refused when they are empty, start with `-` or hold a space; `{path}` may be a folder, so the names of the changed files are read first with the same arguments and `--name-only -z`, and a change that has a name of `.git` or one that starts with `.env` among them (in any part of its path) is a violation and the diff is not read (a name list over 200 KiB is one too); its output is kept up to 200 KiB (`truncated` says it was cut) and the run waits 20 seconds; a path with no change between the two is a violation. `approval_scope` items name a node by `ref` (resolved as a `node`) or a file by `path` with `extras` (they must exist). The page file is at most 512 KiB and the page with its evidence at most 700 KiB, so that one frame of the server (1 MiB) holds it; the evidence that is read is added up as it is resolved, and the first one that finds the 700 KiB used up is a violation (`ここから先の根拠は読んでいません`) and the evidence after it is not read. The cards of a page have each `number` once (a second card with the same number is a violation at its `number`, because the reply names a card by it). A JSON file that nodes and `approval_scope` items name is read and parsed once for the page.
- The record. `formations/{name}/pages.json` is `{"pages": [{child, path, title, page_kind, subject, at, answered_at}]}` (`at` in epoch milliseconds; `answered_at` only on an answered page), one entry for each child, which a later page of the same child replaces. It is read, changed and written through `write_atomic` while the lock of the process and an exclusive `flock` on `pages.json.lock` are held, and a file that is there and cannot be read is not written over; the folder of a formation that was deleted is not made again. A page whose `at` is before the launch of the child with that name (a child that was closed and launched again) is not that child's. A page whose reply was sent (#923) has `answered_at` too (epoch milliseconds), and it no longer waits: it is no mark, and `formation_page_read` refuses it. A later page of the same child replaces the record, with no `answered_at`.
- The wording gate (#924). The Japanese lines of a page (a quotation, and a string that only names a place, are not checked) must be in the record of a final text of yomiyasu: `formation_page` checks that after the input check has passed and before it records the page. When a line is not in the record, it records nothing, and refuses with the list of those lines. When it is refused, the child polishes the Japanese of the page in one yomiyasu run and sends PAGE again.
- The mark. `formation_children` gives each child `pendingPage` `{title, atMs}` from that record, `null` when it has none (this Mac's `pages.json`, or for a formation of a saved environment the answer of that PC's server to `pages.read`, asked on the connection that reads the record of the formation (`children.read`), so that a read of the children opens one connection for the PC and not a second one for the pages; a PC that cannot be asked, or whose orchestrator never started, gives no mark and the list is still read). The command `formation_page_read(name, child)` gives the page that waits on a child (`DecisionPage`, the checked page with its evidence resolved at that moment), and fails with the violations, one on each line, when the file has changed into one that breaks the contract. For a formation of a saved environment the server of its PC checks and resolves it (`page.read`), because the files are on that PC.
- The server. `--serve` answers three more requests besides `children.read` and `children.drop`: `pages.read` `{formation}` gives the pages of that formation's `pages.json`, and `page.read` `{formation, child}` gives the page of that child, checked and resolved on the PC (it asks that PC's Orca for the folders; a request is served on a thread that may block). `page.read` also gives `at`, the time the page was recorded at, and takes `light` (false when it is not sent): with `light` the evidence is checked for its shape and none of it is read, so the page's cards are all there and the evidence has no content (no file, no node, no `git`; the page's size is not looked at), which is what the reply needs (#923). `page.answer` `{formation, child, at}` sets `answered_at` of the page of that child that was recorded at `at`, under the lock of the record (#923); a page that is not there, that a newer page replaced (another `at`) or that is answered already is refused with the reason, and nothing is written. The other requests read only; a name is checked with `check_name`.
- The leader's file `formation_claude.md` tells the leader to call `formation_page` when a child sends `PAGE {path}` and to send the violations back to that child.

Unit tests (`cargo test`, `formation_page.rs`, `formation_server.rs`, `formation.rs`, `tsunagiai-mcp.rs`): a page of each kind is accepted, with every kind of card, block and evidence; every violation of one page is returned with its JSON path (an unknown key, an empty text, a wrong option key, a recommendation outside the choices, a root that `roots` lacks); a root outside the repositories, a link that leaves them, an absolute path, `..`, an empty name, `.git` and `.env` names, and a page file outside the child's worktree and the `.feature-dev/` folders are refused; an excerpt (the range, past the end, the highlight, a file that is too big or not UTF-8), a node (the pointer, the escapes, an index, an `id`, a node that is not found), a git diff (two revisions, the working tree, a revision that is an option, a path with no change, a long diff that is cut, a folder that tracks a changed `.env` file or has a `.git` name in its changes is refused and not read) and the limits of evidence and of the page (the evidence after the page has used up its size is not read), a card number twice, the nodes of one file read once, a page read for its cards alone (every check of the input, no evidence read); the record (one page for each child that a later one replaces, nothing recorded for a page with violations or for a child that is planned or unknown, a record that cannot be read is not written over, a deleted formation's folder is not made again, a page older than the child's launch is no mark); the folders a page may name are the repositories and their worktrees and no other; the read of a page again with its evidence, and its refusal once the file breaks the contract; the page that goes through JSON between the server and the app; `pages.read` and `page.read` of the server by `dispatch` and over a socket, the pages on the connection of the record of the environment (one connection, none for a record that could not be read); the tool is offered with the other formation tools and only to the orchestrator, and its description names what the leader must know.

The screen of the decision page (#922). The page that waits on a child is drawn in the middle pane (layout A: a tab, not an overlay), in place of a terminal.

- The card and the tab. A child whose `pendingPage` is set has a card that says 判断待ち (in place of its state; it looks as an approval that waits does, with the color of `--agent-question`), shows the page's title where it shows the child's last answer, and has the button 「開く」 beside it (not inside it, because the card is a button). Its tab strip has a tab 「判断: {title}」 right after the child's own tab, with the mark of a child that waits. A child with no mark has neither. The card of a child with a mark is still pressed to show its terminal.
- Showing it. Pressing 「開く」 or the tab shows the page in place of the terminal (the leader's too, and the child's), selects the tab and turns 「自動で追う」 off; pressing the leader's tab, a child's tab or its card shows the terminal again, and the page is kept. Turning 「自動で追う」 on moves the selection to the child that waits and hides the page. The tab that was selected before is the one shown again when the page closes. The page is read (`formation_page_read`) when its tab is first shown and again when the time of the mark changes, never on the 3-second read of the children, which only gives the mark; a page whose mark is gone, or whose time changed, is forgotten, so the tab closes and the pane shows the tab that was selected before. A read that fails shows the message (the violations, one on each line) in place of the page, and pressing the tab again reads it again. Selecting another formation forgets every page. The answers typed into a page stay while its tab is not shown.
- What is drawn. The title, the context label and 「{child} から」; 「何が変わるか」 with the lead (`what`) and the change sections (folds on an approval, open on a code review); on an approval, 「リスク・リリース上の注意」 (the risks, with their evidence behind 「根拠を見る」) and 「承認の対象」 (each item by its reference or path, with its text behind 「中身を見る」); the cards in groups headed 「{label} {count} 件」 (論点 on `open-questions`; 判断が必要, 未解消の必ず直す指摘, 構造改善の提案 and ADR（設計判断の記録） on an approval; 未解消の必ず直す指摘, 満たしていないリリース条件 and 判断が必要 on a code review); and under 「参考」 the settled decisions (the provisional ones marked), the result of the quality check, the pull request and the earlier reply.
- A card has its number, title and summary, the first diagram of a card that asks, the choices, and behind 「詳しく見る」 its background, facts, blocks and evidence. The choices of `question`, `nr` and `review-decision` are the lettered options as radios (the recommended one `A`, marked 推奨 with 「理由: …」 under it, and the detail of an option behind 「この選択肢の詳細」), then the fixed choices of the kind (調べてから決めて, 今回は対応しない, その他), with a box for free text in the row of その他 that shows only while it is chosen; the other kinds have the fixed choices of the reply format (指摘のとおり修正する / 今回は対応しない, 承認 / 却下 / 保留, 残す / 不要, 修正して再レビューする / 現状で完了する) and a field for a note under them (「理由（却下・保留のとき）」 on a structural proposal, 「補足（任意）」 on the others); `unmet-criterion` has no answer. 「選択を取り消す」 returns a card's choice to none. Nothing is chosen at first.
- The blocks are `paragraph`, `heading`, `list`, `callout`, `table` (a changed cell has a bar and a ground), `before-after` (each side framed, captioned 今 and 変更後 unless it has its own), `folded` and `evidence`. An excerpt shows the numbers of its lines and marks the highlighted ones; a `diff` shows 変更前 and 変更後; a `git-diff` shows its lines with the added, removed and hunk lines marked, the two revisions (`作業ツリー` for none) and a note when it was cut; a `node` is a tree of folds of its JSON values (its text as it is when it is not JSON).
- The diagram. A `diagram` is drawn by Mermaid 12.3.0 (`src/vendor/mermaid.min.js`, MIT, one file as the other libraries, with its version and license in the comment at its top), which `ts/vendor-load.ts` reads the first time a page has a diagram (`script-src 'self'` stays as it is; the file has no `eval` and no `new Function`). It runs with `securityLevel: "strict"` and `htmlLabels: false`, with the theme of the system's light or dark, and its SVG goes through DOMPurify's SVG profile; the caption is under it and the SVG has it as its alternative text (「図」 when there is none). A diagram that cannot be drawn shows its Mermaid source as text.
- Every text of a page is the child's, and goes in as `textContent`; nothing is read as HTML. The screen shows no text that says what to type or what a button does.

Sending the answers (#923). The page ends with the button 「リーダーに送る」, which gives the answers of the page to `formation_page_answer(name, child, atMs, answers)`: `atMs` is the time of the mark that the page was drawn for, and each answer is the card's number, the key of its choice (null when none) and the free text of its box (as it stands, empty or not). The screen decides nothing about them: the reply is made in Rust (`formation_reply.rs`, `build_reply`) from the page read again, so it is made of what the page holds now; the page is read for its cards alone (its evidence is not resolved, `page.read` with `light` for a formation of a saved environment), because the reply holds none of it. The button waits while the send is on its way (`aria-busy`), so a second press sends nothing, and Rust holds the pair of the formation and the child while it sends, so no second send of the same reply starts either.

- The reply text follows the reply format of the decision pages. On an `approval` and a `code-review` page the first line is the verdict (an approval of a requirement: 要件定義を承認します or 要件定義の修正を依頼します; of a design: 基本設計を承認します or 基本設計の修正を依頼します; a code review: この変更を承認します or 修正を依頼します), the revise line when the answer of a `must-fix` or a `review-must-fix` card is its `fix`, and the approve line otherwise; an `open-questions` page has none. Then one block for each card that has an answer, in the order the page draws the cards, the verdict and the blocks separated by one blank line. A block is `{number} {title}`, the answer line, and `補足: {text}` (the line breaks of the text are kept). The answer line is `{key}. {label}` for a lettered option, and the reply text of a fixed choice (調べてから決めて, その他, 今回は対応しない, 指摘のとおり修正する, 承認, 却下, 保留, 残す, 不要, 修正して再レビューする, 現状で完了する); a card with a note and no choice has no answer line. The free text of a `question`, `nr` or `review-decision` card counts only under `その他`, and `その他` with no text is no answer; that of the other kinds counts whatever is chosen. A card with no answer has no block. `unmet-criterion` takes no answer.
- It cannot be sent while a `question` or a `must-fix` card has no answer: the answer is the failure 「回答が必要なカードが未回答です: {numbers}」, nothing is sent, and the page stays. A number that is not on the page, a number twice, and a key that the card does not offer are refused the same way.
- What the leader's terminal gets is `判断ページの返信（{child}）`, a line break and the reply text. Everything in it is the child's or the owner's text, so the control characters are taken out first (an ESC, the paste marks of the text among them, and a carriage return; a tab becomes a blank, and a title or a label is on one line), and the text goes in as one bracketed paste (`ESC[200~` ... `ESC[201~`), so that no line of it is sent before the rest is in. Enter is a send of its own, 250 milliseconds after the paste. The paste, its two marks (12 characters) counted, is at most 100,000 characters (the limit of a send). The leader reads it as `messages.md` of its skill says and sends the reply text to the child as a `REPLY`.
- Where it goes. A formation of this Mac has its leader in the app's own pseudo terminal (the pane `formation:{name}`, A-36): the paste and then Enter are typed into it, and the send fails with a message when it has no process or the process has ended. A formation of a saved environment has its leader in the terminal of that PC's Orca that `formation.json` records (`orchestratorTerminal`, A-37): the paste and then Enter are two `terminal.send` (no `enter`) over one WebSocket of that environment, and a send that Orca does not take, or a leader that was never started, is a failure.
- The mark. When the reply has been sent, the page is marked answered (`answered_at`): by the app in `pages.json` of this Mac, or on the PC of a saved environment by the `page.answer` request of its server, with the `at` that the screen gave. The screen then takes the mark off the child at once, which closes the tab and shows the tab that was selected before, and the child's card says its state again; a read of the children that began before the send is dropped. A page that a newer one replaced between the read and the mark is refused with the reason: the reply has been sent then, and the message says so. When the reply was sent and the mark could not be made (the PC does not answer, the record cannot be written), the app remembers the formation, the child and the time of the page (in memory), and the next press only marks the page and sends nothing, so that the reply does not go twice; the memory is dropped when the mark is made.
- A send that fails (a required card with no answer, a leader that is not running, an Orca that does not answer) keeps the page, the tab and every answer as they are, shows the message under the page in the place of an error (`role="alert"`), and leaves the button ready to press again. A page that was replaced after it was drawn (the time of the mark differs) is refused with 「ページが差し替わりました。開き直してください」.

Unit tests (`cargo test`, `formation_reply.rs`, `formation_page.rs`, `formation_server.rs`, `claude_code.rs`, `orca.rs`): the reply of an open-questions page, an approval (requirement and design) and a code review, with every kind of card, the verdict line and its revise line, the blocks in page order with one blank line between, `その他` with and without text, a note with no choice, a free text that does not count, a required card with no answer and the numbers in the failure, a key the card does not offer, a number twice or not on the page, a page kind with no reply format; the control characters of a title, a label and a note (an ESC, the paste end, a carriage return, a tab, a C1 control) never reach the text; the terminal text; the paste is one bracketed paste with no Enter and no paste end of the text inside, every line inside it, and Enter a separate send; the limit of the paste with its marks counted; a reply on its way is not sent twice, a reply that was sent and not marked is not sent again by the next press (and one that could not be sent is not remembered); the typing into the pane of a formation (in order, nothing for a pane that is not there or that ended, the limit); the two sends over the WebSocket of a saved environment in order, and no Enter after a paste that Orca refused; the answered mark (the page of that time, once, a replaced or missing page and a deleted formation refused, a later page waits again, an answered page is no mark and is not read) and `page.answer` by `dispatch` and over a socket.

E2E (`formation.e2e.ts`): the card with 判断待ち and 「開く」 and the tab after its child's; the page shown in place of the terminal from the card and from the tab, read once for the formation and the child and not on a poll; the radios, the recommendation and its reason, the free text of その他 and the cancel; the fixed choices of each kind and the groups of an approval; the code review with its open changes, the criterion with no answer and the pull request; the blocks, facts and evidence behind 「詳しく見る」; text that looks like HTML staying text; a diagram drawn as an SVG by Mermaid and one that fails shown as its source; the tab closing when the mark is gone and the earlier tab shown again; a page read again when the time of the mark changes; a failed read and its retry; and another formation forgetting the pages; the answers sent (`formation_page_answer` with the name, the child, the time of the mark and each card's number, choice and text, a card with no choice sent as null, a page with no card that asks, once however often the button is pressed), a failed send that keeps the page and the answers and shows its message and a send that is pressed again, and the tab that closes and the card that says its state again when the reply went through.
